- **Cross-links between control pages** (`subfleet.page_links` → `history.write_page(..., links)`): the history page of every fleet (the browser page next to the control tab) has a top line: with sub-fleets it lists `子 fleet n：` (sub-fleet n:) followed by "M0 · state (outcome) · worker-1"; a sub-fleet's page offers `← 回到父 fleet` (back to the parent fleet). **A click = switch to the other fleet's cmux workspace** (sub-fleets never nest; they are separate side-by-side workspaces in the sidebar; cmux registers only http/https, so the web page cannot switch workspaces itself): on a link click the page stays on its own file and only adds an explicit marker `?fleet-jump=<ms base36>-<random>&rel=parent|child&to=<other page>` (measured on real cmux: jumping from inside a web page to a file:// page outside the page's directory becomes about:blank, while `cmux browser goto` works); this fleet's daemon reads its own history pane URL on every tick (at most once a second, `cmux browser url`) and acts only on a marker (`subfleet.follow_jump`): it accepts only the parent page of this config's `parent_config` (rel=parent) and the child pages in this run's `sub/*/subfleet.json` (rel=child); if the other side's workspace exists in its cmux registry and is not this workspace → `select-workspace`, then `browser goto` this pane back to this fleet's own page (with the hash state last seen); the other side's pane is not touched. Each marker is first recorded in Redis (`history_jump` in the `fx:service` hash) before acting, so repeated ticks, reloading a page with a marker, or a daemon restart only redo "put back this page" and never switch a second time; a marker older than 120 seconds only puts back this page (to avoid stealing focus after a daemon outage). URL changes without a marker (plain refresh, in-page state) are never treated as clicks. A sub-fleet already taken back / failed (workspace closed), a missing registry on the other side, or a failed `select-workspace`: no switch; the other side's history page (marker removed) is left readable in this pane; a relation that does not match or an unrelated page: no switch, put back this page. A fleet with neither parent nor children does not read its pane. The parent page is rewritten and reloaded when a sub-fleet starts, moves to stopping, or is taken back; data is read only from disk (parent config, `subfleet.json`).
- **v2 matrix addition**: a `claude` supervisor + `claude` workers is allowed only when the config carries `adopt` (i.e. a sub-fleet, task mode); a plain `claude` supervisor is still refused. The adopted supervisor does not rotate (rotation would replace the pane that must go back to the parent fleet); context growth relies on Claude's own compaction. On a sub-fleet `--resume` the adopted pane is taken back like a worker, without a next generation.
- **Official Claude worker panes only**: a Pi bridge is bound to the parent Redis socket and never reconnects after a disconnect (§17), so it is refused with the reason stated; Codex workers are not in the v2 matrix.
- **Claude worker session name**: `--name f<main run token>[-<sub-fleet number>]-<slot>` (e.g. `fa1b2c3d4-M0-worker-2`), fixed per slot and kept across rotations (contract spec appendix C). The cmux tab is still set to slot.generation by the fleet's `rename-tab`; **whether `rename-tab` overrides the terminal title Claude writes from `--name` has not been verified on real cmux**; if it is overridden, switch to `…-<slot>.<generation>` per the spec and state "prefix match, take the highest generation" in interface contract 5-A.
- **Fixed along the way**: the v2 daemon main loop used to call `RuntimeV2.tick` directly, so `Fleet.tick` (§16 roster hot reload, §17 writing `result.json` on task terminal state, this section's sub-fleet takeback) never ran in a real v2 daemon; it now calls `self.f.tick()` (which still calls `RuntimeV2.tick` internally), with a regression test.

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_subfleet.py`: two real private Redis instances + two in-process daemons + a fake cmux that records ownership per registry + the real `claude_stop`/`notify` entry points: handover → sub-fleet READY through the parent hook → the child supervisor's decision dispatched to a child worker → delivery → release → parent takes back the same session, READY → parent supervisor gets the result; no delivery and no death verdict while delegated; missing output judged failed, retry numbering; rollback when starting the child Redis fails; Escape before takeback; next generation in place when the session is lost; main resume keeps the delegated slot and brings up the sub-fleet; the four limits and the number range; Pi/busy slots refused; request files executed via tick; status tree; late generations not redirected; tree stop leaves no orphan Redis; main resume brings up the sub-fleet; child resume takes back the adopted pane; parent task terminal state stops the sub-fleet; regression for the v2 main loop running Fleet.tick; cmux_manager's release/adopt/place_after/close_all with a fake `_run`).

**Not accepted / known gaps**: the cross-workspace `move-surface` handover, `reorder-workspace`, and the ordering of tab titles vs `--name` were not measured on real cmux; whether a running Claude session whose CLAUDE.md is swapped mid-way really replies only with decision JSON in the supervisor format (prompt level) was not measured; this is the largest remaining risk of this block. A delegated slot can still be given work by the parent supervisor; the message queues until takeback. Split request, approval and `spawn_child` are wired in section 20. A sub-fleet task's output is only checked for "file exists and is non-empty"; formal acceptance belongs to the contracts block.

## 20. Split-request wiring + sub-fleet interface notices / vetoes

Design draft: an internal plan (§3–§4) and the interface contract spec (§3.0 / §4.2; interfaces inside a sub-fleet "take effect at once + notify the top level, which may veto"). This section wires up the "split request → approval → `spawn_child`" path that §19 left open; the single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the task/service protocol and §16–§19 are unchanged.

- **Worker request**: the report keeps its usual four parts (Done describes the part already done) and ends with exactly one JSON object `{"escalate": reason, "proposal": [{id, instruction, output, kind?}], "workers": n}`; `kind` names the kind of work of a block, and the supervisor message shows it next to the block so the supervisor can pick a model per block (the same object as for contract tasks and `interface_change`; `workers` defaults to the number of proposal blocks). The program checks: proposal ids are distinct and all of the form `<task number>.<n>`, and the task is one this worker is doing (contract: task table `to` = it, state running/escalated; plain task: an item of this task dispatched to it with an `id`), then runs `subfleet.check(..., ready=False)` (number ownership, depth, number of sub-fleets, agents in the whole tree, `child_workers_max`, slot type; idle / delivery in flight are not checked, since the worker is settling this very report; they are checked when the sub-fleet actually starts). Fails -> the reason goes both to the worker ("carry on yourself") and to the supervisor; passes -> stored in Redis `fx:escalations`, and one message goes to the supervisor (the proposal listed block by block + how to answer).
- **Numbers of plain tasks**: a dispatch item may carry an `id` (`M<n>`, inside a sub-fleet `M0.<n>`); only then is it written to `fx:items` with `[M0]` prefixed to the first line of the dispatch, and only then can a split be requested. A request without a number is refused with the reason "the dispatch must carry an id, or use a contract task". `fx:escalations` / `fx:items` are cleared when a new task is submitted.
- **With the decomposition tree on (section 62)**: a dispatch id must be a block of the tree (no automatic number, no dispatch without an id); `fx:items` is written exactly as above. A task-mode worker's request also carries `"delta_t"` (minutes the whole task finishes earlier) and `"delta_c"` (extra session minutes), refused without them; the supervisor's message shows the worker's numbers beside the program's account, an approve the program's account refuses is handled like a limit (no strike, the request stays pending), and an approved split is written into the tree. A spawn answer `rejected` (back to pending) or a sub-fleet returning failed also rolls the split back in the tree, so the block can be dispatched again. Service P7 requests are unchanged.
- **Contract task table**: the report carrying the request is not a delivery; the task row is marked `escalated` (not judged failed); when refused it stays `running` and the refusal message is recorded under that task (the next report settles it as usual).
- **Supervisor decision**: new decision key `escalations: [{task, decision: approve|revise|reject, workers?, note?}]`. Malformed, a task with no pending request, the same reply also dispatching that task, or approve while also dispatching to the same worker → the usual correction path (counts toward the three consecutive refusals). A RUNNING carrying `escalations` is exempt from "must dispatch when nobody is busy".
  - `approve`: the limits are checked again; **failing the limits is not a format error**: the request stays pending and the supervisor gets the reason (it may lower the worker count, revise or reject). Passes → `subfleet.request` (goal = the task's original instruction + the interface ids the parent fleet must check (shapes in the parent `contracts.json`, read-only) + note; split plan = the approved proposal; a contract task carries `contracts: true`, and the sub-fleet runs its own P0 / P1 first; output = the parent paths of all provided interfaces, which the sub-fleet writes into the matching places of its own workspace and which are copied back on takeback, §23) → task row `delegated`, attempts+1. The next tick executes the request: answer `accepted` → recorded as `delegated`; `rejected` (e.g. the worker happens not to be idle) → the request returns to pending, the row returns to `escalated`, and the supervisor is told why.
  - `revise` / `reject`: the worker gets a message tagged `[M0]` (with the note); a contract task row returns to `running` with this message recorded under M0; the worker revises and reports again, or does it itself. The supervisor re-dispatching that number directly also counts as an answer, and the pending request is voided.
- **Two other kinds of escalation (defect F′)**: besides split requests (key = task number; no `kind` means split), `fx:escalations` also holds `dialog:<slot>:g<generation>` (a worker stopped at a non-delete confirmation dialog, `approve | reject`) and `blocked:<slot>` (a worker reported Blocked, `approve | revise | reject`), answered in the same `escalations[]`; details in §24. Answering a `dialog:` / `blocked:` record that no longer exists (dialog gone / already passed to the user) is only logged, not a format error.
- **Sub-fleet return**: `subfleet.collect` appends the task-table outcome after the `[Sub-fleet M0 completed|failed]` sent to the supervisor. Contract tasks: on completed, each of M0's provided files is checked literally against its interface → all pass: interfaces `ready`, consumers notified, M0 `done`, pending items that depend on it released; otherwise / failed → M0 `failed` (the reason states which attempt). Plain tasks only clear the request record.
- **Interfaces inside a sub-fleet**: a passed sub-fleet skeleton (all v1 in effect) and additions / changes approved by the child supervisor **take effect at once** and are written to `<parent run>/subfleet-notices/`; middle levels pass them up unchanged, and the top-level tick records the source and sends the supervisor one `[sub-fleet interfaces] …`. The top level uses `decision: "veto"` in `interfaces[]` (valid only for interfaces reported by a sub-fleet, otherwise corrected) → written to `<source run>/subfleet-vetoes/`; on its next tick the sub-fleet marks the interface `deprecated` (`deprecated_by: veto by the top supervisor`) and notifies the producer / consumers and the child supervisor. **Deviation from spec §4.1 row 4**: compatible / breaking changes of interfaces inside a sub-fleet are also handled as "take effect at once + vetoable", without waiting for top-level approval (no cross-fleet approval channel was built).
- **Guard rails (review r1 P2-01/02/03 + the first real v2 run of `Fleet.tick`)**: `limits()` catches `yaml.YAMLError`; any exception in a single request in `process_requests` writes a `rejected` answer and deletes the request file; a sub-fleet record without `config.json` (killed halfway through starting) is marked `failed` and skipped in tick / stop / resume; child daemon gone, no `result.json`, parent task still running, and the start grace (120 seconds, by `launched`/`created`; a resume relaunch updates `launched`) exceeded → taken back as `cancelled`, outcome `failed`. Each stage of `Fleet.tick` (roster hot reload / writing result.json / syncing contracts.json / sub-fleets / request answers / interface notices / vetoes) catches its own exceptions, writes a `tick.error` log and continues; `BusError` is still raised (an unknown v2 transaction state must stop). Under v2 `contracts.json` is rewritten on every tick against Redis, so an uncommitted version written early inside a transaction is corrected on the next tick. `CONTRACTS.md` is backed up with AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md and restored on takeback.

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_escalate.py`: contract task table escalated / delegated / refused answer back to pending; revise / reject go back to the worker and settle the next report; limit reasons reach both worker and supervisor; approve over the limit is not counted, an unknown task and dispatching in the same reply are counted; sub-fleet return → done/ready/dependencies released, and failed with the attempt count; plain tasks require a dispatch id; re-dispatch voids the request; tick stage exceptions do not kill the daemon, BusError is raised; a bad roster keeps the limits; two real v2 fleets: dispatch M0 → worker report carries escalate → supervisor approves → tick starts the sub-fleet → the sub-fleet goal contains the original instruction and the proposal → completion and takeback → request cleared, the parent supervisor gets `[Sub-fleet M0 completed]`; a bad request is only answered, no repeated crash; a record without config is marked failed; a vanished child daemon is taken back as failed; child interface notices are passed up and a veto lands in the sub-fleet; middle levels pass up).

**Known gaps (P2)**: if a contract notice is queued for the worker between approve and the tick, starting the sub-fleet is refused as "not idle" and returns to pending (no automatic retry); the Pi worker path of `escalate` is not wired (a Pi pane cannot be handed over anyway; `check` refuses); a veto does not cascade to tasks depending on the interface (they are not marked stale; the child supervisor replans after the notice); notice / veto files are written inside the v2 transaction, so a replayed transaction may notify twice; compatible / breaking changes inside a sub-fleet do not wait for top-level approval (see above); the parent supervisor may re-dispatch the same number to another worker while it is `delegated` (not blocked); there is no in-process end-to-end test of contract parent task -> contract sub-fleet (only that the request carries `contracts: true` was verified; the sub-fleet submitting a contract task, writing the supervisor version of CONTRACTS.md into the handed-over worker's directory, and restoring the worker version on takeback have never been run); a middle-level fleet passes the grandchild's interface notices up unchanged and **does not copy its own supervisor** (deviates from spec section 4.2 "the middle supervisor only receives copies"); `tick.error` logs one line per tick for a persistent fault, without rate limiting; worker.md hard-codes "workers 1-3" while the real limit is the hot-reloadable `child_workers_max`; the whole chain is not measured on real cmux or Claude sessions.

## 21. Peer notices: workers send interface changes to each other directly

Spec: the interface contract spec (§4.3–§4.4, appendix B §5-A / §5-B, the two "peer notices" sections of appendix C). Rule: Claude Code workers talk to each other through Claude Code's own inter-session messages; Pi / Codex go through the fleet's Redis mailboxes; if either end is not Claude, the broker is used; peers pass only facts, and anything uncertain is asked of the supervisor in the report (`Question for supervisor:`). This section only adds the channel and does not change the approval authority of §18 (approval still goes only through the supervisor's decision JSON); the single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout and the task/service protocol are unchanged. The code is in `fleetmux/peer.py`; fleet.py / runtime_v2.py only get small hooks.

- **Switch**: there is no separate `peer_notices` setting: peer notices are on with contract tasks (task spec `contracts: true`); prompts, Redis and turn handling of non-contract tasks are unchanged; the broker refuses in non-contract tasks (`peer: interface contracts are off for this task`). Spec appendix C's "with `peer_notices: true`, a CLI that is neither claude nor has a broker → refuse at start" is therefore unnecessary: the broker is available to every CLI.
- **Prompts**: `CONTRACTS.md` (and the AGENTS.md of a generation launched during the task) gets a section 5 after the §18 section, chosen by the worker's current CLI (`agent_cli(runtime_cfg)`, including profile overrides): Claude gets `agents/contracts_peer_claude.md` (5-A: `ToolSearch select:SendMessage,ListAgents` → `ListAgents` filtered by `{{FLEET_PEER_PREFIX}}` → `SendMessage`; for non-Claude consumers use the broker instead), Pi / Codex get `contracts_peer_broker.md` (5-B); **the Pi / Codex prompts never mention `SendMessage` / `ListAgents` / the prefix**. The supervisor gets `contracts_peer_supervisor.md` appended (explaining that `[peer CC]` / unsolicited / `[peer violation]` lines need no approval from it). `{{FLEET_PEER}}` = `python fleet.py peer --config <cfg> --from <slot>`, with the sender pinned in the command (same trust level as `{{FLEET_CHROME}}`: an agent in the sandbox can forge `--from`; the daemon only checks that it is a live worker of this fleet). The prefix `{{FLEET_PEER_PREFIX}}` = `f<root run token>-`.
- **`consumers_runtime`**: each consumer in `contracts.json` gets `cli` (the worker's current CLI) and `session` (for Claude, `claude_session_name`, i.e. the §19 `--name`, fixed per slot and kept across rotations; otherwise `null`). The tick rewrites it on every tick by text diff, so a CLI change at rotation or a roster hot reload is reflected on the next tick.
- **broker `peer`**: CLI `fleet.py peer --from --to <worker|consumers:IF-x> --iface IF-x --text-file <a file in your own directory>`, on the same event path as `request()` (v1 `Fleet.handle`, v2 `runtime_v2.handle` new kind `peer`; unlike chrome, its only side effects are Redis writes, so it can run inside the v2 transaction). Checks per appendix C, with these exact error texts: `--to` is not a live worker of this fleet (slot name / label / Claude session name all accepted; not itself or the supervisor) → `peer: --to must be a worker in this fleet tree or consumers:<IF-id>`; interface not registered and the first line is not `0 -> 1 | new | UNAPPROVED` → `peer: IF-x is not registered`; over 600 characters, a first line not in the fixed format, a first-line interface different from `--iface`, or a body containing a `[Supervisor instruction …]` header → `peer: notice must be <= 600 chars and start with the fixed first line`; `READY` from a non-producer → `peer: only the producer may announce READY`; the 21st message from one sender within an hour (rolling 3600 seconds) → `peer: rate limit 20/hour reached`. Three added messages: `peer: --from must be a live worker of this fleet`, `peer: --text-file must be a file in your own directory`, `peer: no consumer of IF-x has a worker yet` (`consumers:` expands to no dispatched consumer). A valid notice goes as `[peer] <original text>` + one sentence "act on it per CONTRACTS.md, reply in the AGENTS.md format" into the target's v2 mailbox (`contract_queue`, with an empty report ownership, so a notice turn settles no task); the target picks it up on its next idle turn.
- **How copies and supervisor forwards travel**: `[peer CC] <sender> -> <target>:` + original text, the `Peer notices:` / `[worker question]` lines of an unsolicited turn, and `[peer violation]` all go first into the Redis list `fx:peer:notes` and are **appended to the end of the supervisor's next business message** (like §16 `[Roster change]`), never sent alone: a message of its own would force the supervisor to answer a RUNNING with no dispatch, which counts as an error under the existing rule when no worker is busy. Cost: copies wait for the next business message (in a contract task, usually the target worker's reply to the `[peer]`).
- **Logs**: `contracts.log.jsonl` appends `peer` (sender, target, interface, level, state, original text), `peer.rejected` (with error and original text), `peer_notices` (the lines in reports, the only trace of the SendMessage path), `unsolicited`, `peer.violation`.
- **Unsolicited turns**: a turn in which a Claude worker is woken by a peer's `SendMessage` (or by the operator typing) corresponds to no delivery in flight. The existing handling was already "do not settle, only audit `turn.ignored`"; this section has both ignored branches of v2 `tui_turn` (no delivery in flight / the turn was not started by a typed delivery) send the daemon an `unsolicited` event (no reply awaited); the daemon checks run / generation / session and records `turn.unsolicited`, and in a contract task writes the log and forwards the `Peer notices:` / `Question for supervisor:` lines to the supervisor; it touches no lease, pops no report ownership, counts no correction, renews nothing and queues no message, so "idle must dispatch" is not triggered. v1 Claude does the same in the duplicate branch of `on_turn`. The same Stop firing twice: identical to the last entry of `fx:replies:<slot>` (v1 original text / v2 structured value) → recorded as `turn.duplicate` and not forwarded again.
- **Violations**: in the `Peer notices:` line of a report or unsolicited turn, if the x in `from <x>` neither starts with this tree's prefix nor is a worker name / label of this fleet → write `peer.violation` and send the supervisor `[peer violation]`. The program cannot block SendMessage itself; this relies on the prompts on both ends.

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_peer.py`: both section-5 variants injected by CLI and the Pi / Codex text has no `SendMessage`; `consumers_runtime.session` refreshed with CLI / rotation; every broker refusal text + log; delivery into the mailbox + empty report ownership + copy carried by the next business message + drained only once; READY / new interface / session-name targets; rate limit per sender, rolling hour; violation lines; v2 with real private Redis + real `peer` CLI + real Claude Stop hook: `[peer]` typed into a Claude worker, the turn woken by a peer recorded as unsolicited without settling the delivery in flight, then the delivery in flight settles normally, a duplicate Stop is not forwarded again; the same for v1 Claude).

**Known gaps (P2)**: cross-fleet delivery (target in another fleet of the same tree) is refused outright with a request to explain it in the report, and copies go only to this fleet's supervisor, not up the parent chain to the top: the spec needs the root daemon to hold the routes of the whole tree, and section 18 contracts themselves cover a single fleet only, so this comes with sub-fleet contracts; if an official-CLI worker does not reply in Done/Evidence format after a `[peer]`, it goes through the usual 2 corrections and then blocked (like the section 18 `[contract]` notice); while a Claude worker woken by a peer is still idle in Redis, the daemon may type the next delivery into its input box; Claude queues typed text after the current turn, which is recorded as unsolicited because it "was not started by a delivery", but if Claude merges both into one turn it would be taken as the reply to that delivery (not measured); the exact transcript form of a received `SendMessage`, and the visibility of `--name` and `ListAgents` on real 2.1.x, were not measured in this block; `--from` can be forged by an agent in the sandbox (as with chrome). Violation detection takes the word after `from <x>` in the `Peer notices:` line, so a worker that writes the daemon's `[contract]` notice as "from daemon / from the supervisor" causes a false `[peer violation]`; `fx:peer:notes` / `fx:peer:sent:*` are carried into resume by AOF, but resuming with uncopied notes or a half-used rate window is untested.

## 22. Rotation thresholds configured per model / CLI

Requirement: the handoff of a worker window must be configured per model; Claude Code with a 1M context should not hand off often. Before this, Claude's window was fixed at `--claude-window` (200,000) and the rotation ratio was one global `--handoff-ratio`, so a 1M opus worker rotated at 120k. This section only changes where the threshold comes from; the rotation flow (§4), handoff notes and generation identity are unchanged.

- **Rule**: rotate before a delivery when `used >= ratio × window` (the original rule; since §32 also checked when idle with no message). `window` and `ratio` are resolved independently; the first hit wins:
  1. the worker's roster entry `window` / `handoff_ratio`;
  2. the roster's top-level `handoff:` table, keyed first by `<cli>:<model>` (exact model string) then `<cli>`, value `{window?, ratio?}`;
  3. the `handoff:` table of the same name in `config.json` (static, validated at start, not hot-reloaded);
  3b. ratio only: the `handoff_ratio` of the slot's running profile card (section 12 model cards, `runtime_profile` including inheritance), source shown as `card <profile id>`;
  4. built-in: a Claude model name containing `[1m]` / `opus` / `fable` / `sonnet` (lower-case substring) -> 1,000,000, other Claude models -> `--claude-window`; Codex takes the self-reported `model_context_window` from its rollout and Pi its self-reported ctx_window ("measured"); an opencode worker uses `opencode_window` from the config, default 200000; only an explicit `window` overrides them;
  5. ratio only: a `--handoff-ratio` typed at `start`. `--claude-window` (item 4) stays the last layer of the window.
  6. ratio only: `ratio` of the `handoff:` block of `fleet.yaml` (below), source shown as `fleet.yaml#handoff`.
- **The defaults are configuration, not code**: the `handoff:` block of the project file `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml` holds `ratio` (shipped 0.3: every call re-reads the whole context, so large windows are rotated early to save tokens), `midturn_ratio`, `midturn_retry` and `midturn_max_splits` (section 34) and `handoff_timeout` (seconds to wait for the handoff note before the skeleton is used). Python has no default for any of these five. The private file (`$FLEETMUX_CONFIG`, else `~/.fleetmux/fleet.yaml`) may replace the whole block, so it must name all five keys. Keys and valid values: docs/contracts.md section 15.
  - **Read on every check** (`fleet_config.handoff()`): an edit needs no daemon restart. `ratio` applies at the next delivery check (an idle slot that the idle check of section 32 already looked at is checked again at its next delivery). `handoff_timeout` and the mid-turn values apply to handoffs ordered or retried after the edit; a deadline already set does not move.
  - **Bad block**: if the file cannot be read, or the block is invalid, partial or removed, the process keeps the last good values and logs each problem once to stderr. With no good value yet, a `start` that begins a new run, `start --resume` and the daemon (also a sub-fleet's) refuse to start and name `fleet.yaml#handoff`; a `start` that only attaches to a root fleet does not read the block itself. The last good values live in the process: a daemon restarted while the block is bad refuses to start.
  - **Typed flags only**: `--handoff-ratio` and `--handoff-timeout` default to unset. `start` writes `handoff_ratio` / `handoff_timeout` into `config.json` only when typed, and such a value ranks above the block. A start that attaches to a root fleet forwards a typed value to its sub-fleet.
  - **Runs started by older releases** have both keys in `config.json` whether or not they were typed. A resumed run, and the sub-fleets it opens, keep them as typed values.
  - **Mixed releases**: a release without this block rejects a private file that names it, so add a private `handoff:` block only when every running release knows it. A run whose `config.json` records `private_config: none` does not read a private file created later.
- **The model is the current running value**: `worker_model` (Claude, same for the supervisor) / `codex_model` / `pi_model` from `runtime_cfg(name)` (including `runtime_launch.overrides` written by a profile switch); the supervisor uses the same resolution, just without a worker entry.
- **Hot reload**: follows section 16 roster detection and takes effect at the next delivery check, without restart; `roster.changed` carries a `handoff` field with old and new values (table `table`, each worker). Validation: ratio is a number in (0,1), window a positive integer (booleans do not count), table keys name one of the CLIs `claude`, `codex`, `pi`, `opencode`, `dsh`; an entry holds only `window`, `ratio` and `midturn_ratio` (`midturn_ratio` is the mid-turn threshold of section 34); anything invalid rejects the whole file per section 16 and the old thresholds stay. A retiring worker keeps its old threshold until retired.
- **Display**: each resolution writes `handoff_window` (or `measured`) / `handoff_ratio` / `handoff_source` (e.g. `window: built-in opus; ratio: fleet.yaml#handoff`) into `fx:agent:<slot>`; after a roster change all are refreshed at once. `status`'s `context` carries these three, and the control line shows `handoff=<ratio>@<window>`.
- **Known boundaries**: the built-in table matches by substring; treating every `opus` as 1M is an assumption (the real window of each model was not checked at the API level; if some opus variant is really 200k, override with `claude:<model>`); model strings match exactly, so an alias (`opus`) and a full name (`claude-opus-…`) are different keys; an adopted sub-fleet supervisor still does not rotate (§19).

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (`tests/test_handoff_config.py` covers the `fleet.yaml#handoff` block; `tests/test_handoff_limits.py`: built-in table and flags, the five-level order, opus at 300k does not rotate / haiku at 120k rotates, profile running model, worker override + hot reload changing the threshold without restart, bad values reject the whole file and keep the old thresholds, config table validation, Codex measured window untouched unless explicitly overridden, status fields; `tests/test_roster.py` adds bad-value parameters and v2 hot reload into `status`).

**Claude window and self-compaction**: per the official docs `code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config.md`, bare `opus` (Opus 5.5) and bare `sonnet` are both native 1M, so the built-in table also counts `sonnet` as 1M (haiku still uses `--claude-window`). Claude Code self-compacts at about 967K (CHANGELOG 2.1.247); `autoCompactWindow` can only lower the compaction point, not raise it, so the fleet does **not** set it: rotation at `ratio` x 1M (300K with the shipped 0.3) comes before self-compaction. A roster that raises window close to 1M must leave its own margin (ratio x window below about 967K).

## 23. Brake and acceptance contract


### Acceptance contract, layer one: `artifacts_frozen`

- The task spec adds `"contract": {"artifacts_frozen": true}`: the `artifacts` written by the caller are the acceptance contract. When frozen, artifacts must be non-empty; artifacts appended in a supervisor decision are **always ignored**, with audit event `contract.append_ignored` (carrying the dropped entries); a supervisor that wants a change can only go BLOCKED with a reason, and the caller resubmits the task (R14: layer 1 leaves no channel).
- Frozen tasks get the extended checks like `contracts: true` tasks: `not_contains`, `json_keys` (new), `cmd_exit`. `json_keys`: the whole file is a JSON object, or the file ends with one JSON object (the verdict on the last line of a review report), and every listed key is present. `cmd_exit` runs only the argv frozen by the caller (no shell). It takes an optional `timeout_s` (default 120 seconds, at most 600). The command's combined stdout and stderr are written to the artifact path, and the other checks then read that file. A non-zero exit fails the artifact.
- Unfrozen, non-contract tasks: no change; the supervisor can still append, and extended keys are still ignored. `result.json` and the handover recheck of `start --resume` use the same rules (`extended_checks`).

### Brake: budget card + wall table (`fleetmux/brake.py`, `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#walls`)

- Default cash cap (overall design v2 ★3): for an account with `meter: usd_equiv`, a card without `max_usd` uses that account's `caps.day_usd / caps.night_usd` (default 40 USD day / 10 USD night), evaluated per scene (night reads batch B's `fx:night.brake_card`); if the caller sets one, the caller's value is used. A missing quota.json is still unknown and does not trip.
- Budget card: task spec `budget`, or `start --budget JSON` for service (written to config `budget`). Fields `pool / max_usd / max_week_pct / max_calls / max_rounds / max_hours / max_busy`. `pool` names an account id or alias from the `accounts` block of the private fleet.yaml (the shipped block is empty; a name not in that block is refused at once); by the account's `meter`: `usd_equiv` watches walls + an equivalent amount `max_usd` (the pool reading's increase over the baseline when the card was opened); `week_pct` watches walls + a weekly amount `max_week_pct` (the pool's absolute percentage). Fields that do not match the meter are refused at once. No real account name is written in code.
- Counting: `calls` = every delivery (counted in one place for v1 / v2); `rounds` = accepted supervisor decisions; `hours` = awake time since the card opened (section 35; time the machine slept is not counted; soft line; the task's `hours` is still the hard `budget_exhausted`); `max_busy` can only tighten `--max-busy`. Counters live in Redis `fx:brake`; a new task gets a new card.
- Pool readings come from the quota scheduler's `~/.fleetmux/quota.json` (configurable via config `quota_file`), which the daemon ingests into the hot `quota` block of fleet-superpowers; the brake reads only that block (§56): `{"pools": {"<account id>": {"cash_used_usd" | "week_used_pct", "at"}}}`. Missing / unreadable / older than 1 hour = unknown: shown in `brake.json`, **does not trip**.
- On reaching a line: pause (no delivery, no rotation, everything resumable) + audit `brake.tripped` + `<run>/brake.json` + an optional `brake_notify` argv (`start --brake-notify`, no shell). Under v2 the decision is made inside one transaction and the notification outside it.
- Resume: `fleetmux resume` is refused while the budget line is still exceeded (no matter who last wrote the pause record: a later wall, or F's hold rewriting the pause reason, does not unlock it); it needs `resume --raise LIMIT=VALUE` (the pool cannot be changed); a pause caused by a wall can be resumed directly. A new task opening a new card clears the brake pause of the previous card (a manual operator pause is not cleared).
- Pause review (section 24) never lifts a budget line. It lifts a wall pause at the wall's reset time, or after `pause_review_wall_retry_s`, at most 3 times for one wall; it checks the budget first.
- One wall table (plus the `safeguards` class, see the safeguards bullet below): account-level walls (only the usage / weekly / session limit wording of the Claude / Codex CLIs themselves, plus the pi CLI's wording for a Codex usage limit, "You have hit your ChatGPT usage limit", and "usage limit has been reached"; generic `rate limited` / `429 Too Many Requests` / `quota exceeded` are not walls: third-party tools such as Tavily / reddit / WebFetch print these words, and worker reports pasted into the supervisor screen carry them too); per-pool walls ("You've reached your Fable limit", "Run /usage-credits to continue", "Set up usage credits"; a bare `/usage-credits` is no longer recognized, and the percentage usage notices in `walls.notices`, "You've used N% of your ... limit", are skipped as whole lines and count as a wall at neither level, see section 48.4). Sources: the screen tail when stalled (past `stall_timeout`) (v1 in `on_stall`, v2 in the brake transaction of `Fleet.tick`, before the v2 hold); v1 also checks short turn texts (<=400 characters; long reports are not judged as walls). Three more paths do not wait for `stall_timeout`: (1) startup answers with a wall instead of READY -> slot `walled` (no death verdict, not blocked) + resumable pause; (2) short replies of v2 official CLIs (<=400 characters, `tui_turn` carries `wall_text`) and Pi model-error events (`candidate {tool: model, error}`) -> the delivery is committed as `walled` (result not used, not forwarded to the supervisor), and the original instruction is redelivered exactly once with "[Resumed after a usage wall]" (a replayed settled hits `settle.late`); (3) the watchdog wall gate (below).
- **On a wall, move down a model chain**: replaces the old "per-pool wall drops one tier within the same CLI; account-level walls and the supervisor only stop". The supervisor chain is, in priority order, Astra -> Fable -> Opus -> Sol -> DeepSeek Flash; the worker chain is Opus -> Sol -> Sonnet -> Terra -> DeepSeek Flash.
  - **One chain, one source**: chain = `fleet.yaml#discovery.supervisor` / `.worker` (a private file still overrides the whole block, section 52). It is resolved into the (cli, model) pairs usable on this machine: read `clis` / `gateways` from the `<fleet home>/discovered.yaml` cache (no live probes), re-ranked for the **current** mode with `discover.rank` (a cache ranked in an old mode is fine); without a cache, use the model cards (`fleet.yaml#models`) plus the models the run's slots are currently running; the cards carry no pi `openai-codex/...` ids, so without a cache the chain usually holds only claude entries. In a job sub-fleet (section 58) the run's `job_chain` replaces the discovery chain for each role it names. Only models named on the chain are used; unlisted models are never a fallback (reaching the end = stop). The supervisor chain accepts only `cli_pairs.CLIS` (pi / codex / claude). `walls.same_cli_downgrade` no longer takes part in wall decisions; the table stays as a copy of the deployer's `cli_tier_map.yaml` (`brake.downgrade` was deleted).
  - **The chain lives in Redis**: one project config file split into blocks; the most frequently read/written blocks are loaded into Redis, with timed write-back. Every time the daemon starts up (both a new start and `start --resume` go through `Fleet.run`), `Fleet.wall_chains_load` writes the resolved supervisor chain, worker chain, the mode of both chains, `discovery.supervisor_pi_thinking`, `discovery.worker_thinking`, the chain patterns of both roles, `walls.safeguards_prefer`, and the row states of the combination table for this run's `mode` into one key of this run's Redis, `fx:wall:chains` (JSON); wall decisions (`on_wall` / `wall_tick` / supervisor rotation) read only this key at runtime and no longer read `discovered.yaml` / `fleet.yaml`. If the key is missing (resuming a run started before this version), the first wall resolves once on the spot and writes it. A failed resolution stores an empty chain and the error, and a wall then stops. Existing parts that still read files are outside this item: the wall wording table (`brake.wall_table`, cached by file stamp) and `preflight_profile` on a CLI change (the same combination-table check as at actor startup). There is currently no runtime entry point for changing the chain, so this cut writes nothing back; when one is added, changes are written back on a timer into the `discovery` block of `fleet.yaml`, with the write-back interval as a parameter in the same `fleet.yaml`, and no new yaml file.
  - **Starting point**: the position of the current (cli, model) on the chain: the same entry, or the entry of the same CLI under the mode where that model first matches (`claude-opus-5-5` in the run and `opus` on the chain count as the same entry); if not on the chain (e.g. fable / haiku as a worker, a Codex worker), start from the head of the chain.
  - **Find the first usable entry below (`brake.wall_action`), skipping**: account-level wall → every model of that account; per-pool wall → only that one model; entries already walled in this run (Redis `fx:walls`, `'<cli>:<model>' → {cli, model, account, scope, at, agent}`, shared by all slots; no reset time, skipped for the rest of the run; a later per-pool wall never narrows an account-wall record); entries the combination table does not allow: the `cli_pairs` row of a worker with the **supervisor's current** CLI, and of the supervisor with **each live worker's current** CLI, must be verified with no `adapter_missing` (the smoke lifecycle's allowance for unverified rows does not count). **Account definition**: official CLIs (claude / codex) are one account per CLI; bridge CLIs (pi / opencode / dsh) are one account per provider, the part of the model id before the first `/` (`pi:openai-codex`, `pi:workbuddy-ds`): pi carries both a Codex account and a WorkBuddy account, and under "same CLI = same account" Sol hitting the Codex account wall would also skip deepseek flash on pi, so in task mode the worker chain could never reach its end.
  - **Execution**: `on_wall` only makes the pure decision (chain + skip rules + combination table), records `agent.wall` and the pending `wall_downgrade` (`cli` / `from` / `from_key` = before, `to_cli` / `to` / `key` = after), with a resumable pause. At an idle boundary (no delivery in flight, `idle` or `walled`) `wall_tick` executes: on a CLI change it first runs `preflight_profile(cli_change_ok=True)` (binary, pi's `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` / pi_home, the combination-table check the actor itself would do); refused -> the entry is recorded with `scope: refused`, audit `brake.switch_refused`, and the next entry on the chain is tried (`agent.wall` is updated to the new target; `brake.json` keeps the first decision); all refused -> stop as before (`brake.switch_exhausted`, the slot stays walled and the fleet paused). A passing entry starts the next generation with `wall_rotate`: the launch overrides `worker_cli` / `supervisor_cli` + that CLI's model key (worker: `brake.MODEL_KEY`; supervisor: `brake.SUPERVISOR_MODEL_KEY`, claude -> `supervisor_model`, pi -> `pi_model`); a worker switched to pi takes `discovery.worker_thinking` (default `high`), a supervisor switched to pi takes the run's supervisor pi thinking, else `discovery.supervisor_pi_thinking`; handoff skeleton (the same `skeleton` as a handoff_ratio rotation), screen saved, tab in place, old tab killed, v2 `confirm_retired`. After the new generation is READY only the wall's own pause is lifted (compared against `fx:brake.paused_by`; an F hold / operator pause / exceeded budget all stay paused; the audit name is still `brake.downgraded`). If another card swap (batch B night / live profile) has already changed the slot (comparing the **pre-switch cli + model**) -> voided (`brake.downgrade_superseded`). A slot that hit a wall at startup with the chain exhausted restarts its next generation with the original launch after the operator resumes (same for the supervisor slot).
  - **Supervisor**: the main fleet's supervisor slot also rotates down the supervisor chain (Astra -> Fable -> Opus -> Sol -> DeepSeek Flash), and the new generation's CLI may differ from the old one: tab `supervisor-1.<generation>`, not entered into the worker routing name table. The delivery channel follows the slot's current-generation config (`runtime_cfg` / `slot_cfg`: Stop hook mailbox for a Claude supervisor, plugin + operator_input for a pi supervisor); the reply-channel wording of already queued messages is written at enqueue time for the old CLI (pi: `Use fleet_decision ...`; official CLIs: `... JSON from AGENTS.md`), and at rotation `RuntimeV2.reword_pending` rewrites it for the new CLI inside the transaction (audit `queued.reworded`; the closing sentence of service worker requests, `fleet_result` <-> "exactly one JSON object", likewise), and `Fleet.request_message` now words by the slot's current-generation CLI. Adopted sub-fleet supervisors stop on a wall (see the safeguards bullet below). The advisor's wall never pauses the fleet: its tab is held and `advisor_tick` restarts it in place (section 27).
  - **Safeguards refusal (walls class `safeguards`)**: the Claude CLI's own refusal of one message ("API Error: <model>'s safeguards flagged this message (https://www.anthropic.com/legal/aup). This sometimes happens with safe, normal conversations. Claude Code can't respond to this message with <model>.", written as an API-error transcript row with error type `invalid_request` and `stop_reason: refusal`, ending the turn with StopFailure) is a wall, not an API error.
    - **Table**: `fleet.yaml#walls` keys `safeguards` (patterns, at least `safeguards flagged this message`), `safeguards_screen` (the screen line shape), `safeguards_prefer` (the preferred family) and `safeguards_error_types` (`[invalid_request]`, see below). A private `walls` block that lacks one of these keys gets the shipped value; a key present but empty (`[]`) turns that part off. The class is checked after `account` and before `model_pool`; a hit is `{scope: model_pool, kind: safeguards}`.
    - **Only CLI-owned text** (`brake.classify_wall(text, safeguards=...)`): a turn text (Stop hook, v1 turn, startup reply, advisor sheet) is model output and is never read for this class. StopFailure path: `claude_stop_failure` adds `api_text` (the newest main-chain transcript row with `isApiErrorMessage`, never model output, and only when it is stamped at or after the delivery's `received_at`: an older row belongs to an earlier turn), `details` (`error_details`) and `error_type`; `fleet.stop_failure_wall` reads `api_text` and `details`. `last_assistant_message` (possibly model output) counts only when no API-error row of this turn could be read (the CLI may write it after the hook runs) and the error type is one of `safeguards_error_types`; the `error` field (which the clear-after-result hook may fill from the message text) is never read. Only the safeguards class (any other wall wording through StopFailure keeps the API-error handling). Screen path (`brake.screen_wall`, the watchdog gate): the line must also match `safeguards_screen`, by default the CLI's own error line: the result glyph U+23BF, then `API Error:`, then the sentence on the same line; a report pasted into the pane (Stop hook feedback lines are indented, a typed prompt starts with `>`, JSON, the model's own bullet, a tool result without `API Error:`) does not match. A missed screen hit is still caught by StopFailure on hook-channel slots.
    - **Order in the StopFailure path** (`RuntimeV2.api_error_retry`; clear-after-result workers: `RuntimeV2.stop_failure`): after the identity / authorization checks and before the release as `failed`, the refusal and service branches, the held state, the same-model retry (`STOP_FAILURE_RESUME`) and the supervisor regeneration (`REGEN_RESUME_SUPERVISOR`, `regen_log`). On a hit `RuntimeV2.wall_commit` (shared with `wall_settle`) trips the brake through `Fleet.on_wall`, commits the delivery as `walled` (the slot is idle, never held) and queues the instruction again once (workers behind `brake.WALL_RESUME`, the supervisor unprefixed; `wall_of` = the old message id); a replayed event finds the message committed and queues nothing. The switch runs through `wall_tick` / `wall_target` / `wall_rotate`, which never write `regen_log` / `stopfail_*`: the regeneration budget (`supervisor_regen_max` 3 per `supervisor_regen_window_s` 3600 s) is not charged and the slot does not go held. A service-mode request is not failed: its instruction is queued again like any wall.
    - **Switch order** (`Fleet.wall_decide` with kind safeguards, `brake.wall_action(prefer=...)`): the role's WHOLE chain, not only the part below the flagged model: first the entries whose model matches a `safeguards_prefer` pattern (`discover.matches`, the `discovery.only_via` pattern form; shipped `[gpt]` = Astra / Sol / Terra on pi), in chain order, then the other entries in chain order (Fable included), skipping the flagged entry; the usual skips apply (account / model-pool / refused records, the pair table, the CLI-change preflight `preflight_profile(cli_change_ok=True)` in `wall_target`, whose re-decision keeps the kind); none usable -> stop. With the shipped supervisor chain a supervisor flagged on Opus takes Astra (above Opus), then Sol, then Fable. A run without `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` has every pi entry refused at the preflight and lands on Fable.
    - **Record**: `fx:walls['<cli>:<model>']` with `scope: model_pool` and `reason: safeguards` (only that model is skipped, never the account); the trip reason reads "hit a safeguards wall", `agent.wall` and the pending `wall_downgrade` carry `kind: safeguards`, a job sub-fleet's trace records `safeguards wall`. Recovery is the existing rule: the record stays for the rest of the run, a new run starts from the chain head. A flagged message on the next model adds that model's record too, so the walk is bounded by the chain and ends in stop. Pause review never lifts a safeguards stop (the flagged model would get the message again); resume stays with the operator.
    - **Sub-fleet supervisors**: a sub-fleet supervisor this fleet owns (`subfleet.adopted` false: no `adopt` in the config, or a generation other than 0) rotates itself on a safeguards wall (`wall_chained(name, kind)`); it runs official Claude only, so `wall_refused` refuses every non-Claude entry with `job_chain.NOT_CLAUDE` and the walk falls through to the second pass (Fable when Opus is flagged). **Current limit**: every child config the program writes today sets `adopt` (`subfleet.child_config`), and an adopted pane (generation 0) is the parent's: a context handoff never gives it a successor (`window = 0` for an adopted pane in the delivery path), the watchdog never kills it and regeneration skips it, so no existing rotation path replaces it. In practice every sub-fleet supervisor therefore stops on a safeguards wall: reason safeguards, the model recorded on its chain entry, the flagged message queued once behind the pause, and the parent told at once through `held_escalate` (its `subfleet-notices`, kind held, reason safeguards), as the adopted-refusal branch did before. The child-owned branch is in code and tested, but no current path reaches it.
    - **Any safeguards stop** (nothing usable, or an adopted pane) is escalated at once the same way (`held_escalate`; a root fleet: its channels / supervisor as for a held slot). The wall's brake pause holds the whole fleet until the switch (or, on a stop, until the operator resumes), as for any usage wall; on a service fleet one flagged request therefore pauses the fleet until the next model is READY.
  - **Not done**: switching back to the head of the chain *automatically* after the quota resets; parsing reset times from wall wording. The way back to the head is the parent supervisor's: a `heal` item `{"clear_wall": "<cli>:<model>"}` drops a wall record of this run and `"reopen": "head"` starts the slot from the head of the chain again (section 67).
  - **Remaining gaps**: the watchdog's dialog table is chosen by the run-level CLI (`watchdog.Watchdog.cli`) and official-CLI card rendering chooses slots by the run-level CLI (`RuntimeV2.render_cards`); this has been so since night card swaps, so a slot that changed CLI is still treated with the run's default CLI in these two places; mixing Claude TUI slots + Pi bridge slots in one run and in-place rotation between a Claude supervisor ↔ pi supervisor have had no real-cmux acceptance (unit tests: `tests/test_wall_chain.py`).
- A v1 task's `pause` now also stops delivery (it used to apply only to service / v2).

### E before F: the watchdog's wall gate (`Fleet.wall_gate`)

- Interface: `Fleet.wall_gate(slot, screen, source='watch') -> record | None`. The watchdog (F, §24, `watchdog.Watchdog.check`) calls it after every screen capture, before the dialog allowlist / unknown-dialog hold / stalled ladder / frozen. It looks only at the last `brake.GATE_TAIL`=25 lines of the screen (`brake.screen_wall`).
- Returns a record: the wall has tripped the brake (pause, `agent.wall`, audit `brake.tripped`, `brake.json` source=watch, optional notification; under v2 inside one transaction). On this tick the watchdog **presses no key, does not hold, does not kill or rotate** that slot: a wall menu (e.g. `1. Yes, switch...`) is never answered as a dialog. Calling again when the same agent's wall has already braked only returns the original record and does not trip twice. Exception: on the advisor tab the gate does not trip the brake; it holds the advisor slot, returns a record marked `advisor`, and the fleet keeps running. Returns None: no wall; the watchdog follows its own table.
- The hold exit (F's `turn_ended` → `release_held`) only puts the slot back to idle and **does not lift the pause**; F's hold rewrites the pause reason, but the brake record is kept separately in `fx:brake`, and `resume` rechecks the budget card every time and refuses when over the line. Exception (`RuntimeV2.handoff_pause_arm` / `handoff_pause_lift`): a pause caused by the slot's own handoff delivery being held (handoff timeout etc.) registers an ownership marker `agent.handoff_pause` at hold time (conditions: the fleet was not paused before the hold (the same handoff held twice in one tick, by expire and then `finish_rotation`, counts once), and the old pane is not a tab behind tmux); `RuntimeV2.tick` rechecks at the end of every tick, and only when all hold does the daemon resume automatically: the pause has not been rewritten by another reason (`fx:service.at` unchanged; every pause rewrites it; only at counts, not reason), the task is still running, the old generation is confirmed closed (kill + `confirm_retired`: handoff delivery cancelled + `stop_confirmed`, `launch_ready=1`), the new generation is READY (idle, `runtime_ready=1`, its own new lease), the brake has not tripped (`fx:brake.tripped` / `paused_by` empty) and the budget is not exceeded. Lifting is audited as `execution.auto_resumed`, and the slot and the supervisor each get a notice card; if only the brake is in the way → audit `execution.auto_resume_refused`, and it waits for the operator's resume as before. Lifting writes no lease / message: uncertain deliveries of other slots stay held. Slots behind tmux, pauses already set by another reason at hold time, and pauses with several stacked reasons stay as before.


## 24. Five-state watchdog (transport v2)

v2 transport only; v1 `check_stalls` / `on_stall` are unchanged. Code: `fleetmux/watchdog.py`, called by `RuntimeV2.tick` outside the Redis intents (screen capture / key presses / kill / new generation are all side effects); settlement (hold / enqueue / publishing a result) still goes through `transaction`. Every non-idle slot is judged once per `watch_interval` (default 5 s). The watchdog never judges a slot that is held, dead, retired, delegated, in handoff or clearing, or that has a `handoff_id` or no pane; someone else owns the next step for those slots. a state change writes the audit `agent.state` (from / to / evidence).

| State | Criterion | Action |
|---|---|---|
| `idle` | No in-flight delivery and not starting | Nothing; never judged dead however long it lasts (E1) |
| `working` | In flight, and **any** of the three signals in the table below moved within `stall_window` (default 300 s) (one moving resets all window baselines), and the transcript / rollout moved within `stall_timeout` (default 1200 s) | None |
| `prompting` | The last `tail_lines` lines of the screen match the dialog-table allowlist (also checked while starting; the trust dialog comes before READY) | Save the screen to `<run>/watchdog/`; **a text match is not an authorization**: also verify ① the pane belongs to this fleet's registry and is not adopted by a sub-fleet, ② the entry's `verify` rule proves the concrete action / path in the dialog lies within the run's existing authorization; only when both pass send the configured keys (audit `watch.prompt_answered` with `authorized`), otherwise treat it as an unknown dialog: save the screen + pause dispatch + mark the slot `prompt_wait` (lease untouched; once a human answers, that turn settles normally), audit `watch.prompt_unverified`. If delete / move words appear at the bottom of the screen (§24 delete classification), no key is pressed even when the allowlist authorizes it. The same dialog on the same delivery still present after `max_hits` (3) answers → treat as `stalled` |
| `prompting` (unknown) | Matches `unknown_markers` but is not on the allowlist | Save the screen and press no key. A non-delete dialog goes to the supervisor first (see "Non-delete dialogs go to the supervisor"). A delete-class or unreadable dialog, or one the supervisor cannot rule on now, marks the slot `prompt_wait` (not a hold) and pauses dispatch |
| `stalled` | In flight, and ① **all** three signals still for a full `stall_window` (a slot whose Claude Stop for this delivery was deferred for background tasks and whose transcript has not moved since does not take ①; see "Stop deferred" below); or ② transcript / rollout still for a full `stall_timeout` (hard cap: catches a screen that keeps spinning while the stream behind it has died) | Escape, at most `stall_escapes` (3) times, spread evenly over `stall_grace` (300 s); no hold. If the **transcript** moves meanwhile → `working` (the screen does not count: Escape itself changes the screen, and counting it would mean the ladder never reaches frozen) |
| `frozen` | stalled for a full `stall_grace`; or the screen digest unchanged after 3 Escapes; or `ps` sees the tty process in state `U` (Linux `D`) twice in a row | Save screen -> skeleton handoff note -> new generation (launch-held) `tab_into` in place -> `kill_pane` (only surfaces in this fleet's registry; kills their tty process tree) -> `confirm_retired` (delivery cancelled, `stop_confirmed`) -> open the launch gate -> worker: send the supervisor a `FROZEN` report, the original instruction is **not rerun**; supervisor: the original message is requeued with a note; service: publish the request as `stalled`. **Since section 43 an official Claude worker first resumes its own session** (`claude --resume`, same order, same tab) and gets that delivery again; only a resumed generation that freezes again takes the fresh-generation path here. Run paused / adopted by a sub-fleet / pane not in this fleet's registry -> hold only, no kill. Frozen rotation also does nothing while the slot waits on a confirmation dialog (`prompt_wait`; a dialog never authorizes a kill) or while the slot's `session_env` route port is closed (section 57); the watchdog check skips that slot until the port answers. A frozen advisor slot is not rotated here: it takes the section 27 fault branch (`advisor_restart`) |

- **The three stall signals**: a slot is stalled when all three signals stay still for `stall_window` (default 300 s), or the transcript stays still for `stall_timeout` (default 1200 s). A long tool call leaves the transcript still, so all three signals must be still.

  | Signal | How it is read | What counts as alive |
  |---|---|---|
  | Session record | `Fleet.last_activity`: mtime of the Claude main transcript + sub-agent transcripts, the Codex rollout, the pi session file, and the delivery time `busy_since` | Newer than the window baseline |
  | Screen | First 16 hex chars of the sha256 of each tick's `capture_output` (a separate field from the frozen criterion's `esc_digest`) | Differs from the window baseline; an empty capture = unreadable |
  | Process-tree CPU | One `ps -axo pid=,ppid=,tty=,time=`: cumulative CPU seconds of the processes on the pane's tty (tmux panes use `#{pane_tty}`) **plus all their descendants by ppid**. Tool subprocesses of Claude Code / Codex start their own session (tty `??`) and are invisible to `ps -t`, hence the ppid walk | Sum of positive per-tick increments within the window > `stall_cpu_pct` (default 5) percent of `stall_window` (default 300 s); a tick where the total drops because a child exited counts 0; `ps` failure / no process on the tty = unreadable |

  Baselines live in slot fields `quiet_since / quiet_act / quiet_digest / quiet_cpu / quiet_gain` (same style as `esc_digest / esc_at`); `classify` stays a pure function with new inputs `quiet` (seconds all three signals have been still together) and `stall_window`. **An unreadable signal cannot prove stillness**: the window restarts at that tick and only the hard cap applies (the fake mgr has no CPU probe, so existing tests are unaffected). Baselines reset on slot idle / settlement / same-generation rebind / frozen rotation. The new signals only govern "entering stalled"; leaving stalled still depends on the transcript alone. The `frozen` criterion "`ps` sees U/D twice in a row" is unchanged and still looks only at the tty's direct processes.
  - **Stop deferred = waiting for its own background tasks, not stillness**: Cause: an ended Claude turn that waits for its own background task leaves all three signals still; Escape on an empty input box changes nothing, so the slot froze and the kill also killed the task. Process-tree CPU does not cover it (a `nohup` process orphaned to pid 1 cannot be found by ppid). Fix: when the hook `fleet.claude_stop` sees `background_tasks`, besides the audit `turn.deferred` (now with `types`) it also sends a `turn_deferred` event (`agent / generation / run_id / thread / tasks[{id,type,status,description}]`); the daemon (`RuntimeV2.handle`), after checking same run / same generation / same session, writes the slot fields `bg_wait=<current delivery>`, `bg_wait_at`, `bg_wait_act=<last_activity at that moment>`, `bg_tasks` only when the lease is `received`; otherwise it only audits (held and idle slots are not written). Watchdog `check`: `bg_wait == current scope` and `last_activity <= bg_wait_act + BG_WAIT_GRACE` (30 s: Claude Code writes the hook result into the transcript about 1 s after the Stop hook returns, and that does not count as waking) -> this tick skips `quiet_for`, `quiet = 0`, `quiet_since` reset; if the condition fails (another delivery / the transcript moved after the grace = the background task finished and woke the turn) -> clear `bg_wait` and restart the window from this tick. **Unchanged**: the `stall_timeout` hard cap (`age` still counts: a hung background task still goes stalled -> Escape -> frozen), `ps` U/D, dialogs, the wall gate, `classify` itself; a stall in the middle of a turn has no Stop and gets no marker. `bg_wait` is cleared wherever `quiet_since` is: slot idle / settlement, hold-exit release, same-generation rebind, frozen rotation. This corrects a misjudgment, so there is no switch (`--stall-window 0` still turns the whole window rule off). Tests `tests/test_turn_deferred.py`, `tests/test_watchdog_stall_window.py` (five deferred cases).
  - **The CPU threshold is not 0**: an idle Claude TUI still uses about 0.6 to 1.5 percent of one core. The default of 5 percent leaves a margin over that idle use. A child doing real work passes it easily. Spinners are covered by the screen signal, not by CPU.
  - **Screen assumption**: all three CLIs redraw a timer or spinner while a tool runs, so a long tool call changes the screen every tick and is not judged stalled. A paste without Enter leaves the screen still and is judged stalled after about one `stall_window`. An API retry countdown changes the screen, so it is not stalled (that is the startup READY deadline path). The screen signal is the first 16 hex characters of the sha256 of the captured screen.
  - **The advisor slot (§27) gets no exemption and no separate threshold**: the resident advisor is always official Claude; during long thinking its screen keeps changing with a seconds counter, so the window never judges it stalled; a consultation with all three signals still for 300 s has already exceeded `ADVICE_WAIT` (180 s), so its answer is dropped as late anyway (`advice.late`); conversely, exempting it would mean that when the advisor truly hangs, every dispatch first waits 180 s and then goes out with defaults, until the hard cap. A one-shot `claude -p` advisor is not a pane and is not the watchdog's business.
  - **Impact**: `stall_timeout` defaults to 1200 s in `fleet.DEFAULTS`; it is shared by v1 and v2, so v1 `check_stalls` uses the same value; `stall_window` / `stall_cpu_pct` apply only to the v2 watchdog. The v2 wall gate's `stalled_screens` still captures by `stall_timeout` (the watchdog `check` passes the wall gate first itself, so a window-judged stalled slot that hits a wall also gets no key press).

- **Frozen rotation order and failure**: start the new generation first, then kill the old one (same order as `respawn` / handoff, which keeps the layout). Until the old generation is confirmed retired the new one cannot get work: it is `starting` (`pump` only delivers to `idle`), the old lease still holds the delivery (`offer` needs an idle lease, `register_slot` reports `busy`), and with `launch_ready=0` its READY is refused. If any step fails (new generation does not start / cannot enter the original tab / old generation cannot be killed or survives the kill) → kill the new generation first, restore the slot fields to the old generation, hold (delivery stays uncertain, not cancelled, not rerun), audit `watch.rotate_failed`; only if the new generation also cannot be killed can two sessions coexist: slot held + launch gate closed + task blocked, and the report names both panes for a human.
- Dialog table `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#watchdog_prompts` (replaceable with `--prompt-table`), hot reloaded when its mtime or size changes. Table keys: `tail_lines` (default 25, 1-500), `max_hits` (default 3, 1-20), `prompts` (`name`, `match`, optional `any`, `keys`, `verify`), `unknown_markers`, `delete_markers`, `user_approve`. `user_approve` registers the keys that approve a delete dialog from the phone; it is read only from an explicit `--prompt-table` file, never from fleet.yaml; a bad edit is audited as `watch.table_rejected` and the old table stays. Every entry must have a `verify`: `own_cwd` (all paths in the dialog are this slot's directory, trusted at spawn), `paths_in_run` (all paths are in this slot's directory or `<run>/workspace`, i.e. already authorized by `--add-dir`); a path that cannot be read -> no key. Long paths wrapped by the TUI are joined back as "consecutive single-token lines". Default table: `read_outside` (Enter), `trust_folder` (recorded from Claude 2.1.280: the default selection is "No, exit", keys `Down, Enter`, and the screen must show the selection marker (U+276F) before "No, exit"), `trust_folder_legacy` (Codex / older Claude, Enter, wording not recorded). **Deletion always goes to the user for confirmation**: the `dangerous rm` dialog is not on the allowlist; it is listed in `unknown_markers` (save the screen, mark the slot `prompt_wait`, pause dispatch, press no key; the slot is not held); the old `rm_in_cwd` check is deleted; the fleet installs no PermissionRequest hook that auto-answers delete confirmations.
- **A dialog waiting for a human is not a hold **: `RuntimeV2.prompt_wait` only writes the slot fields `prompt_wait=<scope>` (the in-flight delivery id; for a starting slot `startup-g<generation>`) / `prompt_wait_at` / `prompt_route=user`, audits `execution.prompt_wait`, posts one notice card each to the slot and the supervisor, and pauses dispatch; **lease, message and slot state are untouched**. For a marked slot the watchdog: marker != current scope -> clear (`watch.prompt_cleared reason=scope_changed`); transcript / rollout has not moved -> skip this tick (no capture, no stalled / frozen ladder, zero key presses, even if the dialog scrolls off the bottom), except in a run with channels: there a delete dialog the user approved from the phone is pressed after the full fresh-screen check (section 53); transcript moved (the human answered, the worker continues) -> clear the marker, back to `working`, judge normally; slot idle / after READY -> clear (`inactive`); slot goes held / dead etc. -> clear (`hands_off`). `resume` does not clear the marker: if the human resumes without answering the dialog -> the slot keeps waiting and the fleet is not paused again; if the human answers without resuming -> that turn settles normally and the report is recorded; when the dialog is gone and the pause it set is still the only pause, dispatch resumes automatically (section 48.2, `prompt_pause_lift`). A manual resume is needed only when another pause source exists. A starting slot carrying this generation's `startup-g` marker is not judged dead by `expire()` on `startup_deadline` (waiting for a human is not failing to start); READY registers as usual.
- An opencode or dsh permission request (`prompt_channel` opencode or dsh) waits the same way, but activity never clears it. The watchdog reads no screen and presses no key; only a supervisor permission reply or the user in the pane answers it.
- **Delete classification **: `watchdog.dialog_class` does a case-insensitive, word-boundary match on the bottom `tail_lines` lines against the built-in word list `DELETE_MARKERS` (rm / rmdir / unlink / del / delete / remove / rimraf / shred / trash / purge / wipe / erase / truncate / drop table|database|schema / git clean / git reset --hard / git push --force|-f / git branch -D / git checkout -- / git restore / dd if= / > /dev/null / **mv / move / rename** (moving and renaming count as deleting) / the Chinese words for delete, empty, remove, clean up, move and rename, English words including inflected forms) or `delete_markers:` in `fleet.yaml#watchdog_prompts` (append-only; built-in words cannot be removed; a bad hot-reloaded table keeps the old one) -> `delete`; an unreadable screen -> no classification (goes to the user). With a `security.yaml` policy the class also comes from the guard action rules (`send_out`, `config`, `install`, `secret_read`, `real_money`); every class other than `other` goes to the user, never to the supervisor, and no key is pressed. Err on the strict side: misclassifying as delete only costs the user one extra question. The delete class always goes to the user; neither the program (including the allowlist) nor the supervisor presses keys. **With `security.yaml`**: Claude's PreToolUse gate lets **this one tool action** through only on **a valid single-use ticket from the user personally** (`fx:guard:ticket:<action_hash>`, written by `RuntimeV2.user_dialog_answer` after two-step confirmation); this is not the program or the supervisor answering a delete dialog for the user, and the delete dialog itself still goes to the user per this item. Without a policy the hook's existing baseline (`test_no_permission_hook_answers_delete_dialogs` and the like) is unchanged.
- **Non-delete dialogs go to the supervisor (defect F′ 8.3; rule: apart from deleting files, anything a worker needs a human for is handled by the supervisor)**: an allowlisted dialog that cannot be verified / an unknown dialog is classified `other`, and when task mode is running, not paused, the slot belongs to this fleet and is not adopted, the slot is not the supervisor, and the supervisor is not held / dead / starting / handoff / clearing / walled etc. and is not itself waiting on a dialog → `RuntimeV2.prompt_escalate`: mark the slot `prompt_route=supervisor` (likewise no slot hold, no pause), `fx:escalations['dialog:<slot>:g<generation>'] = {kind: dialog, generation, session_id, scope, delivery_id, prompt, screen, digest(first 16 hex chars of the sha256 of the screen bottom), keys_approve, keys_reject: [Escape], message_id, delivered_at}`, queue a `[dialog]` for the supervisor (raw screen bottom + how to answer), audit `watch.prompt_escalated`. `keys_approve`: for an allowlist entry, the table's keys; for an unknown dialog `Enter` only when the cursor visible at the screen bottom is on Yes (`❯ 1. Yes` / `❯ Yes`), otherwise no keys (a supervisor approve also goes to the user). The supervisor answers `approve | reject` in `escalations` of its decision JSON (`revise` is a format error); `escalation_apply` only marks the record `answered` and **presses no key**. On the next tick the watchdog recaptures the screen and presses only if generation / session / scope / pane ownership / screen-bottom digest / same dialog / delete re-classification **all** hold (approve presses `keys_approve`, reject presses Escape, audit `watch.prompt_answered authorized=supervisor:<decision>`), then clears the marker and deletes the record; dialog gone → `dialog.stale(dialog_gone)`, no key; a different dialog → `dialog_changed`, no key, reclassify and register again; identity changed → `dialog.stale(identity)`; reclassified as delete → `dialog.reclassified_delete`, goes to the user. If a human answers in the pane while waiting (transcript moves) → delete the record and judge normally. **Fallback to the user** (= the delete path: `prompt_wait`, pause, notice): supervisor unavailable (at registration or while waiting), record gone (e.g. a new task cleared `fx:escalations`), no answer within `dialog_escalation_timeout` of the supervisor receiving `[dialog]` (default 600 seconds, config key, `validate_config` requires ≥ 60), the supervisor replies RUNNING without answering (the first bounce-and-correct does not count as a strike, the second goes to the user), the supervisor itself hits a dialog; audit `dialog.to_human(reason=…)`. service mode also goes to the supervisor (see the item below). Rejecting a trust dialog presses Escape, which makes that Claude session exit (then liveness → hold); the supervisor prompt says so.
- **A worker reporting Blocked goes to the supervisor**: the `Blocked:` section of a task-mode worker report (`blocked` in the v2 settlement JSON; `none`, `-`, `n/a` and the Chinese word for none do not count) -> `fx:escalations['blocked:<slot>'] = {kind: blocked, slot, item(contract owner / running row / latest dispatch id), reason, message_id}`, and the supervisor's `[worker report]` gets a `[blocked]` how-to-answer appended. The supervisor answers `approve | revise(note required) | reject` in `escalations` -> the worker is queued `[Supervisor ruling] [<item>] <decision>: <note>` (for a contract task it is recorded under that row and the row returns to running; the next report settles it as usual), audit `blocked.ruled`; dispatching to that slot in the same reply = answered (answer and dispatch together -> the dispatch wins, no separate ruling is sent, audit `escalate.answered_by_dispatch`); the supervisor's correction message goes to the **head** of its pending queue (like worker corrections); a RUNNING reply to that report that neither answers nor dispatches -> the first bounce-and-correct does not count as a strike, later ones do. A supervisor replying `BLOCKED` still `finish('blocked')` (the human escape hatch stays open); the prompt says only delete / move matters or things truly only the user can decide warrant BLOCKED. The fleet is not paused. **A ruling is bound to the generation that asked**: the record also stores the asking `generation` / `label`; if at ruling time the slot has rotated (or the label changed) -> audit `blocked.stale`, not delivered, the same check as service mode's "request is no longer dispatched" (`Fleet.ruling_stale`, one stale path shared by both modes). Old records without `generation` are not checked. When a split is already approved, a ruling for the same task is handled per section 44 "approval reserves the slot".
- Self-check: `capture_fail_alarm` (3) consecutive capture failures write one `watch.capture_fail` (error; reported once per run of consecutive failures).
- **Hold exit**: when a slot is held (uncertain / expired), **any turn end of the same session and same generation** (Claude Stop via `tui_turn`; since defect E the turn no longer has to be started by this delivery: Claude session turns are serial, so no turn is running at Stop; a Stop with unfinished background tasks never enters `tui_turn` anyway, it only audits `turn.deferred` and sends the `turn_deferred` event, and on a held slot the daemon only audits and does not release) makes the hook send `turn_ended`; **Codex** has no official withdrawal and another turn's end cannot prove the queued one will not run, so the round in the rollout carrying that delivery's token must have reached `task_complete` (`codex_queue.token_turn`); not started / not finished → `turn.ignored` as before; after checking identity the daemon does `resolve_uncertain(cancelled, execution_stopped=True)`, the slot returns to idle, the result is **not applied**, the pause state is untouched (audit `hold.released`), and a Codex slot then does `codex_queue.clear` (otherwise the next delivery is refused with "still unsettled"). Forged events from an old generation / another session / a slot that is not held are always refused. **In task mode the exit informs the supervisor**: when a worker slot is released, the last text of that turn (tail ≤ 4000 chars) is queued to the supervisor as `[<slot> report]\nHELD-RELEASED: the program held this slot (<uncertain_reason>) and did not apply the result of its turn; …` (written like the `API ERROR` report for exhausted API errors below; sent only while the task is running), audit `hold.released` with `notified`; service mode does not send it (the request already has a public result, and `result.late` keeps the raw text). Settlement semantics do not change, so there is no switch; it guarantees the hold exit always gives the supervisor a turn, so a run cannot sit with every agent idle and a dispatch unsettled.
- **An API error holds only that slot (StopFailure; decision: hold only the slot that failed)**: for official Claude workers on the task-mode hook channel (also the supervisor, see "Supervisor StopFailure" below; the advisor takes the section 27 fault branch; service mode see "service mode: API error / model refusal" below; section 28 clear-after-every-result unchanged). Cause: a turn that ends in StopFailure gets no further Stop, so its delivery stayed received and nobody settled it. Now:
