  - **StopFailure = turn ended = execution stopped**: the hook (`fleet.claude_stop_failure`) still audits + `turnend`, and in task mode also sends a `stop_failure` event (without waiting for the daemon); **only the delivery handed over by this session's hook** (`hook_delivered`: the bound message / delivery / session match the lease) carries identity; a delivery still in the mailbox and not yet handed over belongs to a later turn (the failed one was some other turn a human typed), so it is only audited and is still handed over by the fallback hook. The daemon (`RuntimeV2.api_error_retry`) checks same run / same generation / same session, lease `received` (or already held as `uncertain / expired` by the lease-expiry item below; in the incident the hold came first, then the StopFailure), PEL still owned by that consumer (`stop_gate`, as in §28), message is a worker / continuation instruction; then it commits `failed` (result `{stop_failure: raw text}`, **not applied, not a report**).
  - **Redeliver the same message to the same slot**: copy the original message the way `wall_settle` does (same request_id / kind / deadline / mid-turn rotation fields, with a `STOP_FAILURE_RESUME` paragraph prepended: "retried after an API error: check the directory first, do not redo"), placed at the **head** of the slot's queue; counting reuses §17's `replay` and `max_attempts`: this is attempt number `replay` (default 1), redelivered only if `replay < max_attempts`, and the new message gets `replay+1`. **No separate retry queue.**
  - **Only that slot is held during backoff**: slot `state=held` (equivalent to `hold(pause=False)`: no dispatch, watchdog hands-off, still counted in `max_busy`), writes `stopfail_at / stopfail_g / stopfail_retry_at`; backoff seconds `runtime_v2.STOP_FAILURE_BACKOFF = (60, 120, 300, 600)`, the k-th by `replay` (past the end, the last one). **The fleet is not paused and the supervisor is not held**; the slot and the supervisor each get a notice card `<槽> API 错误（原文前 200 字），N 秒后自动重试第 k 次` ("<slot> API error (first 200 chars of the raw text), automatic retry number k in N seconds"; a UI card that starts no supervisor turn), audit `execution.held` (`paused: false`) + `stop_failure.retry`.
  - **Automatic recovery**: the daemon's own tick (`stopfail_tick` in `expire`) returns the slot to idle when the time comes, and `pump` redelivers the head message to the same slot; no external timer (§1). If before then a Stop hook of **the same session and same generation** is waiting (`armed` key: a human logged in again, typed something, and the turn ended normally) → release early (`stop_failure.released reason=a normal turn ended`; to guard against a stale `armed`, it is ignored for `ARMED_TTL` 15 seconds after the StopFailure). The redelivery reaches Claude through the hook mailbox: if a Stop hook is waiting it is handed over directly; otherwise by the fallback hook. **The StopFailure group gets its own asyncRewake `claude-rewake`, the same as the Stop group's** (task mode, non-advisor), because a turn woken by the fallback hook that ends in StopFailure again has neither a Stop hook nor the old fallback hook; the new fallback increments the `rewake` counter and the old one yields automatically.
  - **Attempts exhausted**: still only that slot is held (`held`, no more dispatch, no timed release), **no pause, task not judged blocked**; the supervisor is queued `[<slot> report] API ERROR: …` (raw text, attempt count, and "the instruction was not rerun, it may be half done, the slot stays held until that session ends a turn normally; reassign it or reply BLOCKED"), audit `stop_failure.exhausted`; a managed run (`lifecycle_close.managed`) also sends tg / parent via `lifecycle_close.notify` (`slot_held`, a subprocess, sent in the tick outside the transaction). After that, a normal turn end of that session → released to idle through `armed` as above (no redelivery).
  - **By default only one retry**: `max_attempts` defaults to 2 (the run in that incident also had 2), so by default there is one redelivery after 60 seconds and a second failure exhausts it; for more (120 / 300 / 600 seconds) start the run with a larger `--max-attempts` (it governs both §17 resume replay and this item). Reason: a StopFailure can come mid-turn (in the incident attach-1 had already run tools for a minute), so a redelivery replays half-done work and must share the resume-replay cap.
- **service mode: an API error / model refusal holds only that slot**: Cause: in service mode a StopFailure used to leave the delivery received until the request timed out, which held the slot and paused the fleet. Now:
  - **Entry same as task mode**: in service mode the hook `fleet.claude_stop_failure` also sends the `stop_failure` event (likewise only for the delivery handed over by this session's hook), and reads the `stop_reason` of the last main-chain assistant message from the tail of the session record; if it is `refusal` it adds `refusal: true` (`fleet.claude_refused`). The daemon `api_error_retry` checks the same way, commits `failed` (not applied), then hands over to `RuntimeV2.service_stop_failure`.
  - **The request fails at once; the fleet does not redeliver**: publish `failed` (`error` = `API error: …` or `model refusal: …`); whether to retry is up to the requester (e.g. the requester's bridge program's own retry rules), consistent with §7 "service requests are not rerun" and opencode's "a refusal is not redelivered and the model is not switched".
  - **Only that slot is held, released after backoff**: `state=held`, backoff seconds as in `STOP_FAILURE_BACKOFF`, the k-th by this slot's consecutive failure count `stopfail_streak`; when the time comes the same `stopfail_tick` returns it to idle (or a normal turn end of the same session releases it early). The fleet is not paused and the supervisor is not held; the slot and the supervisor each get a notice card (including `连续第 k 次，上限 N`, "consecutive failure k, cap N"), audit `execution.held` (`paused: false`).
  - **A refusal gets a new session**: on `refusal` the slot is also marked with §37's `fresh_pending=1` and `stopfail_fresh=1`, and after release it goes through `fresh_rotation` (save the screen, generation +1, no handoff note): with requests queued in the mailbox the swap happens in `deliver` (the §37 path); with an empty mailbox `idle_rotate` swaps in place when it sees `stopfail_fresh`, instead of clearing it as "dispatch cancelled" like an ordinary `fresh_pending`. The refusing session takes no more work (in the incident the same session took the next batch the next night and was refused again). `fresh_rotation` clears both markers whether the swap succeeds or starting the new session fails (on failure, as in §37: the old session stays). An ordinary API error keeps the session, and the next message is handed over by the StopFailure group's asyncRewake (service mode now has it too).
  - **Consecutive cap**: `stopfail_streak` accumulates across generations and resets to zero when any request completes normally (`ok`); reaching `service_stopfail_max` (key in the run's config.json, default `runtime_v2.SERVICE_STOPFAIL_MAX = 3`; no start flag yet) → the slot stays held with no timed release and no pause, audit `stop_failure.exhausted`, a supervisor card + tg / parent notice for a managed run (`stopfail_notices`; a sub-fleet with `parent_config` does not count as managed and only gets the card; alerting relies on the requester's own consecutive-failure alert, e.g. a review bridge); after that only a normal turn end of that session releases it.
  - **Unchanged**: a timeout without StopFailure (unknown whether execution stopped) still holds (since section 43 only that slot, no fleet pause); the advisor and section 28 clear-after-every-result slots do not take this path (a service supervisor does, see "Supervisor StopFailure" below). Verified by `tests/test_service_stop_failure.py` (8 cases).
- **Supervisor StopFailure (the supervisor is recovered like a worker)**: Cause: the StopFailure hook posted no event for a supervisor, so a failed supervisor turn was never settled. Now:
  - **Hook**: `fleet.claude_stop_failure` posts the `stop_failure` event for every v2 agent: workers, the supervisor, a sub-fleet's adopted supervisor (after `subfleet.delegated_route` re-targets the hook to the child: `name='supervisor'`, generation 0, the child's Redis), and the advisor. Only the delivery handed over by this session's hook carries identity, as before. The supervisor's StopFailure group also gets the `claude-rewake` asyncRewake (the advisor still does not), so a re-sent message is handed over after a failed turn. An adopted supervisor already had it through the parent worker's pinned hooks.
  - **Daemon, task mode**: `RuntimeV2.api_error_retry` accepts the supervisor slot and its message kinds (`supervisor`, `followup`). The checks are the same as for a worker (same run / generation / session, lease `received / uncertain / expired`, `stop_gate`). The turn is committed `failed` (not adopted, never a decision), and the same message is queued at the head (`requeue_after_stop_failure`) with `STOP_FAILURE_RESUME_SUPERVISOR` in front ("your decision was not read; answer this message again"). The worker text "check your directory, do not redo" does not fit a supervisor. Only that slot is held for the backoff (`STOP_FAILURE_BACKOFF`, `replay` / `max_attempts`, no pause), and `stopfail_tick` releases it. `adopted_held_tick` ignores a slot with `stopfail_at` (it is `stopfail_tick`'s), otherwise it would take the slot back at once and queue the message twice.
  - **Retries used up**: a worker's supervisor is told as before. A supervisor cannot be told about itself, so it is escalated upwards (`held_escalate`, below). Its message is not dropped: its turns are decisions nobody else can make, so the same message is queued again and retried at the longest backoff (`STOP_FAILURE_BACKOFF[-1]`, 600 s), with one more escalation per failed attempt, until the API answers. The run is never paused for it.
  - **Model refusal**: a root supervisor's retry is marked `fresh_pending=1` / `stopfail_fresh=1`, so the message is delivered to a fresh session (section 37 `fresh_rotation`). An adopted supervisor's pane belongs to the parent and the child cannot replace it: the refused message is not re-sent, the slot stays held, and the parent's supervisor is told (`stop_failure.refused`).
  - **service mode**: the existing service rule applies. A message with a live request fails that request at once (`service_stop_failure`, never re-sent, `stopfail_streak`); a settled supervisor decision resets the streak (`release`). A message with no live request behind it is re-sent like task mode, so it is not lost.
  - **Advisor (decision)**: it posts the event, but its consultation is never re-sent (section 27: faults fall back, never re-run). `api_error_retry` holds it on the advisor branch of `hold` (`pause=False`, `advisor_fault`; `advisor_tick` restarts it in place and the waiting consultation falls back at once instead of after the stall ladder). Audit `stop_failure.advisor`.
  - Verified by `tests/test_supervisor_hold.py` (8 cases, including the incident reproduction `test_subfleet_supervisor_stop_failure_is_recovered_and_redelivered_without_any_command`).
- **Held watchdog backstop (rule: guarding, watching and recovering are the fleet's own roles, the daemon manages agents, never an outside session)**: `RuntimeV2.held_watch_tick`, called in every `tick` outside any intent, before `adopted_held_tick`.
  - **Stuck**: a slot (not the advisor, whose holds `advisor_tick` restarts) is `held` or waits on a user dialog (`prompt_wait` with `prompt_route=user`) for `held_after_s` awake seconds. The held clock is `held_since`, stamped on the watch's first look and cleared when the slot leaves `held`; a dialog uses `prompt_wait_at`. Awake time is used, so a lid-closed sleep does not count. Two cases are not stuck: a StopFailure backoff whose `stopfail_retry_at` is still ahead (it releases itself), and a session record (`Fleet.last_activity`) that moved since the hold (a new episode starts). A run paused that long with no held slot and no dialog is reported once per pause (`fx:service.at`); that pause is never lifted.
  - **Recovery, once per episode (`held_tried`)**: (1) The turn is proven stopped (`stop_proof`): the StopFailure hook's `turnend` mark, or an API-error end in the transcript tail (`claude_hook.failure_end`), at or after the held delivery's `received_at`. The daemon then builds the `stop_failure` event that never arrived and settles it through `api_error_retry` (audit `held_watch.recovered` with `proof`). (2) A sub-fleet's adopted supervisor whose hold set the pause: the daemon lifts that pause and `adopted_held_tick` takes the slot back in its session, as after an operator resume (Escape, held delivery cancelled, message at the front; audit `held_watch.resumed_owned_pause`). Without proof nothing is re-sent (duplicate-execution protection holds).
  - **Pause ownership**: `hold(pause=True)` writes `hold_pause = {at, message_id, generation}` only when the run was not paused before. `hold_pause_lift` resumes only while `fx:service.at` still equals that `at` (nobody paused again since), through `pause_owner_resume`, so a tripped brake or a budget over the line refuses (`execution.auto_resume_refused`). An operator pause, a brake, a dialog pause (`prompt_pause`) or a handoff pause is never lifted here.
  - **Escalation (`held_escalate`, audit `held_watch.escalated`)**: used when nothing was recovered, or when the slot was recovered but the run stays paused by someone else. Repeated at most every `held_realert_s` (`held_alert_at`). A sub-fleet writes a `held-*.json` note (kind `held`) into its parent's `subfleet-notices`. The nearest parent handles it (`Fleet.held_notice`): it queues `[sub-fleet held] ...` to its supervisor (a notice with nothing to rule on) and, at a managed root, sends it to tg / parent through `lifecycle_close.notify` (`slot_held`). A root fleet queues `[<slot> report] HELD: ...` to its supervisor (task mode, not about the supervisor itself) and notifies the channels when managed. Escalations decided inside an intent (used-up supervisor retries, an adopted refusal) go to `held_notices` and are sent by the next tick.
  - **Delete confirmation dialogs (escalated only, never recovered)**: `prompt_wait` now stores the dialog class on the slot (`prompt_class`: `delete` / `other` / `unread`; cleared with `prompt_wait`). A user dialog of any class is only escalated, and the text says deleting is the user's alone. Before any recovery the daemon also reads the bottom of a held pane's screen with `watchdog.dialog_class`. If it is a delete dialog, it presses nothing (an Escape there would answer the dialog for the user), lifts no pause, and only escalates.
  - **Thresholds**: `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch` keys `held_after_s` (default 300) and `held_realert_s` (default 1800), in seconds and > 0, re-read at most every 30 s. One invalid key among `held_after_s`, `held_realert_s`, `subfleet_stuck_after_s` and `subfleet_check_s` resets all four to `runtime_v2.HELD_WATCH`; the audit is `held_watch.config_rejected`. `pi_adapter.daemon_watch` accepts these two keys but does not export them to the pi extension.
  - Verified by `tests/test_supervisor_hold_watch.py` (8 cases, including the no-event path of the incident: `test_frozen_adopted_supervisor_is_recovered_by_the_daemon_after_the_threshold`).
- **Supervisor regeneration (`supervisor_regen`; rule: the supervisor of a fleet must not stay held waiting for a human, the daemon replaces it)**: Cause: a root service fleet's pi supervisor got a network error on a followup notice (`fetch failed`, `Retry failed after 3 attempts`); the turn ended failed, the slot was held as "execution stop unconfirmed" and stayed held for days. The held watchdog could not prove the stop (`stop_proof` needs the Claude hook channel), escalated to the held supervisor itself (a root has nobody above it), `resume` answered accepted without touching the slot, and `operator` was refused. Now `RuntimeV2.held_watch_tick` calls `RuntimeV2.supervisor_regen` for a held `supervisor` slot that the existing recovery did not release:
  - **Scope**: the supervisor of a root fleet, and of a sub-fleet whose supervisor is not adopted (same code path). Never an adopted supervisor (section 19, `adopted_held_tick` unchanged), the advisor (section 27) or a worker (StopFailure retry, hold exit and backoff unchanged). Switch `daemon_watch.supervisor_regen` (default on); off = the previous behaviour.
  - **When**: held for `held_after_s` with no session progress, or at once when the turn ended on a recognized API / network error: a pi model-error candidate matching `runtime_v2.API_NETWORK_ERROR` (`OFFLINE_ERROR` plus `Retry failed after N attempts`, `Connection lost mid-response`, `API Error: Connection ...`; `OFFLINE_ERROR` itself is unchanged because `startup_offline` uses it) records `api_error` for that delivery and the failed `settled` hold sets `regen_due`; a Claude StopFailure whose same-session retries (`max_attempts`) are used up sets `regen_due` too. The existing recovery always comes first: `stop_proof` -> `api_error_retry` and its backoff. `regen_due` holds only while the session stays still: when its record moves after the hold, the held watch starts a new episode and clears `regen_due` (then `held_after_s` applies again).
  - **Ownership with the StopFailure path**: while a StopFailure backoff runs (`stopfail_retry_at` ahead), `stopfail_tick` owns the slot. When a supervisor's retries are used up and regeneration applies, `api_error_retry` queues the message once at the head (lead `REGEN_RESUME_SUPERVISOR`, no bus deadline), sets `stopfail_retry_at=0` (no timed release into the same session) and `regen_due`; there is no 600 s retry loop and no escalation at that point. The lease is idle, so the rotation has no in-flight message and adds none: the message is never queued twice. With regeneration off the 600 s loop is unchanged.
  - **Guards (escalate only, no count)**: not held; a handoff in progress (`handoff_id`); a section 35 link restart running (`link_lost` 1 / restarting; `capped` or `failed` is taken over by regeneration); a model refusal's fresh session (`fresh_pending` / `stopfail_fresh`, section 37); a StopFailure backoff still ahead; a dialog waiting for the user; a dialog on the held pane's screen (`watchdog.match_prompt`, or the delete class: delete dialogs are the user's alone; a screen that ends in the CLI's own idle input box shows no dialog, whatever words are above it; read once per tick before the existing recovery, and the delete class blocks that recovery too, as for any held slot); a tab that is gone (no proof the session stopped, section 36) or a pane outside this fleet's registry; the fleet stopping. The audit is `supervisor_regen.guarded` and the existing escalation runs.
  - **Order**: guards -> budget -> backoff -> screen guard -> connectivity probe -> lift only its own pause -> `Watchdog.rotate(reason='regen')`: the successor `supervisor-1.<N+1>` starts launch-held, `tab_into` the old tab, the old process tree is killed and the pane confirmed gone (the old screen is saved to `handoffs/` and recorded in history with `source: regen`), `confirm_retired` cancels the held delivery (`stop_confirmed`, result not applied), the message is disposed of (below), the launch gate opens, READY, idle. Kill-and-verify is the only stop confirmation; there is no "proven stopped, skip the kill" branch. A failed rotation is the existing `abort_rotation` (successor removed, slot held again, delivery still uncertain, never re-run); the attempt counts. A regenerated supervisor that misses its startup deadline is held again and regenerated within the budget (`supervisor_regen.not_ready`) instead of blocking the task or pausing a service run.
  - A regenerated or rotated supervisor also gets the section 60 restart state: a snapshot is written before the new generation starts, and at its READY the daemon reconciles and hands the open items to it.
  - **The unsettled message**: a notice (`followup`) or a task-mode supervisor message is queued once to the new generation with `[Previous generation did not finish] ...` in front and no bus deadline (`Watchdog.regen_requeue`). A request-bound `[Request <id>]` routing prompt: if the request is still open it is routed again by the new generation (the old routing decision was never applied, and routing executes nothing by itself); if it already has its public result (both incident hold paths publish `failed` / `timeout` when they hold), that result stands and the prompt is not sent again (`supervisor_regen.request_settled`). Nothing is dropped silently.
  - **Pauses**: only the pause this slot's own hold set (`hold_pause`, same `fx:service.at`) is lifted, through `pause_owner_resume`. An operator pause, a brake or budget line, a usage wall, a service BLOCKED, a dialog pause or a handoff pause is never lifted; regeneration then waits (`regen_state=guarded: paused`) and spends nothing.
  - **Connectivity probe** (`supervisor_regen_probe`, no new dependency): `auto` (default) = the CLI's declared route port (`session_env.route_closed`), plus the existing TCP probes (`Fleet.network_up`) when the last error text is a network error; `tcp` = both always; `off` = none. Routed proxies (the `session_env` routes) are probed; a proxy or base URL the CLI inherits from the daemon's own environment is not stored per slot and is not probed. Offline: wait `backoff[0]`, audit `supervisor_regen.waits_network` once, no count spent; a network wait that outlasts `held_after_s` is escalated like any held slot (paced by `held_realert_s`), so a probe that keeps failing never holds the supervisor silently.
  - **Budget**: at most `supervisor_regen_max` (3) new generations within `supervisor_regen_window_s` (3600 awake seconds), `supervisor_regen_backoff_s` ([60, 300, 900], the last repeats) between two. Its own log (`regen_log`), separate from the section 35 link-restart limit, `max_attempts` and the startup retry.
  - **Used up -> outward, never to the supervisor itself**: `held_escalate` for the supervisor: a sub-fleet writes its `held-*.json` note for the parent as before; a managed root sends `lifecycle_close.notify` (`slot_held`); a root with `channels` also pushes the existing default-on `daemon_down` event through `channels.actor.send_direct` to the entries that list it (section 53: the fleet's own control loop cannot run; an entry a managed run already reaches through `run_closed` is skipped); a root with neither writes a `no_channel` record to `<run>/notifications.jsonl`. Repeated at most every `held_realert_s`.
  - **`fleetmux resume`**: after the brake / pause part, `RuntimeV2.resume_held` answers for every held slot: `released` (a proven stop: a supervisor StopFailure settlement whose backoff is running ends that backoff now, or a `stop_proof` that the held watch settles on its next tick), `regenerating` (an unproven held supervisor: `regen_due` set, guards / budget / probe / backoff still apply), or `held` with `reason` and `next` (budget used up, a guard, regeneration off, a worker whose turn is not proven stopped or whose StopFailure backoff / exhausted hold is left exactly as it is: workers are never regenerated or killed). The answer is `rejected` only when the call changed nothing at all (no brake cleared, no pause lifted, nothing released or regenerating) and a slot stays held; the CLI then exits 2. An adopted supervisor is left to `adopted_held_tick`.
  - **Parameters** (`fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch`, read by `runtime_v2.regen_knobs`, hot within 30 s; each key validated on its own, a bad value keeps its default and is audited as `held_watch.config_rejected`; listed in `pi_adapter.HELD_KEYS`, never exported to the pi extension): `supervisor_regen` (bool, true), `supervisor_regen_max` (int >= 1, 3), `supervisor_regen_window_s` (seconds > 0, 3600), `supervisor_regen_backoff_s` (seconds > 0 or a non-empty list, [60, 300, 900]), `supervisor_regen_probe` (`auto` / `tcp` / `off`).
  - **Known gaps**: an egress the fleet does not know about (a tunnel set only in the CLI's own settings) passes the `auto` probe, so a regeneration may start while offline; the successor then misses READY and the cost is bounded by the budget. A session-record write that lands after the hold defers an API-error regeneration to `held_after_s` (a new episode). A supervisor tab gone without tmux evidence stays hold-only (section 36). pi workers still have no in-place hold exit; an interrupted pure request is requeued ("Held requeue" below), other holds are not. The successor's startup failing for a reason other than the network or the deadline (e.g. a refused startup reply) still takes the existing startup path.
  - Verified by `tests/test_supervisor_regen.py` (the pi incident without stop proof, a Claude supervisor ended by `API Error: Connection refused`, one negative test per guard, the probe, the budget and its outward report, rotation failure, the routing prompt, resume).
- **Held requeue of an interrupted request (`held_requeue`; rule: the daemon re-runs a pure request interrupted on a service worker; the fleet does not pause)**: Cause: a service worker's pure request was interrupted by an Escape; the slot was held as "execution stop unconfirmed" and the fleet paused, with no way to prove the stop. Now:
  - **Rule** (`held_requeue.eligible`). Collect a hold only when all of these are true: the switch is on; the fleet is in service mode; the slot is a worker; the hold cause is listed in `held_requeue_causes` (default `aborted`, `lease_expired`, `renew_failed`; `failed`, `unfinished` and `unconfirmed` are known causes, not listed); no tool was running when the turn ended (pi `unfinished`); the message is a worker message bound to a request dispatched to this slot; it is not a mid-turn or handoff message; the request does not say `replay: false`, is not an operator request and has attempts left (`max_attempts`); the slot holds no applet lease. Hold and report anything else as before.
  - **Hold** (`RuntimeV2.requeue_hold`, from `settle` (cause from `held_requeue.settle_cause`), the `expire` lease / delivery deadline (`lease_expired`) and `renew_tui` (`renew_failed`)). Hold only that slot. Publish no result. Do not pause the fleet. The request's own `timeout_s` still runs.
  - **Order** (`held_requeue.step`, first in the `held_watch_tick` slot loop). A hold exit wins (a released slot is left alone). Wait `held_requeue_after_s`. Guards: a dialog wait, a tab that is gone, a pane outside this fleet's registry, a tmux view tab, a delete dialog on screen. Budget: `held_requeue_max` new generations within `held_requeue_window_s`. While the run is paused, wait and spend nothing. Then `Watchdog.rotate(reason='requeue')`: start the successor launch-held, kill and verify the old session, `confirm_retired`, then `Watchdog.requeue_after_retire` routes the request again through `redeliverable` / `replay_request` (`replay` + 1). Publish `failed` for a request that can no longer be re-run. Keep the result of a request that already has one (for example its own timeout).
  - **Give up** (a guard, the budget used up, a failed rotation). Do today's path: publish `failed`, pause the run (the hold owns that pause), report `not_recovered` through `held_escalate`. Audit `held_requeue.gave_up`.
  - **Proof of stop**: only kill and verify counts. Killing a tmux view tab ends only its client, not the session, so never requeue a tmux view tab (`held_requeue.retire_provable`).
  - **`fleetmux resume`**: list a slot with a requeue mark under `regenerating` with `requeue: true`, and run it on the next tick.
  - **Audit**: `held_requeue.held` / `started` / `replayed` / `released` / `waits_paused` / `gave_up`. History records `source: requeue`.
  - **Parameters** (`fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch`, read by `held_requeue.knobs` through `RuntimeV2.held_knobs`, hot within 30 s; each key validated on its own, a bad value keeps its default and is audited as `held_watch.config_rejected`; listed in `pi_adapter.HELD_KEYS`, never exported to the pi extension): `held_requeue` (bool, true), `held_requeue_causes` (list, [aborted, lease_expired, renew_failed]), `held_requeue_after_s` (seconds >= 0, 30), `held_requeue_max` (int >= 1, 3), `held_requeue_window_s` (seconds > 0, 3600).
  - **Known gaps**: Claude, Codex, opencode and dsh report no per-turn tool record (the `claude-posttool` hook only hands over mid-turn prompts). For them only the declarations count: `replay`, operator, applet slot. A request field that declares side effects is not built. The pi bridge's `lease_expired` path is the section 35 link loss, not this hold. Transport v1 runs have no held requeue.
  - Verified by `tests/test_held_requeue.py` and `tests/test_pause_recovery_incident.py`.
- **Pause review (`pause_review`; rule: every pause has an exit; the daemon checks whether its cause still holds)**: Cause: a root fleet stayed paused for 5 days. Its queued job never started, because `job_tick` returns while the run is paused. Now `RuntimeV2.tick` calls `pause_review.tick` after the held watch, outside any intent:
  - **When**: after `pause_review_after_s` awake seconds of pause, then every `pause_review_every_s`.
  - **Cause**: `fx:service.cause` (section 11 "Pause cause"). A later pause of a liftable kind never replaces the cause of a run that is already paused, so the review judges the first cause. An empty cause stays empty and is never lifted. The kind table is `pause_review.LIFTABLE` / `pause_review.NEVER`, one definition, also read by `Fleet.pause`. Without it, use the brake record (`fx:brake.paused_by` with the same reason) or the slot pause marks (`hold_pause`, `prompt_pause` with the same `at`). Last fallback: the reason text is matched against fixed patterns to name a kind for the record (source `text`); a pause classified only from text is always kept. The reason text only names a kind for the record; never act on it, because a valid service decision rewrites `fx:service.reason`. runtime_v2 names a kind at each of its own pause calls: `integrity` (ambiguous or invalid intents), `supervisor_fault` (supervisor mid-turn limit), `opencode`, `slot_hold` (with `slot`, `message_id`, `generation`), `dialog`, `restart_recovery`, `startup` (startup timeout), `budget` (time budget), `adopted`; `channels/stop.pause_service` writes `security`. `RuntimeV2.finish(status, reason, kind=None, **ref)` passes a kind to the service pause.
  - **Never lifted**: `operator`, `supervisor_blocked`, `budget`, `integrity`, `opencode`, `security`, an unclassified pause, a delete dialog. Never lift past a brake or budget line.
  - **Lift when the cause is gone**: `slot_hold` (the slot is released, runs a new generation, or left the roster); `dialog` (the dialog is gone); `restart_recovery` (no slot held, no uncertain lease); `startup` (the slot said READY); `adopted` (the supervisor is not held); `subfleet` (the run is gone, not running, or not paused with nothing held); `supervisor_fault` of an earlier supervisor generation. Lift through `pause_owner_resume`. Keep a `slot_hold`, `dialog` or `restart_recovery` pause while another slot still holds its own pause reason: a dialog that waits for the user, or a hold that pauses the fleet on its own (`hold_pauses` is not `0`; `RuntimeV2.hold` writes it). A slot-only hold (section 43 expiry, a held requeue wait, a link loss) does not block. Name the blocking slot in the audit (ruling 30).
  - **Wall**: a safeguards stop wall (no usable model left on the chain) is never lifted. A wall record that carries `reset_at` is lifted after that time; one without it is lifted after `pause_review_wall_retry_s`. Check the budget first. Lift one wall record (same line on the same slot) at most 3 times, then keep it.
  - **Supervisor fault that still holds** (`supervisor_fault`, or `slot_hold` of a held supervisor): hold the supervisor without a pause, make this pause its `hold_pause`, set `regen_due`. Supervisor regeneration then lifts the pause through `hold_pause_lift` and rotates. Its guards, budget and probe apply. When they refuse, keep the pause.
  - **Record**: write `fx:service.review` (`pause_at`, `checked`, `at`, `kind`, `source`, `action`, `check`, `evidence`, `slot`). Audit `pause_review.lifted` / `kept` / `handed_to_regen` / `unknown` with `paused_at`, `paused_s`, `reason`, `kind`, `source`, `check`, `evidence`, `action`, `slot`. Audit a kept verdict once per change. `Fleet.resume` empties `review`.
  - **Parameters** (`fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch`, read by `pause_review.knobs` through `RuntimeV2.held_knobs`, same validation and audit; listed in `pi_adapter.HELD_KEYS`, never exported): `pause_review` (bool, true), `pause_review_after_s` (seconds > 0, 1800), `pause_review_every_s` (seconds > 0, 300), `pause_review_wall_retry_s` (seconds > 0, 3600).
  - **Known gaps**: a pause from `Fleet.finish` called without a kind (a startup failure before READY, a resume that could not close an old session, an adopted supervisor whose session is gone after a resume) has no cause and stays with the operator. Transport v1 runs have no pause review.
  - Verified by `tests/test_pause_review.py` and `tests/test_pause_recovery_incident.py`.
- **Parent watch of sub-fleets (the second line of defence)**: Cause: Its tick (`subfleet.tick`) only collects a sub-fleet whose daemon no longer holds its run lock; a hung daemon (lock held, no beat) or a live but stuck sub-fleet (supervisor held, run paused) was invisible to it. Now the parent daemon also judges every running sub-fleet itself (`subfleet.watch_children`, called at the end of `subfleet.tick`, outside any intent):
  - **Facts, read from the sub-fleet's own Redis, never through its daemon** (`child_progress`): the daemon heartbeat `fx:daemon:beat`, plus what `runtime_v2.daemon_outage` reports (last daemon event, unread events, held slots); the time of the newest audit row that is not bookkeeping (`WATCH_NOISE`: `renewed`, `lease.*`, `ui.*`, `watch.*`, `held_watch.*`, `agent.state`, `cli_check*`, `subfleet.watch*`; lease renewals and the Escape ladder keep the stream moving while nothing progresses); `fx:service` paused / at; the supervisor's slot state and lease; `fx:task` status. A Redis that cannot be read counts as a dead daemon after the launch.
  - **Not moving** (`stuck_reasons`; awake seconds, so a lid-closed sleep does not count) means any of: heartbeat older than `subfleet_stuck_after_s` (or never written that long after launch); paused that long; supervisor held that long (counted from the parent's first sight, `watch_held_since` in the sub-fleet record); or, while its task is `running`, no progress row that long.
  - **Report** (`report_stuck`): audit `subfleet.stuck` in the parent, and `[sub-fleet stuck] ...` queued to the parent's supervisor (reasons, the facts line, and what to look at). When the parent is the root (no `parent_config`) and managed, the same text also goes to the user through `lifecycle_close.notify` (`subfleet_stuck`: tg, or the run's `channels`). It repeats at most every `held_realert_s` per sub-fleet (`stuck_alert_at` in its record), and the mark is cleared once the sub-fleet moves again. A middle fleet tells only its own supervisor. When a sub-fleet's own held watchdog has already sent `[sub-fleet held]`, the parent supervisor may get both; each is paced on its own. A sub-fleet that carries `attention` (the supervisor answered BLOCKED, see the next item) is not queued to the supervisor again; the user channel still gets it at the same pace. When the sub-fleet moves again, `attention` is cleared with `stuck_alert_at`, and the next stuck episode goes to the supervisor again.
  - **Thresholds**: `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch` keys `subfleet_stuck_after_s` (default 600) and `subfleet_check_s` (default 60), read through `runtime_v2.held_watch_knobs` like the held watchdog keys; never exported to the pi extension.
  - Verified by `tests/test_supervisor_hold_parent.py` (5 cases, including a sub-fleet whose daemon stopped beating while it still holds its lock: `test_parent_reports_a_subfleet_whose_daemon_stopped`).
- **Supervisor BLOCKED on a sub-fleet notice (rule: a root never pauses because one sub-fleet waits for a human)**: Cause: a root supervisor answered a sub-fleet notice with BLOCKED and the whole root paused with no way out. Now (`fleetmux/blocked_scope.py`):
  - **Input**: the settlement passes the text of the message the reply is bound to as `about`, and on transport v2 its bus message id as `about_id` (v2: the delivery's `body.text` and `message_id`, `RuntimeV2.settle`; v1: the slot's `last_msg`, no id). A service decision routes only its bound message, so `about` names what the BLOCKED answers.
  - **Class** (`blocked_scope.classify`): drop every leading `[Previous generation did not finish]` note (section 24 "Supervisor regeneration" requeue). The fleet must be a root (no `parent_config`) and the switch on. Then the text must start with one of these program prefixes and resolve to a live record of this fleet; anything else is no match:
    - `[sub-fleet stuck] sub-fleet <id> (<run>): ` (`subfleet.report_stuck`) or `[sub-fleet held] sub-fleet <id> (<run>): ` (`Fleet.held_notice`): `<run>` is one of this fleet's own sub-fleet directories (`subfleet.children_dirs`).
    - `[sub-fleet report] sub-fleet <id> (<label>): ` (`Fleet.report_notice`): the open report record (`fx:escalations` `report:<task>:<id>`, status `escalated` or `awaiting_user`) is found by the bound message id (the record's `message_id`, following `stopfail_of` of an API-error requeue). A regeneration copy has a new id; then the record is the one whose own notice text (`report_notice(key, record)`) the message starts with, the longest such. A key written inside the request text never selects a record. The record's `origin_run` must be, or lie under (a grandchild's report), one of this fleet's own sub-fleet directories.
    - `[Sub-fleet <id> completed|failed] ` (`subfleet.collect`): the sub-fleet directory whose record has that task id and the notice's `(attempt N)`.
    - `[job <job id>] ` (job accepted / carried over): the job is in this fleet's `fx:jobs`; its newest sub-fleet, if it has one yet.
  - **Effect** (`blocked_scope.absorb`, called for a BLOCKED in `on_service_supervisor` and `on_supervisor`): the root is not paused (service) and its task does not end (task mode; the queued jobs of a task root do not fail). The rest of the decision is applied as before. The reason is never written to `fx:service.reason`.
    - stuck / held: an active sub-fleet (`subfleet.ACTIVE`) gets `attention = {reason, notice: stuck | held, notice_text (first 300 characters), by: "supervisor BLOCKED", label, at}` in its record (`subfleet.json`); audit `blocked.subfleet_marked`; one push when the mark is new. A sub-fleet that has already ended gets no mark; audit `blocked.subfleet_gone`.
    - report: see "Awaiting user" below.
    - completed / failed / job: nothing is marked (the sub-fleet has ended, a queued job has no sub-fleet yet, and a running one would lose a mark at the next watch); audit `blocked.subfleet_noted` / `blocked.job_noted` and one push. `status` has no row for them.
  - **Awaiting user** (report): the record goes from `escalated` to `awaiting_user` with `blocked_reason`, `blocked_at`, `blocked_by`, `child_run`, `pushed_at`, `push_status`. The own sub-fleet's record gets `attention_reports[<key>] = {reason, what, who, paths, task, by, label, at, answer}`. One line goes down the ruling path (`<origin run>/subfleet-vetoes`, kind `report_pending`); the origin sub-fleet's supervisor gets `[top ruling] <key> pending: ...` (`Fleet.report_pending`), and the report stays in its `fx:reports_up` with its task clock stopped. An `awaiting_user` record is never nudged, struck (`escalations_missed` counts only `escalated`), re-asked (`report_reask`) or approved by the program. It leaves that state only by a ruling (the user's `fleetmux answer`, or the root supervisor's later `escalations` answer, which is accepted for an `awaiting_user` key) or by a drop: `escalation_tick` drops it when its sub-fleet is no longer active (audit `report.awaiting_dropped`). A repeat BLOCKED on it changes nothing and pushes nothing. Audit `blocked.report_awaiting`.
  - **Re-sent request**: each report record carries `fingerprint` = the origin run directory, the worker (`who`), the sorted paths and the request text (`what`; whitespace and case folded, a leading `Re-sent, still open` marker dropped); `why` is not part of it. A report whose fingerprint equals an `awaiting_user` record is not queued to the supervisor (`report_intake`); its key joins that record's `aliases` (`resent`, `resent_at`; audit `report.resent_held`). A copy queued before its twin went to the user and then answered with BLOCKED joins the twin the same way. Any other text, worker, paths or run is a new request and goes to the supervisor as before.
  - **Push** (`blocked_scope.push`, event `subfleet_blocked`): a managed root uses `lifecycle_close.notify`; a root without a lifecycle uses `notify_route.unmanaged_send` (`RuntimeV2.channel_alarm`, else counted in `notify-undelivered.json`, shown by `status` as `human_notice`). A report push names the sub-fleet, the key, the request, the supervisor's reason and the `fleetmux answer` command. A completed / failed / job push names the notice, the supervisor's reason and that the root goes on; it does not point at `status`. Once only: a report on its transition to `awaiting_user`; a stuck / held mark when it is new; a noted notice by its fingerprint (`fx:attention:pushed`). An answer or a drop pushes nothing.
  - **Answer** (`fleetmux answer <key> approve|reject [--note TEXT] [--config PATH] [--wait S]`): `blocked_scope.answer_send` posts event `escalation_answer` (`by: user`, the caller's pid, the run's `run_id`; another run's id is refused as `foreign run`). The daemon (`Fleet.handle` -> `blocked_scope.user_answer`) applies it through `escalation_apply` -> `report_ruling`: one ruling file per key and alias (`by: user`, shown to the sub-fleet as `[top ruling] <key> <decision> (by the user)`), the record leaves `fx:escalations`, the sub-fleet's `attention_reports` entry goes (audit `subfleet.attention_cleared`), audit `report.user_answered`. Every closed or dropped key and alias is kept in `fx:escalations:closed` (`{decision, by, why, at}`, last 500). Refused with the reason: a key that is not `report:` shaped, a decision other than approve / reject, an unknown key (also another fleet's key), a closed or dropped key (with who closed it and why), a key still with the supervisor. Best effort, not an isolation boundary: the command refuses to run when the working directory, `$PWD` or `$CMUX_AGENT_LAUNCH_CWD` is inside the run directory (every agent of the run and of its sub-fleets works there). Transport v1 runs are refused.
  - **Every other BLOCKED is unchanged**: a request routing prompt, an operator instruction or result, a worker `[blocked]`, a `[dialog]`, contract and ruling notices, slot reports, advisor notes, corrections, and any of the notices above that does not resolve to this fleet's own sub-fleet, report record or job. Service mode pauses (`supervisor_blocked`; a stuck / held notice of an own sub-fleet with the switch off or at a fleet with a parent: `subfleet`); task mode ends the task as `blocked`. The root's own pauses (three invalid replies, budget, security, operator, the delete dialog) never pass through `absorb`.
  - **Not a root**: a fleet with a parent keeps the previous behaviour for every BLOCKED and never runs the re-send check or the push. Its pause or blocked end reaches its parent through the existing paths (parent watch, `[Sub-fleet ... failed]`, held notes), up to the root, which applies this rule. A root has no parent, so only the root needs it.
  - **Clear**: `subfleet.watch_children` drops `attention` when the sub-fleet moves again (audit `subfleet.attention_cleared`); it never touches `attention_reports`. While `attention` is set, `report_stuck` sends nothing to the supervisor (see "Parent watch of sub-fleets").
  - **Status**: `fleetmux status` of the root lists every marked active sub-fleet under a top-level `attention` (`[{subfleet, run, reason, notice, notice_text, by, label, at}]`, present only when not empty) plus one row per awaiting report (`{subfleet, run, notice: report, key, reason, what, who, paths, answer, ...}`) and prints one stderr line per entry (with the answer command for a report); the `subfleets` tree carries `attention` on the sub-fleet. `service.paused` stays 0.
  - **Switch**: `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch.subfleet_blocked_marks_child` (bool, default true; a bad value keeps the default and is audited as `held_watch.config_rejected`; listed in `pi_adapter.HELD_KEYS`, never exported to the pi extension). It covers the classification, the awaiting state, the re-send check and the push. Set false to pause the root (service) / end its task (task) for every BLOCKED as before. `fleetmux answer` stays available for records that already wait for the user.
  - **Known gaps**: a sub-fleet that re-sends a request in other words is a new request: it reaches the supervisor and, if answered BLOCKED again, is pushed once more (the root is not paused). Channel replies (`/approve`, `/reject`) are not wired to report keys; they serve delete dialogs only, so the user answers with `fleetmux answer`. `fleetmux answer` cannot tell the user from an agent of the same OS user that works outside the run directory; the audit keeps `by` and the caller's pid. An `awaiting_user` record lives in the run's Redis; a run that does not keep its Redis loses it with the other escalations.
  - Verified by `tests/test_subfleet_blocked_scope.py` (13 cases: the incident shape, jobs still start, a regenerated notice, other notices still pause, an ended sub-fleet, a fleet with a parent, the switch, status and clear, no repeat to the supervisor, task root v2, task root other notice, v1 transport, the parameter) and `tests/test_subfleet_blocked_scope_report.py` (27 cases: the report incident with a job starting, the key from the bound message, the message id and its requeue link, a regeneration copy cross-checked, one push for a report and for a stuck mark, re-sent same and other requests, no nudge or re-ask after a pause, the pending line down and its write, completed / failed with the attempt, a job notice, unresolvable notices still pause, the undelivered count, status, the mark survives, the drop, answer approve / reject, answer refusals, a supervisor ruling on an awaiting key, the CLI and help, the agent-pane refusal, the switch, a fleet with a parent, a task root).
- **No route for human notices (rule: a notice for a human that cannot leave the machine is reported, not dropped)**: Cause: a notice for a human that had no channel and no initiator was only written to `notifications.jsonl`, and nothing told anyone. Now (`fleetmux/notify_route.py`):
  - **Route check** (`notify_route.route_problem`): a root fleet (no `parent_config`) has a route when any of these holds: a `channels` entry whose push list (or the default push) carries `run_closed` or `daemon_down`; with a lifecycle, `$FLEETMUX_TG_CMD` or the mode's `notify_commands.tg`; with a lifecycle, an `initiator` while the mode notifies the parent session. A root without a lifecycle never calls `lifecycle_close.notify`; it reaches channels only through `RuntimeV2.channel_alarm`, so only an entry whose push carries `daemon_down` counts there. An injected `lifecycle_close.NOTIFIER` counts as a route. A fleet with a parent reports upwards and is never checked.
  - **Start**: `fleetmux start` and `start --resume` print one `warning: human notices have nowhere to go: ...` line to stderr. The daemon audits `notify.no_route` once when it starts.
  - **Count** (`notify_route.record_undelivered`): `<run>/notify-undelivered.json` holds `{count, last: {event, text, at}}`; `text` is the full text of the latest notice. Add one per notice, not per leg. Count a notice when every leg of `lifecycle_close.notify` ended `no_channel` or `no_initiator`. At a root without a lifecycle, count a held supervisor's `daemon_down` alarm (`RuntimeV2.held_escalate`) when `channel_alarm` returns `no_channel`. A re-sent stuck notice of a sub-fleet marked `attention` is counted the same way. A partly delivered notice is not counted. The file is written with a rename, so a reader never sees half of it.
  - **Root without a lifecycle** (`notify_route.unmanaged_send`): send a `[sub-fleet stuck]` (`subfleet.report_stuck`) or `[sub-fleet held]` (`Fleet.held_notice`) notice through `RuntimeV2.channel_alarm` (event `daemon_down`, the existing default-on push: the control loop of a fleet in this tree cannot run). Write one `notifications.jsonl` row (`channel: channels`, its status). Count the notice when the status is `no_channel`. Transport v1 has no `channel_alarm`: count the notice when the route check fails. A managed root keeps `lifecycle_close.notify` for these notices.
  - **Status**: `fleetmux status` (v2 and v1) shows `human_notice = {route: none | ok, problem, undelivered, last}` when the route check fails or the count is above 0, and prints one stderr line.
  - **Switch**: `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#daemon_watch.no_route_alarm` (bool, default true; a bad value keeps the default; listed in `pi_adapter.HELD_KEYS`, never exported to the pi extension). Off: no warning, no count, no `human_notice`, and a root without a lifecycle sends these notices to no channel; `notifications.jsonl` is written as before.
  - **Known gaps**: a channel that exists but fails to send (`failed`) is not counted here; `notifications.jsonl` keeps it. The count is never reset by the program; delete the file to reset it. No new dependency, no cron or launchd job.
  - Verified by `tests/test_notify_route.py` (22 cases).
- **Claude / Codex worker lease expiry holds only that slot (same incident; tui slots now try reclaim first and fall to this item only when reclaim fails, see "lease reclaim" below)**: the daemon renews official CLI slot leases itself (`renew_tui`), so "lease expired while the dispatch deadline has not passed" only shows the daemon did not run for a while (laptop lid closed, daemon stuck), not a worker error, just like a bridge slot whose lease was not renewed in §35 (`link_lost`, `pause=False`). A task-mode worker slot (not the supervisor or advisor; service mode unchanged) then gets `hold(pause=False)` (no public_status passed; the slot and the supervisor still get notice cards, otherwise nobody would see a hold that does not pause): the delivery is marked `uncertain` and the slot gets no more work; **duplicate-execution protection still holds** (no redelivery, the slot takes nothing new), other slots and the supervisor carry on; the hold exit is as before (a turn end of the same session → release, result not applied). If the dispatch deadline has also passed → hold + pause as before; the supervisor slot and a failed `renew_tui` renewal still pause; a service request timeout holds only the slot executing it since §43, no pause.
- **Lease reclaim: a lease expiry caused by the daemon's own absence holds no slot (default on)**: the previous item, holding only that slot, still stalls work: a lease that expires only because the daemon did not run (for example during sleep) held the slot, and the finished turn was then discarded by the hold exit. In service mode the same case also pauses the whole fleet. Now when an official CLI slot (`tui_slot`, including the supervisor; the advisor is excluded, its hold already restarts it in place) has an expired lease while the message's `deadline_at` has not passed, **it is reclaimed first**: `RuntimeV2.reclaim_tui` checks the lease is `received`, consumer = `tui-<slot>-g<current generation>` (the daemon is this slot's only consumer and renewer, nobody else can claim the PEL, so reclaim causes no second execution), and the pane is alive (`get_pane_status`, not-in-registry liveness check), then `MessageBus.reclaim` (`LUA_RECLAIM`: the same identity / consumer / `received` checks as `LUA_RENEW`, except it ignores `lease_until`; if `deadline_at` has passed it returns `deadline` and writes nothing) extends the lease to `min(now + lease, deadline)`, Lua audits `reclaimed`, Python audits `lease.reclaimed` (`gap_s` = seconds the lease had been expired). Reclaim writes Redis directly, not as an intent effect (it corrects the present; replaying intents should not carry it). Three hook-in points: in `expire()` before both hold branches; in `renew_tui`, when the lease has already expired, reclaim first and then renew (`LUA_RENEW` holds by itself on expiry, so it cannot wait for it to return expired); in `settle()` before the expiry check (for a Stop queued in the event stream during sleep, `commit_gate` reads the reclaimed lease at apply time). Reclaim impossible (pane gone, deadline also passed, identity mismatch) -> hold per the previous item. Switch: config `lease_reclaim: on|off` (`start --lease-reclaim`; given on `--resume` it changes, omitted it is kept; an old run without the key = on); `off` = the previous item's behavior. **Watchdog counterpart**: `RuntimeV2.tick` records `last_tick` (wall clock); a gap between two ticks >= `max(30 s, stall_window / 2)` (lease duration when `stall_window` is 0) is a `daemon.gap` (audited with `gap_s` and slot names): every in-flight slot gets `quiet_since=0`, `act_floor=now` (a slot waiting on a dialog gets no floor: it would read "activity after the dialog" as the human having answered), and `Fleet.last_activity` uses `act_floor` as a lower bound, so the first tick after waking does not count the slept time as worker stillness (otherwise `quiet` / `age` would both include the whole sleep and go stalled at once, or even hit the hard cap); after that a transcript still for a full `stall_window` goes stalled as usual. Verified by `tests/test_lease_reclaim.py`.
- **service mode (decision: option B, service works the same)**: in service mode `fx:escalations` holds only the two record kinds `dialog:` / `blocked:`; `parse_service_decision` accepts `escalations[]` (only those two key kinds, same shape as task mode), `on_service_supervisor` checks / applies them; the first non-answer is bounced without counting toward `bad_replies`, after that as in task mode (dialog goes to the user, blocked counts strikes). Non-delete dialogs: same as task mode (same verification, timeout `dialog_escalation_timeout`, fallback to the user); the `[dialog]` message is bound to the request id the worker is working on, kind `followup` (delivered even if the request ends midway). **blocked**: a service worker has no Blocked section; by convention its answer is exactly `{"blocked": "<reason + proposal>"}` (only when the request's own reply keys do not include `blocked`; `none` etc. do not count) → that delivery settles but **no result is published**, the request stays `dispatched`, `blocked:<slot>` is recorded (with the request), and the supervisor is queued `[blocked] … (request <id>)`; after the supervisor rules, the same request is sent to that worker again (`request_message` + `[Supervisor ruling]`), and the worker's next answer is published as usual. In service mode dispatching another request does not count as answering; if the request reaches a terminal state first (timeout etc.) → the record is void (audit `blocked.moot`) and a late ruling is only logged. A pi worker's `fleet_result` lets `{"blocked"}` past the local required-key check. Delete-class dialogs still go only to the user, zero key presses.
- **Known gaps **: if after the supervisor answers "approve" the TUI redraws before the next tick and the screen-bottom digest changes, it is treated as "a different dialog" and the supervisor is asked again; the `[dialog]` timer only starts when the supervisor receives the message, so while the supervisor stays busy the worker is bounded only by the lease `deadline_at`; the hold exit for pi slots is still not covered (plan (7)-4; meanwhile, while the supervisor slot is held the same run refuses new tasks and suggests starting a new run, which is only a stopgap; the exit itself is still to do); old instructions piled up in the FIFO while held / waiting on a dialog are delivered in order after release (plan (7)-3). API error redelivery and lease expiry: when the supervisor slot itself is held and then released, the supervisor gets no separate notice (its decision was not applied; there is only the UI card); support for the StopFailure group's asyncRewake is only inferred from how the hook executor in the Claude Code 2.1.280 binary is written (asyncRewake goes through the generic command-hook branch) and has not been verified by triggering StopFailure on a real machine; if unsupported, the redelivery lands in the mailbox with nobody to hand it over, falling back to the old watchdog stall path (stalled -> frozen rotation, no rerun). Stop deferred: two weaknesses of the process-tree CPU probe are not fixed: a `nohup ... &` process orphaned to pid 1 cannot be found by ppid, and taking positive per-tick increments of the total swallows other processes' increments in the tick a child exits; Claude slots now rely on the deterministic `turn_deferred` instead; pi / Codex slots have no Stop payload and are not covered; for a worker pane adopted as a sub-fleet supervisor, the hook returns before `delegated_route`, the event goes to the parent fleet (the slot is `delegated`, ignored by the watchdog), and the sub-fleet's watchdog never sees the marker; when a session is killed, `nohup` orphans keep running in the worktree, as is.
- **Tab gone (liveness check; decided after an incident; `RuntimeV2.tab_missing`)**: every 30 seconds, when a slot's tab is found missing → **it counts as "stopped" only when tmux proves the session has ended** (`cmux_manager.tmux_session_alive`: server answers and the session is absent / `no server running` / socket missing → False; a non-tmux tab or tmux unreadable → None, no evidence). ① A stopped worker (READY, not paused, not in handoff / clearing): follow §17 `start --resume`'s per-slot handling of dead windows: `clear_slot` releases lease and queue → `respawn` the next generation in place → task uses `requeue_lost`, service uses `replay_request` to replay the lost dispatch / request (counts `replay`; over `max_attempts` a task is judged blocked and service sends timeout; `replay: false` does not replay); **a Claude slot first resumes its own session** (the new generation's argv carries `claude --resume <original session>`; the transcript must exist); if the resumed generation times out without READY, or is lost again after resuming → a fresh generation + skeleton handoff note (if the working directory has a PROGRESS.md it goes into the next step); a fresh generation that never reached READY is only held when lost (prevents a restart loop); while paused it is only held and restarted at the next check after resume. ② Session still there (only the client tab closed) → `reattach_client` reopens a client tab alongside, inheriting the registry entry, no hold. ③ No evidence → hold only that slot (`hold(pause=False)`, cards still sent), **no fleet pause**; the supervisor (never in tmux, no evidence) is still held + paused. ④ A supervisor_slot that is delegating (§29) is never held (a hold would break the hook rerouting): stopped → `subfleet.delegated_lost` takes the existing reclaim (named `stop_child`, §19 records failed) → collect starts the next generation with a skeleton handoff note → autostart / engine brings it up again; requests the sub-fleet had not answered at that moment are written into the handoff note per §29 and redelivered by the caller (a sub-daemon that itself dies still goes through revive + `--resume`, replaying per §17 in the child's Redis); session still there → reattach the parent tab and sync `pane` in `subfleet.json`; a §19 placeholder tab gone → audit only. A supervisor adopted by a sub-fleet that stopped → hold only, no pause, wait for the parent fleet to reclaim it; session still there → reattach the view tab.
- **The wall gate comes before the watchdog**: after capturing, `Watchdog.check` first calls `Fleet.wall_gate` (section 23). A wall hit means no key press, no hold, no kill and no rotation for that slot in this tick (test `test_wall_on_screen_trips_the_brake_and_the_watchdog_presses_nothing`).
- Parameters: `--stall-timeout` (default 1200; under v2 the transcript-only hard cap), `--stall-window` (default 300; 0 = window rule off, only the hard cap), `--stall-cpu-pct` (default 5), `--stall-grace`, `--stall-escapes`, `--prompt-table`; invalid config (negative / non-numeric) is refused by `validate_config`. A named sub-fleet (section 29) entry `timeouts: {stall, stall_window, request}` can set `stall_window` (the entry's value; if absent the built-in default 300, not the parent's); `stall_cpu_pct` is not a time limit and is not in `timeouts`; a named sub-fleet otherwise follows the parent for it. An attached start (`fleetmux/join.py`) may forward `stall_timeout`, `stall_window`, `stall_cpu_pct`, `stall_grace` and `stall_escapes` to its sub-fleet as overrides. `walled` (wall wording) is not part of this item; it belongs to the brake (E).
- **Dialogs are judged by structure, not only by text**:
  - **Cause**: an agent's own report text naming a delete dialog was matched as an unknown dialog and paused the fleet, while the screen bottom was the normal input box.
  - **Root cause**: `match_prompt` joins the bottom `tail_lines` (25) lines into one lowercase text and does only substring matching against allowlist `match` / `any` and `unknown_markers`, without telling a dialog apart from text the agent itself wrote on the screen (report body, tool output, dialog text quoted in test fixtures). The allowlist has the same problem: an acceptance42 pi screen showed, in tool output, another machine's Claude trust dialog and matched `trust_folder`; the `own_cwd` check failed only because pi's status line `9.4%/400k` was read as the path `/400k`.
  - **New criterion** (`watchdog.input_box`, only for claude and pi slots; the CLI comes from `fleet.agent_cli`): when a Claude Code or pi dialog appears, the CLI's own input box is replaced. In pi's source `interactive-mode.js`, `showTopExtensionDialog` first does `editorContainer.clear()` and then places the dialog; none of the 14 saved real Claude dialog screens or the 2.1.316 trust-dialog recording has an input box. So while the screen bottom is still an input box, there is no dialog, and neither the allowlist nor the unknown markers count. A screen is recognized as an input box only when all of the following hold; if any one fails, text matching applies as before:
    - The last two horizontal rules are ≤ 12 lines apart; a rule may carry a label (e.g. `── f91472563-approve-1 ─`, `── ⠋ Working ──`);
    - Non-empty lines below the lower rule ≤ 8 (status lines);
    - From the upper rule to the end of the screen there are no dialog parts: numbered cursor `❯/›/>/→ 1.`, pi cursor `→ `, `Esc to cancel`, `Enter to confirm`, `esc cancel`, `↑↓`. A pi dialog is itself between two DynamicBorders, and this condition rules it out;
    - For Claude, the first line inside the box starts with `❯` (in 2.1.316, `❯` plus a no-break space).
  - **Unchanged**: codex, opencode, dsh and calls that pass no CLI still use text matching. `dialog_class`, `DELETE_MARKERS` and `delete_markers` still apply to screens already judged to be dialogs. The paths after a screen is judged a dialog (verification, to the supervisor, to the user) are not changed by a single line.
  - **Samples**: all 22 local saved screens, plus 1 recorded 2.1.316 trust dialog and 2 synthetic ones, are in `tests/fixtures/watchdog_screens/`, file names ending in `real` / `false`; test `tests/test_watchdog_screens.py`.
    - 14 real dialogs: 3 Dangerous rm from claude-hook (delete), 11 LSP plugin recommendation from fleetmux (other).
    - 8 false positives: 1 channels approve-1 (Claude); 2 acceptance42, 5 layout-finish (pi).
    - 2 synthetic: a pi confirm dialog (synthesized from the structure of `extension-selector.js`, not recorded); the body of this false-positive screen plus a real rm dialog.
    - After the fix: real dialogs are still recognized with unchanged classes, and none of the false positives is judged a dialog any more.
  - **Known gaps**:
    - There is no recording of the 2.1.316 Dangerous rm dialog (the step that triggers rm in an isolated session was not done). This version's criterion is inferred from the older version's saved screens and the 2.1.316 idle screen and trust dialog.
    - There is no recording of a real pi dialog, only synthetic samples.
    - codex, opencode and dsh have no saved screens and still use text matching, so the same kind of false positive can still occur with these three CLIs.
    - If a future CLI version keeps the input box below a dialog, this criterion will let the dialog pass. The err-on-the-strict-side guarantee rests only on the fact that "a dialog replaces the input box", so after a CLI upgrade, capture a dialog screen again and check.

## 25. Night switch: roster profile column + `night on|off`

Requirement: when switching to night mode, hot-reload the workers' tasks, unload the current workers, and continue from the checkpoint with the free tier of FlashNext. This section builds on §16 roster hot reload, §17 resume from a checkpoint and §12 profiles; it does not change slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the handoff flow or CLI boundaries.

- **Roster `profile` column**: `workers: [{name, role?, profile?}]`, where `profile` is a profile card id. At start / hot reload the daemon resolves the card (`profile_launch.resolve_launch`) and calls `runtime_v2.validate_config` on the slot's merged config (the combination-table entry point; it reads the pair table `fleet.yaml#cli_pairs`); an unknown card or a refused combination -> the whole roster is refused (section 16 semantics), not one rule relaxed.
- **Changing the model = the slot's next generation**: the slot name stays (renaming in the roster would be refused as "already used in this run", so switching back to day would be impossible). For a slot whose `profile` changed: after the in-flight turn finishes and the slot is idle (no in-flight delivery, idle confirmed), write a machine-skeleton handoff note the same way as a handoff, save the screen, start the next generation with `launch_held` (the new card's CLI/model), `tab_into` in place, close the old tab, v2 `confirm_retired`; the queue is not drained, and delivery resumes as usual once the new generation is READY. While the switch is pending the slot takes no new deliveries. A preflight or window-close failure → block the task per §12, no silent swap.
- **Checkpoint briefing**: once every slot in this switch is READY, the supervisor gets one `[Night switch <task>]` / `[Day switch <task>]`: each slot's new label and profile, outputs verified / missing, completed dispatches (the same `RuntimeV2.progress()` as the §17 briefing), queued instructions left on slots (stating plainly not to dispatch them again), and "dispatch only what is missing". With no running task it is only audited.
- **Trigger**: `start --roster day.yaml --night-roster night.yaml` (slot names in the two rosters must match); `fleetmux night on|off|status --config`: validates both rosters first, then atomically writes `<run>/night.json` (the /sleep trigger file). The daemon reads it every tick to decide which roster to follow. The night switch never swaps the supervisor.
- **Brake**: Redis `fx:night {on, brake_card: night|day, since}` plus audit `night.on` or `night.off`. The brake reads `brake_card` and uses the night cash cap (`caps.night_usd`, default 10 USD) while it is `night` (section 23).
- **Relation to live profiles**: while a task's `apply_profiles_live` is in effect, roster card swaps are deferred until that task ends (audit `roster.profile_deferred`), so the two sources do not flip back and forth.
- **v2 per-slot CLI**: places in the v2 transport that decide "official CLI window / Pi bridge" now read the slot's current-generation config (`runtime_cfg`); slots without a profile are exactly as before.
- **Changing CLI is allowed only for the night card swap **: `preflight_profile(launch, cli_change_ok=False)` under v2 still refuses "this slot's worker CLI differs from the run's"; only the roster card swap `swap_profile` and the wall chain switch (`wall_target`, section 23) pass `cli_change_ok=True`; every slot of a card swap has already passed the combination table in `night.resolve_launches` (`pair_refusal` + `validate_config`). Other paths such as task-level live profile switches still refuse a CLI change. Mixing Claude TUI slots and pi bridge slots in one run is not verified on real cmux.
- **Not verified**: only fake-cmux / real-Redis regression tests cover the switch. The pair `codex` supervisor + `pi` workers is verified in `fleet.yaml#cli_pairs` for task and service mode. A real day Opus -> night FlashNext -> morning switch back has not been run end to end.
- **Unification with section 22 rotation thresholds, section 12 model cards, section 23 brake **: there is only one "per model" mechanism: the night switch only changes the slot's `profile` (i.e. the `runtime_launch` override) and brings no threshold of its own; before every delivery section 22 `handoff_limits` re-resolves the rotation threshold from the slot's **current-generation running model** (roster worker row > roster `handoff:` table > config table > profile card `handoff_ratio` (section 12, layer 3b) > built-in / flag), so a slot switched to FlashNext automatically uses the FlashNext card's 0.5 and switching back to Opus automatically restores the 1M window. The `profile` column and section 22's `window` / `handoff_ratio` columns and `handoff:` table are validated in **the same roster by the same parser** (`roster.parse_entries` + `roster.handoff`); any invalid one refuses the whole roster; whichever roster the daemon follows (day / night, decided by `night.json`), that roster's `handoff:` table is used. New rows hot-added to the roster: rows with `profile` are counted by `night.resolve_launches` (including `model_usage.check_slots`), rows without by `default_capacity_problem` against the default model; one row is never counted twice. A pending wall chain switch (section 23) is voided for a slot whose card this section has swapped (audit `brake.downgrade_superseded`); the night cash cap reads this section's `fx:night.brake_card`.

## 26. Acceptance fixes

Acceptance report: an internal plan. This section fixes only the defects the report names; the single daemon, slot/generation identity, one-page layout, task/service protocol and the rest of §16-§22 are unchanged. Principle: things with side effects (writing files across workspaces) are done by trusted Python; models write only in their own directories.

- **P0 sub-fleet delivery path**: the sub-fleet's rules (CONTRACTS.md "path lives in the producer's own directory", the dispatch tail "write only in your own subdirectory") conflicted with "write to the parent path", Claude workers refused to write, and all 4 sub-fleets failed. Now: a parent output `<parent ws>/<slot>/<rest>` is mapped to `<child ws>/worker-1/<rest>` (outputs not under that slot's directory go into `worker-1/` by file name), the mapping is recorded in `deliver` of `subfleet.json`; the child goal writes only the child path and says "nobody writes outside this workspace; after COMPLETED the parent daemon copies"; a contract parent task's goal lists only interface ids + the parent `contracts.json` (to read the shape). `collect` copies when the outcome is `completed`, before any check: `worker-1/<rest>` first, otherwise the same relative path under another sub-fleet worker's directory (only if exactly one), skipping symlinks and empty files; nothing is copied unless completed; a copy error only records `subfleet.deliver_failed`, panes are reclaimed as usual, and "output missing" judges it failed. After copying, the "output missing" check and `escalation_finished`'s literal interface check run as before: **the parent interface's `path` is still the parent path, semantics unchanged**. Paths in the `[sub-fleet interfaces]` notice are now absolute (they used to be relative to the child workspace, which the parent supervisor would read as its own).
- **P1 resume cannot reattach Claude**: a natively installed Claude process is named `claude.exe`. `RESUME_CLIS` narrowed to `('codex', 'claude')`; `has_cli_process` compares the executable name from `ps -o comm=` with `.exe` stripped; `node` counts only when the script path in `ps -o args=` contains the CLI name (npm install); a bare shell or unrelated node process is not a live CLI (resume review P2-N1).
- **P1 READY check**: the startup reply passes when its last non-empty line (with markdown emphasis `*`, `_`, backticks and a trailing `.` stripped) equals `READY`; `NOT READY` does not count. Failing (and not failed / aborted / launch not ready) → first type one correction "reply exactly READY" into the pane, slot still `starting`; only a second wrong reply is blocked under the original rule. Corrections are recorded as `startup_nudged = startup_deadline`, once per READY request (start, adopt, reclaim, rotation); the typing happens after the transaction (like `rotation_pending`).
- **P1 sub-fleet attempt cap**: when the sub-fleet records of one task id (`sub/M0`, `sub/M0-attempt-<n>`, counting failed ones and ones rolled back half-started) reach `--max-attempts` (default 2) -> `subfleet.check` refuses (`max-attempts N: sub-fleet M0 already started N time(s)...`), covering escalate acceptance, approve, `spawn_child` and the operator's `subfleet spawn`. In a contract task an escalate refused this way -> the task row is `failed` (with the reason) and the supervisor is told it may redispatch differently or finish; the worker is not told "carry on yourself". Ordinary tasks follow the original refusal path (both worker and supervisor get the reason). The attempt cap does not apply to named sub-fleets (section 29) or to a job sub-fleet that resumes its run (section 58).
- **P1 choice dialog hang**: every Claude pane (workers, and sub-fleet supervisors that were worker panes) starts with `--disallowedTools AskUserQuestion`. Prevention only; the watchdog (section 24) detects and handles dialogs.
- **P2 `Interfaces:` line**: when the literal interface file check has passed but the report has no valid `Interfaces: IF-x@n provided: ready` line → the task row's reason names the missing line, the worker gets a hint filed under that task, and the supervisor gets a line "worker asked, no need to redispatch"; the worker's next report re-settles the task. No hint when the file itself fails the check (still not ready).

**Verification**: `python3 -m pytest -q tests` passes in full (new `tests/test_acceptance_fixes.py`: contract parent task → escalate → approve → sub-fleet writes only its own directory → parent copies, checks pass, interface ready, M0 done, parent supervisor receives completed (regression of the acceptance P0 path); another child worker directory's output is accepted, `worker-1` takes precedence; nothing copied unless completed; READY last-line check, pass after one correction, blocked on the second; the attempt cap judges failed at escalate, and `check` reports "already running" first while the task is still running; the `Interfaces:` hint then re-settles to done. `test_cmux_control` adds process checks for `claude.exe` / npm node / bare shell / unrelated node; `test_fleet` asserts argv carries `--disallowedTools AskUserQuestion`; the `test_v2_codex_claude` startup case now blocks only after two wrong replies).

**Known gaps (P2)**: after a sub-fleet is reclaimed the old child workspace still keeps a history tab (`close_all` only closes registered surfaces; why history was not closed under real cmux is not established and needs a real-machine repro); the new child workspace stealing focus, the child goal carrying the parent worker's full instruction verbatim (including the escalate instructions), `roster.changed` re-recording handoff after resume, and M1 no longer receiving `[contract] ready` after being judged done on a stub are report P2s left untouched; the delivery mapping always lands in `worker-1/`, so if the child supervisor gives the final draft to another worker and both places write a file of the same name, `worker-1` wins; not re-verified on real cmux / Claude sessions.

## 27. Advisor: picks model / effort / domain before dispatch, rules on relay or replacement mid-way

The single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the task/service protocol, the section 12 Phase 5 rotation mechanism and section 16-section 26 are all unchanged; the advisor is not a second dispatcher.

- **What it is**: a one-off consultation the daemon runs on behalf of the supervisor: `claude -p --model fable --effort high`, a stripped session (`--setting-sources ""`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--disable-slash-commands`, `autoMemoryEnabled:false`), cwd fixed at `<run>/advisor/`, 90 s timeout with 1 retry. Not resident, takes no slot, not in the layout. **(v2 task mode now uses a resident advisor tab instead, see the "Resident advisor" item below; this one-off call remains only for v1 and sub-fleets; a service main fleet is also resident.)** **It listens only to the supervisor**: input = the task brief + this dispatch instruction + the supervisor's optional `advise_note` + the slot's current state + that CLI's model cards and capability table + the domain list; no worker conversation. The advisor never talks to workers directly.
- **Switch = roster `advisor: on|off`, default off, hot-reloaded** (section 16, the run does not restart): the roster is the only entry point - the user editing the file, `fleetmux advisor on|off`, or the supervisor's decision key `advisor`; `start --advisor` / `start --resume --advisor` are just sugar that writes `advisor: on` into the roster; config.json does not store the switch. With a night roster (section 25) the switch is written to both day and night rosters, and `night.check_rosters` requires both to have the same `advisor` (the advisor is per run; the night switch does not toggle it). Kit validation follows the "go-live point": on a `set_advisor(on)` hot reload a broken kit refuses to turn it on and does not write the roster (audit `advisor.rejected`); at `start`, either `--advisor` or a roster already saying `on` is validated before any resource is opened; if the daemon starts with the advisor online and the kit is broken, the whole thing is refused.
- **Always consulted**: while the advisor is online, every dispatch passes through it before delivery (including a contract task held by `depends_on`, which is consulted again when released). Notices / corrections are not consulted. **When it is off nothing changes** (the full existing test suite passing with zero changes is the regression evidence). Turning it off only stops new consultations: generations already running or started from a config sheet are not rolled back; a slot held waiting for its first sheet is released with defaults; sheets still waiting for a ticket and not yet launched are dropped (`advice.wait_dropped`; queued messages are delivered by the runtime the slot is running).
