The late settled receipt commits normally. There is no `uncertain`, `settle.late`, `bridge_fault`, `execution.held` or `service.paused`. The supervisor takes its next delivery and the task completes.

**Known gaps**:
- Daemon throughput is unchanged: each round still takes only one event, and the watchdog's slot-by-slot screen reads are in the main loop, so with many busy slots event latency is still tens of seconds (settlements and the `指令已排队` (instruction queued) receipt of `operator_input` both arrive late; past 30 seconds `operator_input` still shows `输入未确认` (input unconfirmed), but the event is queued and will be processed). This is the root of the latency; separate work.
- A late receipt is picked up by `renew_loop`, at most one lease-renewal period later (`min(10 s, lease/3)`). The receipt stream the daemon writes (`<prefix>:reply:<correlation id>`) has no expiry, so a late `rejected` is never missed.
- On the dsh plugin's normal path the `completed` frame and the `settled` receipt each report idle once (the duplicate is harmless).
- §24 ⑦-4 (pi / bridge slots have no hold exit) is unchanged; this section only stops the wrongful hold.

## 48. Never type into a Claude input box; outside messages go through `operator`; a vanished confirmation dialog resumes automatically; a usage warning is not a wall

Decision: in no scenario does the fleet type into a Claude input box. Outside messages to the supervisor go through the `operator` command. A pause caused only by a confirmation dialog lifts automatically once the dialog is gone. This section does not change the section 1 division of labor, the single daemon, the private Redis or the task/service protocol; it does three things.

### 48.1 `operator`: outside messages to the supervisor bypass the input box

- `fleetmux operator --config <run>/config.json "<text>"` (no text or `-` reads stdin; `--wait` defaults to 30 seconds). The CLI reads the current `generation` / `thread` of `fx:agent:supervisor` and posts an `operator_input` event on the fleet's private Redis bus (the same event and the same `wait_reply` as the pi extension and the Claude `UserPromptSubmit` hook `claude_prompt`; the extra `source: cli` only goes into the audit), then prints the daemon's reply JSON: `accepted` (service carries `id`, task carries `task_id`) exits 0; `rejected` carries `reason` and exits 2; no reply in time is `pending` (with a correlation id; the event is already in the stream and the daemon will process it sooner or later, do not resend) and exits 3. Accepted text reaches the supervisor as a formal delivery per §5: pi through the bridge, a Claude supervisor through the Stop hook mailbox.
- The definite outcome per supervisor (the `operator_input` branch of `Fleet.handle`; the former single message "supervisor is not ready or this input belongs to an old session" is split into separate reasons, and the CLI makes the same decision locally first, `operator_channel_problem`): pi -> accepted; Claude (a directly started v2 supervisor with `claude_supervisor_hook_delivery`, or an adopted supervisor with `adopt.hook: true`) -> accepted, delivered through the hook mailbox; Codex -> rejected: "a Codex supervisor has no operator queue: `codex queue` only queues the daemon's own deliveries; type in the Codex supervisor pane yourself, or submit with `request` / `task -`" - this follows the existing design: no new channel is invented, and `fx:advice:notes` is not borrowed (that is only a note appended to the end of the next supervisor message, not a delivery); a Claude supervisor without a hook mailbox (old run) -> rejected with an explanation; wrong generation / session -> "belongs to an old supervisor session"; supervisor not ready -> reports its current state; v1 run -> rejected.

### 48.2 When the confirmation dialog is gone, a pause caused only by it lifts automatically

- This reuses the §4 handoff-timeout approach "whoever paused it lifts it" (`handoff_pause_arm` / `handoff_resume_tick`): `RuntimeV2.prompt_wait` (dialog handed to the user: pause dispatch) reads `fx:service` once before `pause()`; after the pause, `prompt_pause_arm` records `prompt_pause = {scope, at, generation, label}` on the slot. It records only when the fleet was not paused before (or the earlier pause was this very slot's own dialog, same `at`); otherwise nothing is recorded - someone else's pause is not its to lift.
- At the end of every tick (`handoff_resume_tick`), `prompt_pause_lift` runs for each marked slot: if `fx:service` is still paused and `at` has not changed (any `pause()` rewrites `at`: BLOCKED, a slot hold, the link-loss cap, a manual pause, another slot's dialog, a daemon restart recovery), the run still exists, the generation is unchanged, this slot's `prompt_wait` is cleared (the dialog was answered, rejected by the CLI itself, or the turn moved on - the watchdog clears it on transcript activity / end of delivery / scope change), no other slot is waiting for the user to answer a dialog, and the brake has not tripped / the budget is not exceeded -> `resume()`, audit `execution.auto_resumed` (`detail.source = prompt_wait`), and the slot and the supervisor each get a card `确认框已不在…已自动解除` (the confirmation dialog is gone ... lifted automatically). Blocked by the brake / budget -> `execution.auto_resume_refused`, left to a human. `at` changed or the run ended -> the mark is dropped and the pause stays. Dialog still there -> the mark stays until the next tick.
- The slot need not be idle: after the CLI rejects the command the turn is still running and the lease is still received; what resumes is dispatch to other slots. A dialog ruled on by the supervisor (`prompt_escalate`, route=supervisor) never pauses in the first place, so it is never marked. Delete-type dialogs remain "user only": the program never presses a delete dialog for the user; this only handles the pause after the dialog is already gone. The shared tail `pause_owner_resume` (brake check -> resume -> audit -> card) is factored out; the handoff-timeout path now calls it too, with unchanged behavior.

### 48.3 Inventory: every path that could type into a Claude pane

There is only one typing primitive at the bottom, `send_prompt_to_idle` (three backends; a Claude pane goes to `claude_input.send`). Handling is in three layers: ① configuration layer - a configuration that would need typing is refused outright at `start` / `start --resume` / daemon / actor / roster load (`runtime_v2.typed_claude_problem`, the reason quotes the rule); ② call sites - the Claude branch never calls the typing primitive, it holds the slot / marks it blocked and writes the reason; ③ last line - `claude_input.send` refuses unconditionally (no paste, no keys), so no backend can type into a pane registered as Claude.

| Path (file:line before the change) | When it fires | Before | Handling |
|---|---|---|---|
| `runtime_v2.type_into` 2572 | v2 delivery to a Claude slot without a hook mailbox (worker `claude_hook_delivery: false` / §28; the Claude supervisor of an old run; an adopted supervisor without `adopt.hook`) | typed | refused at the configuration layer; at the call site Claude is not typed into, the slot is held with the reason |
| `RuntimeV2.step` startup nudge 688 | a non-hook Claude slot not READY after start | typed | same as above (slot held, "startup nudge not sent") |
| `RuntimeV2.clear_tick` 1620 (§28 `/clear`) | `clear_after_result` after every result | typed `/clear` | `--clear-after-result` refused at start (a hook has no way to trigger `/clear`; for a clean context use §37 `"fresh": true`); the call site no longer types, it holds the slot |
| `Fleet.send` 6222 (v1) | a v1 run sends a pointer to a non-hook Claude | typed | refused at the configuration layer (v1 Claude supervisor, `claude_hook_delivery: false`); the call site refuses to send and records `send.refused` |
| `subfleet.adopt` 1010 | a sub-fleet adopts a Claude pane as supervisor with `adopt.hook` false (parent run older than `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery`) | typed the role change | the parent fleet's `subfleet.check` refuses to open that sub-fleet; the child config validation refuses too; the call site marks blocked without typing |
| `subfleet.collect` 1281 | sub-fleet reclaimed, role-change notice to a non-hook Claude worker | typed | Claude always takes the hook branch (the next generation starts with CLI arguments, the original §19 R1-01 approach) |
| `Fleet.midturn_type` 4967 (§34) | mid-turn rotation prompt | Claude already used the PostToolUse hook (`claude_posttool`), no typing | unchanged (the bracketed argument for Claude is removed; Claude never gets here) |
| start / rotation / handoff / fresh first prompt | every Claude session start | CLI argument (`prompt_file` -> `"$(<file)"`) | unchanged |
| READY correction, three-strike correction, handoff delivery, sub-fleet end notice, advisor note, peer notice | while running | all via Redis delivery -> hook mailbox (same as `type_into`) | unchanged; the non-hook case is the same as the first row |
| operator messages | someone outside wants to talk to the supervisor | only `cmux send` typing into the pane | new `operator` subcommand (48.1) |
| `--no-claude-hook-delivery` / `--clear-after-result`, roster `subfleets[].claude_hook_delivery: false` / `clear_after_result: true` | user configuration | switches for the typing channel | flags kept but refused at start with the reason; the whole roster is rejected |
| `lifecycle_close` | closing a workspace | closes panes only, sends no text | unchanged |
| `backends/cmux.py:_type_command` | when cmux opens a pane it pastes the start command into the **login shell** | a shell, not a Claude input box | out of scope, kept |
| Control keys: watchdog `KEYS` (Enter / Escape / arrow keys / `y` `n` `1`-`3`, pressed only when a confirmation dialog is recognized), the various `Escape` turn aborts (`fleet.py` 4469 / 6843, `runtime_v2` 2232 / 2890, `subfleet` 1267, watchdog 737) | answering a dialog / aborting | key presses, not typing text into the input box | left as is, as required |
| Test tools `scripts/smoke_claude_input.sh`, `scripts/smoke_pair.sh` check 4 | smoke / acceptance fixtures simulating a human typing | via `send_prompt_to_idle` | now refused on a Claude pane (broken; to be made manual or switched to testing `operator`); not a fleet runtime path |

pi / Codex / opencode / dsh panes are outside this section (Codex uses `codex queue`; pi / opencode / dsh use the bridge).

**Verification**: `tests/test_no_claude_typing.py` (real private Redis + real daemon step/pump/tick + fake panes) - (1) operator: a pi supervisor accepts and queues with zero pane traffic; a Claude hook supervisor accepts and delivers through the Stop hook mailbox with zero typing; a Codex supervisor is refused both locally and by the daemon with a reason; an old generation gets its own reason; the CLI exits 2 on rejection and exits 0 on accepted stdin input. (2) dialog gone: sole cause -> automatic resume, one audit and one card each, other slots dispatched as usual; afterwards a manual pause / another slot's hold / another slot's dialog / a daemon recovery -> no resume, mark dropped; already paused before -> no mark; brake tripped -> `auto_resume_refused`; another slot still waiting for the user to answer a dialog -> waits until that one is gone too; dialogs ruled by the supervisor are not marked. (3) no typing: all seven typing configurations are refused by `validate_config` (the reason cites section 48), the five hook-channel ones pass; the two retired `start` flags are refused before provisioning; the two roster keys reject the whole roster; `claude_input.send` neither pastes nor presses keys; `type_into` / startup nudge / `clear_tick` / v1 `Fleet.send` hold the slot or refuse on a Claude slot without a hook, `mgr.sent` stays empty; all three backends refuse a Claude pane (`test_claude_editor_send.py::test_claude_pane_is_never_typed_into`); an old parent run may not hand a Claude pane to a sub-fleet (`test_subfleet.py::test_adopted_supervisor_handover_without_a_hook_channel_is_refused`).
Old tests changed as a consequence: cases that only verify the typing channel itself are marked `RETIRED` (skipped, the reason points to this section) - `test_claude_editor_send.py` (87 paired-backend paste transactions), `test_claude_editor_runtime.py` / `test_claude_editor_review_repro.py` (old typed runs), `test_clear_after_result*.py` (§28 behavior), `test_claude_supervisor_hook_edges.py` (old v2 run kept typed), 11 cases and 1 parameter for typed adoption / typed reclaim in `test_subfleet.py`, 3 in `test_subfleet_named_tmux.py` and 2 in `test_subfleet_tmux.py` (all use typed same-session reclaim); `test_acceptance_fixes.py` / `test_escalate.py` / `test_long_split.py` / `test_subfleet_named_tmux.py::adopt_view_child` (borrowed by `test_liveness_selfheal.py`) now receive role changes through the real hook; the `tree` / `svc` fixtures of `test_subfleet.py` now pin `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery` (a new start pins it anyway), and `adopt_child` waits for the role change through the real hook, so most sub-fleet cases now run on the hook channel.

**Known gaps**:
- Two slots raise dialogs one after the other: the second `pause()` rewrites `at`, the first mark becomes void, and the second is not marked because the fleet was already paused; even after both dialogs are gone a manual `resume` is needed. This is the literal, conservative reading of "if there is another pause source, stay paused".
- Upgrading old runs: Claude supervisor runs started before this version (no `claude_supervisor_hook_delivery`), v1 Claude supervisor runs, runs with `--no-claude-hook-delivery` / `--clear-after-result`, and parent runs older than `adopted_supervisor_hook_delivery` that adopt a Claude pane - on this code `start --resume` and daemon start both refuse with an explanation; start a new run.
- §28 clear-after-every-result is disabled: no hook can trigger `/clear`. To save tokens use §37 `"fresh": true` instead (the cost is one cold start and generation +1 per item); "automatically fresh after every result" would be separate work (mark the slot `fresh_pending` when the result is published). The §28 text and code are kept as history and are unreachable through configuration.
- A Codex supervisor still has no programmatic operator entry (only typing in its own pane, or `request` / `task -`).
- The smoke / acceptance scripts in the table above now fail where they simulate a human typing into a Claude pane; not changed.
- Some of the retired typed sub-fleet cases test mechanisms unrelated to the channel (autostart crash loop, roster hot-reload removal, drain timeout, parent resume reclaiming an external sub-fleet, same-session reclaim, tmux two-workspace display and crash reclaim); they all used typed reclaim before. On the hook channel reclaim means "retire the session, start the next generation", so they need to be ported separately.
- This section had no isolated smoke test on the real Claude / pi / Codex TUIs; the results for the three supervisor kinds come from integration tests with real Redis + real daemon + real hook entry points.

### 48.4 A usage warning is not a wall

Observed: in the channels dev fleet the whole fleet was braked to a stop twice within 40 minutes, audit `brake.tripped` "model_pool wall ... no lower tier on this", while the screen showed Claude Code 2.1.316's reminder line "You've used 78% of your weekly limit - resets 3am (America/Toronto) - /usage-credits to keep using..." (the same at 84%). Root cause: the `model_pool` entry of the wall table had a bare `/usage-credits`, which also appears in the reminder line, so it was taken as a pool wall; Opus has no lower tier -> the brake paused.

- The wall table gains `notices` (`fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml#walls`): `You've used <N>% of your` and `/usage-credits to keep using` (the second half when a narrow pane wraps the reminder onto two lines). `brake.classify_wall` first drops lines that match `notices`, then checks account level -> pool level in order; so a reminder line is neither an account wall nor a pool wall.
- The bare `/usage-credits` in `model_pool` is replaced by "Run /usage-credits to continue". Account level matches "You've hit / reached your weekly / session / usage ... limit", "... limit reached", "Claude usage limit reached", "usage limit has been reached" (Codex / pi) and "You have hit your ChatGPT usage limit" (pi). Pool level matches "You've hit / reached your <model name> ... limit" for any model name that is not usage / weekly / session / daily / monthly / 5-hour, and "Set up usage credits".
- Tests: `tests/test_brake.py::test_percent_usage_reminder_is_not_a_wall` (the original 78% / 84% texts, with the U+23BF result-glyph prefix, wrapped, `screen_wall`; red on the old code: classified as model_pool) and `test_spent_pool_and_account_limits_still_trip_next_to_a_usage_reminder` (a reminder line followed by a real wall still classifies as model_pool / account).
- Known gap: a private fleet.yaml (`FLEETMUX_CONFIG`) that overrides the whole `walls` block must add `notices` itself and drop the bare `/usage-credits`.

## 49. Blocked manual input goes back into the input box; pressing Enter again does not register it twice

- **Problem**: Claude Code puts a line blocked by the `UserPromptSubmit` hook back into the input box. Pressing Enter again fires the hook again. Without a check each Enter registers the same text as a new `operator_input`.
- **Mechanism (measured on the real TUI, Claude Code 2.1.316)**: the line for which the hook returns `decision: block` is put back into the input box as is; pressing Enter again fires the hook again. The fleet used to register it as a new instruction each time.
- **Approach**: after `claude_prompt` registers successfully (`accepted`, or the event is in the stream but the reply has not arrived), it writes the sha256 of the line with leading/trailing whitespace stripped, the generation, the session and the registration id into `<prefix>:hook:<slot>:operator_last`. Submitting the same line again in the same generation and session -> not registered, audit `hook.prompt_resubmit`, reply `这段文字已登记过（<id>），没有再排队` + `按 Ctrl+C 清掉即可` (already registered (ID), not queued again; the box holds the restored text, clear it with Ctrl+C; to really send it again, change it first). Only the "previous one" is compared: if other text was submitted in between (the box content changed), typing the same sentence again registers as usual. A rejected (`rejected`) line is not recorded; resending after the state is fixed is handled as usual.
- **Scope**: only the manual input entry of a Claude supervisor; outside messages bypass the input box through §48 `operator`. The pi supervisor's input box is outside this section.
- **Test**: `tests/test_claude_supervisor_hook.py::test_restored_operator_line_resubmitted_is_not_queued_twice` (real private Redis + real daemon; red on the old code: the second Enter queued again).

## 50. One command name: `fleetmux`

This section is the design record of the change.

- **One entry point**: the only command is `fleetmux`. The Rust binary itself handles `--version`, `--help` / `-h`, `server`, `attach`, `reap`, `web`, `gui --runtime D`; every other subcommand (everything in `fleet.py` `cli()`: start, task, request, status, stop, resume, pause, results, logs, operator, channel, subfleet, advisor, night, agents, plan, peer, chrome, gui without `--runtime` ...) is `exec`ed as is by `rust/crates/app/src/forward.rs` to the Python command line of the same release: `<release>/.venv/bin/python <release>/fleetmux/fleet.py <args>`. exec replaces the process, so argv, stdin / stdout / stderr and the exit code are Python's own. Rust does not know the fleet subcommand table; a new Python subcommand needs no Rust change.
- **Order for finding Python** (first match wins): (1) `$FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI` (an executable, run as is, e.g. `<release>/.venv/bin/fleetmux-py`; set but not executable is an error, no further search) -> (2) the release containing this binary (two levels above `<release>/bin/fleetmux`, tried once via the invoked path and once via the real path after resolving links) -> (3) `~/fleetmux-current` -> (4) an executable `fleetmux-py` in an absolute `PATH` directory (the console script of the Python package). A directory in (2) or (3) counts if it has an executable `.venv/bin/python` and `fleetmux/fleet.py`. None found: stderr lists the places checked, and the hint names three fixes: install the Python package of this version and run `fleetmux setup --install`, set `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI`, or install a release with `install_release.sh --activate`. Exit code 127; a failing exec itself exits 126.
- **`gui` does not loop**: Rust only checks whether the word `--runtime` is among the arguments: if so it opens the window itself, otherwise it forwards to Python. Python's `gui` (scans resumable runs, writes grey cards) finally only execs `fleetmux gui --runtime <this HOME's runtime>`, a form that Rust handles itself and never forwards again (pinned by `tests/test_fleetmux_command.py`, `crates/app/tests/forward_cli.rs`). `fleetmux gui --devtools` without runtime -> forwarded to Python, argparse reports an unknown argument, exit 2.
- **Other behavior**: the `fleetmux --version` output format is `fleetmux <version> (protocol <n>)`; `deps.yaml`, `check_deps.py` and `backends/fleetmux.py` rely on it. No arguments runs `fleetmux gui` (next item). `--help` prints two lines to stdout and exits 0; the second line says to use `fleetmux <subcommand> --help` for fleet subcommands.
- **Bare `fleetmux`**: the command with no arguments shows this machine's root fleet window. Rust forwards it as
  `fleetmux gui` (the Python CLI receives the single argument `gui`), so bare `fleetmux` and `fleetmux gui` without
  `--runtime` behave the same. `fleet.gui()` first calls `fleetmux/open_window.py::open_root_window`, the only code
  that finds the root and opens its window for a person at a terminal:
  1. Root: `join.find_root(HOME)` (a service root first; `~/.fleetmux`, every `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/` and this
     HOME are searched, so a join stub in `~/.fleetmux/latest.json` does not hide it). Its runtime directory is
     `manager_for(<root config.json>).runtime_root`. The root is used when this HOME runs no fleet of its own
     (`gui_live_run()` is None) or runs the root itself (a shell whose `FLEETMUX_HOME` is the root's home, such as
     the root's own panes); a HOME that runs another fleet (a sub-fleet, a `--no-join` fleet) keeps the old path.
  2. No desktop, no `fleetmux-web`, or an ssh session (`fleet.window_here`, the rule `auto_view` uses): exec
     `fleetmux attach --runtime <runtime>`, also when a window is open (the root daemon keeps one open on the
     desktop, which a person over ssh does not see). This runs before `gui_problem()`, which would refuse without a
     desktop.
     The same attach runs in a host terminal, a terminal that is itself a pane of another program (such as a Herdr
     terminal), so the root fleet's view is drawn in that terminal: `open_window.in_host_terminal` holds when one of
     the variables in `bare.host_terminal_env` is set and non-empty (shipped: `HC_TERMINAL_ID`, which a Herdr
     terminal pane has and a command Herdr runs without a terminal does not), stdin and stdout are TTYs
     (`interactive_terminal`), and no ancestor is a `fleetmux server` (`inside_fleetmux_pane`; panes inherit the
     variable from a fleet started in a host terminal, and inside a pane the window path is kept). It attaches also
     when a window is open. Without a terminal (a pipe, a script) the window path of items 3-4 is kept. The list is
     read and validated only when a window could open here (`window_here` holds), before any raise, spawn or exec,
     and in item 5 before the start; over ssh or without a desktop it is not read, so even a bad value leaves that
     attach path as it was.
     `fleet.window_here` is not changed: it is shared with `start`'s automatic view (section 33.6), which keeps
     opening the window in a host terminal.
  3. Window already open: `gui_keep.gui_running(runtime)` returns the pid of `fleetmux gui --runtime <runtime>`
     (an `attach` does not count). On macOS `bring_window_to_front(pid)` raises its `fleetmux-web`. No new window and
     no server start; one line (it says "brought it to the front" only when the raise worked); exit 0.
  4. No window: `gui_keep.spawn` starts `fleetmux gui --runtime <runtime>` in a new session (stdin from /dev/null,
     output to `<run>/gui.log`), macOS raises it, the command prints `Opened a window (log: <run>/gui.log)` and exits
     0 at once. Unlike `start`'s automatic view (section 33.6) this also happens inside a fleetmux pane and without
     a terminal: the person typed the command to get the window.
  5. No live root fleet and no fleet running in this HOME (`gui_live_run()` is None): start the default root
     fleet from the `bare` block of `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml` (`open_window.start_bare_root`). The block is a
     user block: `~/.fleetmux/fleet.yaml` replaces it as a whole, missing keys take the built-in defaults
     (`fleet_config.BARE`), a bad value is refused before anything starts (`fleet_config.bare()`). Defaults:
     `enabled: true`, `name: main`, `lifecycle: service`, `home: ~/.fleetmux/fleets/{name}`, `roster` / `workers`
     null, `start_args: []`, `window_wait_s: 30`, `window_poll_s: 0.5`, `host_terminal_env: [HC_TERMINAL_ID]`
     (item 2; a list of environment variable names, `[]` turns it off, anything else, `null` included, is
     refused; a private block without the key takes the shipped list). No model key: `start` takes the
     `discovery` default pair; `start_args` may not carry the CLI / model flags, the flags bare sets itself, or
     `--no-view` / `--gui` / `--attach`.
     - One line names the fleet, its lifecycle and its home. Then `fleet.run_start` runs this release's
       `start --lifecycle <lifecycle> --name <name> --resume auto --no-join [--roster R | --workers N]
       <start_args>` in a child with `FLEETMUX_HOME=<home>`, `FLEETMUX_STATE=<home>/state` and stdin from
       /dev/null; stdout / stderr are the caller's. A non-zero exit is bare's exit code (no history fallback).
     - `--resume auto` (the semantics of `start --resume auto`): a resumable earlier run named by `<home>/latest.json`
       is resumed, otherwise a new run starts. A run stopped with `fleetmux stop` has `result.json` and is not
       resumable, so a new run starts in the same home. Exception: a service root stopped while it holds open jobs is continued (section 58 "Restart"). No other fleet home is read or written. `--no-join`
       keeps the new fleet a root even if another root appeared meanwhile.
     - The window: stdin from /dev/null keeps `start`'s automatic view off in the child, whatever terminal bare
       has. Bare takes the root from `<home>/latest.json` (right after `start` its daemon may not hold its lock or
       answer yet, so `join.find_root` is not asked) and runs the shared step of items 2-4 (`open_window.show`)
       with a wait: no window here or a host terminal (item 2) -> exec `attach --runtime` (over ssh or in a host
       terminal the fleet is started, then attached, no wait; the root daemon still keeps its desktop window,
       `gui.keep_open`). Otherwise the window comes from the new root daemon's `gui_keep`: bare looks for
       `fleetmux gui --runtime <runtime>` (`gui_keep.gui_running`) at once and then every `window_poll_s`, at most
       `window_wait_s` in total, and raises it on macOS. The daemon's first check runs only after `start_all` (a new
       run) or a resume's reconcile and replay, so the wait can run out on a healthy start: then bare falls back
       once to items 3-4 (an open window is raised, else `fleetmux gui --runtime` starts in the background and is
       raised) and returns; the daemon's next check then finds that window. The wait is bounded by
       `window_wait_s`, so the command never hangs. With `gui.keep_open: false` no daemon opens the window, so
       there is no wait. The shipped block waives no safety check: when the default pair runs pi, `start` refuses
       without `--allow-unsandboxed-pi`, which only the operator's own block may add.
     - `bare.enabled: false`: the history window of this HOME as before (grey cards), or one line and exit 1 with
       no history. A HOME that runs a fleet that is not the root also keeps that path. No usage error.

  `fleetmux --help` / `-h`, `--version`, `server`, `attach`, `reap`, `web` and `gui --runtime D` are unchanged. Tests:
  `tests/test_open_window.py` (root lookup and runtime, join-stub HOME, raise without a second window, background
  start and log, inside a pane, attach without a desktop / over ssh / without `fleetmux-web` also with a window
  open, history fallback with bare off and the bare start with bare on,
  exit 1 without history, a HOME running another fleet, a HOME running the root, one implementation),
  `tests/test_open_window_bare.py` (no root -> the bare start argv and environment, the line before it, the
  daemon's window waited for and raised, no terminal and inside a pane, never `--no-view`, a HOME running another
  fleet, a failed start, `gui.keep_open: false` (no wait), the wait from fleet.yaml, the wait running out and the
  one fallback (opening, or raising a window that appeared), the shipped and private `bare` block, no sandbox waiver,
  refused values, no model flags, `enabled: false`, `--resume auto` in the bare home only, a resumed run, ssh /
  no desktop -> start then attach, a live root starts nothing, `fleet.run_start` and the conftest guard),
  `tests/test_host_terminal.py` (host terminal with a terminal -> attach, also with a window open; no terminal, no
  listed variable, `HC_SCOPE` alone, an empty value, `[]`, inside a pane -> the window path; ssh unchanged; shipped,
  private and default list; bad values refused on every path before anything is shown),
  `tests/test_host_terminal_bare.py` (no root in a host terminal -> start then attach without a wait; no terminal
  or `[]` -> the wait as before; a bad list refused before any start),
  `tests/test_host_terminal_auto_view.py` (`start`'s automatic view unchanged in a host terminal),
  `tests/test_gui_keep.py` (`gui_running` returns the pid), `rust/crates/app/tests/forward_cli.rs`
  (`no_arguments_forwards_gui_to_the_python_cli`, `dash_h_still_prints_usage_and_exits_0`).
- **The Python CLI's own entry point is `fleetmux-py`**: `pyproject.toml` installs the console script `fleetmux-py` (`fleetmux.fleet:main`) into `<release>/.venv/bin/`, for launch scripts and `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI`; `python -m fleetmux.fleet` works as well. In a release it is not called `fleetmux`: that name belongs to the Rust binary in `<release>/bin/`, and whichever came first on `PATH` would win. A platform wheel also installs a `fleetmux` console script ("Platform wheels" below). There are no aliases for the pre-fleetmux command, environment variables or release paths; only `fleetmux home migrate` (section 50.1) knows the old names.
- **Environment variable names**: every fleetmux variable is `FLEETMUX_*`, read by that name only (exception: `CMUX_BIN`, the path of the cmux binary for the cmux terminal backend, keeps its name; the run config pins it as `cmux_bin`) (four were renamed rather than prefixed: `FLEETMUX_BIN`, `FLEETMUX_WEB_BIN`, `FLEETMUX_REAL_BIN`, `FLEETMUX_THEME`). Programs that do not import the package read the same names: `scripts/install_release.sh` (`_PYTHON`, `_RELEASES`, `_TOOL_SHA`), `scripts/check_deps.py` (`_BACKEND`), the Rust `fleetmux` (`FLEETMUX_WEB_BIN`), `launchers/*.command` and `extensions/*.ts` (`_PI_DIR`, `_LEGACY_LAUNCHER`).
  - Do not confuse: `FLEETMUX_CONFIG` is the path of the private fleet.yaml; `FLEETMUX_CONFIG_DIR` (default `~/.fleetmux/config`, see §50.1; channel credentials / security.yaml) is something else.
- **Release directory**: the root of `install_release.sh` = `$FLEETMUX_RELEASES`, else `~/fleetmux-releases`; `--activate` points `~/fleetmux-current` at the new release. Nothing else next to `HOME` is created or moved.
- **Platform wheels**: `scripts/build_wheels.py` builds one wheel per target (macOS arm64 and x86_64, Linux x86_64 and aarch64) from the pure wheel and the GitHub release assets. Each wheel carries `fleetmux` and `fleetmux-web` in `fleetmux/_bin/`, checked against the release sha256 files. The wheel's `fleetmux` console script (`fleetmux/wheel_launcher.py`) sets `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI` to the `fleetmux-py` beside it and `FLEETMUX_BIN` to the bundled binary, unless the user set them, then execs the binary. Without a bundled binary it exits 127. A source checkout or a release directory runs the binary directly. An installed wheel reads no repository file: the key table is the packaged `fleetmux/backends/keys.json`.
- **`install_release.sh --bin-dir DIR`** installs already-built binaries of one source commit into `<release>/bin`. Both binaries must match their `SHA256SUMS` lines and `manifest.json` must name the same source commit; any mismatch stops the install before anything is written. `--headless` with `--bin-dir` installs only `fleetmux`, for a machine without a desktop. The release venv interpreter: `FLEETMUX_PYTHON` wins; otherwise Python 3.14 candidates are tried (Homebrew paths on macOS, then `python3.14` on `PATH`, `~/.local/bin`, uv-managed builds). The first that meets `deps.yaml` `python.min` and builds a venv with pip wins; there is no fallback to an older Python. A failed install removes the release directory it created.
- **Session names**: the main fleet's registry / session is `fleetmux`, a sub-fleet's `fleetmux:<task id>` (registry files `<state>/fleetmux*.json`, runtime dirs `<state>/fleetmux/<token>`, `<state>/wezterm/<token>`); an unmanaged Redis socket of an older config is `/tmp/fleetmux-<token>.sock`. Plugin ids: dsh `fleetmux-bridge`, opencode `fleetmux.bridge` / `fleetmux.bridge.tui` (package `fleetmux-opencode-bridge`).
- **Not changed**: the Redis keys `fx:*`, the `<run>/config.json` field names (including `cmux_state_dir` / `cmux_bin`, which name the cmux terminal backend), the kit and roster formats (external contracts).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_fleetmux_command.py` (readers in a new process getting the `FLEETMUX_*` values, `usage: fleetmux`, Python gui only execs the form with `--runtime`, install_release root and current link - temporary HOME); `tests/test_install_release.py` (the start command names `<release>/.venv/bin/fleetmux-py`); `rust/crates/app/tests/forward_cli.rs` (argv / stdin / stdout / stderr / exit code passed through unchanged when an unknown subcommand is forwarded, `gui` dispatch with / without runtime, search order, 127 and the hint when nothing is found, a wrong `FLEETMUX_PYTHON_CLI` does not fall back, `--version` format, `--help` / `-h`, no arguments forwarded as `gui`).

### 50.1 One data directory: `~/.fleetmux/`

Each user's fleetmux data lives under one root, `~/.fleetmux/`:

| Content | Default | Environment variable (wins) |
|---|---|---|
| fleet state root: `runs/`, `latest.json`, `codex-home/`, private `fleet.yaml`, `quota.json`, `discovered.yaml`, `dsh-home/` | `~/.fleetmux/` | `FLEETMUX_HOME` |
| window registry, fleetmux runtime | `~/.fleetmux/state/` | `FLEETMUX_STATE` |
| channel accounts, allowlists, leases, `secrets.env`, `security.yaml`, cursor mirrors | `~/.fleetmux/config/` | `FLEETMUX_CONFIG_DIR` |
| launcher private `KEY=value` | `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env` | `FLEETMUX_LAUNCHER_ENV` |
| session progress board | `~/.fleetmux/board/` | `FLEETMUX_BOARD_DIR` |
| a second fleet's home / state | `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/`, `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/state/` | `FLEETMUX_HOME` / `FLEETMUX_STATE` |

- **A second fleet**: while a root fleet runs, a `start` in any of these homes attaches to it instead of starting a second fleet (§19): a task start as a job, a service start as a service sub-fleet. A task root takes task starts (jobs) only (§19 "Task root"). A fleet of its own in a second home needs `start --no-join`.
- **Resolution (`fleetmux/paths.py`)**: fleet home, state, config dir and launcher file are resolved only there: the environment variable when set, else the default above. The session board directory is resolved by `session_board.py` (`FLEETMUX_BOARD_DIR`, else `~/.fleetmux/board`, section 65). The private `fleet.yaml`, `quota.json` and the shared `dsh-home` follow the default root, **not** `FLEETMUX_HOME` (a per-fleet home under `~/.fleetmux/fleets/` reads the same private config).
- **Start-up guard**: the human-typed commands check once for data or settings under the names used before fleetmux (`fleetmux/home_migrate.py` `check`): old data whose new place is not in use yet, a `FLEETMUX_HOME` / `FLEETMUX_STATE` path under an old name, an old environment variable without its `FLEETMUX_*` value. `start` / `resume` / `setup` / `gui` stop with one line saying what to do (exit 2); `status` / `stop` print the line and go on. A separate new-style fleet is not blocked by old top-level data: when `FLEETMUX_HOME` (and `FLEETMUX_STATE`, when set) name a path that is neither under an old name nor the default (`~/.fleetmux`, `~/.fleetmux/state`), for example `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>`, old data in `~/.cmux-fleet`, `~/.local/state/cmux-fleet` or `~/.config/fleetmux` is one note (`old data in X is not read; run 'fleetmux home migrate' after its fleets stop`) and the command goes on, so a new fleet can start while older fleets still run on that data. It stays fatal for the default home (no `FLEETMUX_HOME`, or a value equal to `~/.fleetmux`), for a `FLEETMUX_STATE` equal to `~/.fleetmux/state`, for an old-name value, and when the explicit home is the migration target of an old `~/.cmux-fleet-<name>` that still holds data. With `FLEETMUX_STATE` unset the fleet writes its registry to `~/.fleetmux/state`; a later `home migrate` then skips the old state dir (both hold content), so set both variables for a separate fleet. A fleet started by an older release is stopped with that release's own CLI before migrating (its main run records no session name, so this version would look at another registry). Internal entry points (hooks, actors, the daemon) never check, so an agent in a pane never sees the line and never starts a migration itself. Old data is never read in place.
- **Migration only: `fleetmux home migrate [--dry-run]`** (a Python subcommand; the Rust `fleetmux` forwards it as is, §50; `fleetmux/home_migrate.py` is the one module that knows the old names). It moves `~/.cmux-fleet` -> `~/.fleetmux` (child by child, since `~/.fleetmux` exists already for `board/`), `~/.local/state/cmux-fleet` -> `~/.fleetmux/state`, `~/.config/fleetmux/launchers.env` -> `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env`, `~/.config/fleetmux` -> `~/.fleetmux/config`, every per-fleet home `~/.cmux-fleet-<name>` (only one holding fleet data) -> `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>` and `~/.local/state/cmux-fleet-<name>` -> `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>/state` (only together with its home, or when that home is already migrated, and never when the old home has a `state/` of its own). Each move is a `rename` on one file system and leaves a symlink at the old path; across file systems, when both sides hold content, or when an old home child is named like another item (`board` / `state` / `config` / `launchers.env` / `fleets`) the item is skipped and reported, never merged, exit 1; data is never deleted (only an emptied old directory is `rmdir`ed before its symlink). After the moves, under `~/.fleetmux` and every `~/.fleetmux/fleets/<name>`: registry files and runtime dirs named after the old session (`cmux-fleet*`) get the `fleetmux` name, and every run record (`latest.json`, `runs/*/config.json`, sub-fleet `sub/*/config.json`) is rewritten: moved paths to their new place (by path prefix, so `run`, `fleet_home`, `cmux_state_dir`, `private_config`, `roster`, role files ... all follow), `/tmp/cmux-fleet-<x>.sock` to `/tmp/fleetmux-<x>.sock`, session names `cmux-fleet` / `cmux-fleet:<id>` (also inside a workspace id such as `fleetmux:cmux-fleet`) to `fleetmux` / `fleetmux:<id>`, `CMUX_FLEET_*` environment names to `FLEETMUX_*`. Run records then no longer depend on the symlinks; other files in a run (roster, prompts, a home's `start.sh`) may still name old paths, which the symlinks keep valid. Old releases (`~/cmux-fleet-releases`) are not moved: a record may still pin one as its `python`. Any fleet process on this machine (the fleet CLI of this or an older release, `agent_bridge.py`, `fleetmux server|gui|attach|web`, anything holding a path inside a directory about to move, such as a fleet's `tmux -S` socket) or a run whose daemon holds its lock -> refuse, list them, exit 2 (`--dry-run` only reports). Repeated runs -> `nothing to do`, exit 0.
- **Launchers**: `launchers/start-dsh.command` reads `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env` (or `$FLEETMUX_LAUNCHER_ENV`); a file still at the pre-fleetmux place stops it with one line pointing at `fleetmux home migrate`. `guard` counts `~/.fleetmux/launchers.env` as a config file.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_paths_unify.py` (temporary HOME: environment variables win, defaults under the root, private files do not follow `FLEETMUX_HOME`, inventory of the root and `fleets/*`, launcher file); `tests/test_home_migrate.py` (the guard's lines and exit codes, a separate new-style fleet home beside old data is a note while the default home and old-name values stay fatal, dry-run, moves + symlinks, per-fleet homes, run-record rewrite with the symlinks removed afterwards, records under an already-new home, refused while a fleet runs, skips, idempotent, process recognition, the CLI in a new process).

### 50.2 Package and directory names

- **Python package**: `fleetmux/` (`python -m fleetmux.fleet`, `fleetmux/fleet.py`); console script `fleetmux-py` (§50).
- **Rust workspace**: `rust/`; the binaries are `fleetmux` and `fleetmux-web`.
- **Environment variables**: see the "Environment variable names" item of §50.

### 50.3 Dependencies on the user's machine

These rules apply to every fleetmux release and every applet.

1. **Find programs with one resolver.** `fleetmux/discover.py::search_path()` returns the interactive login shell's `PATH` (`$SHELL -lic`) first, then the process `PATH`. Discovery, `start`, `scripts/check_deps.py` and applets all get the user's `PATH` from it. Do not write a second lookup. Discovery and applets use it as is. Details:
   - `scripts/check_deps.py` puts the checked release's own `bin/` first, so its `fleetmux` binaries win.
   - `start` uses the process `PATH` first and falls back to `search_path()` only for a needed binary it did not find (`discover.which`). With `FLEETMUX_DISCOVERY=off` (tests, scripted starts) it uses the process `PATH` only.
   - When the shell cannot be read (no shell, timeout, no output), use the process `PATH` and print one line saying so.
   - After the login shell's `PATH` and the process `PATH`, `search_path()` appends the directories this machine recorded when it found a program before. The sources are, in this order: the `dependencies` rows of `~/fleetmux-current/bin/installed.json`, the rows of the running release's own `bin/installed.json`, and the `clis` rows of discovery (the `models` block of `fleet-superpowers.yaml`, then `discovered.yaml`). The records are read only; reading them never migrates or rewrites a file. From each row the resolver takes `dir` and `interpreter_dir`. A row written before these fields existed has only `path`; its directory and, for an npm package (`<prefix>/lib/node_modules/...`), `<prefix>/bin` are tried instead. A recorded directory is used only while it exists and `<dir>/<name>` still resolves to the recorded `path` (or `interpreter_path`). A release's own `bin/` (a `bin/` that holds an `installed.json`) is never added, so an older release's `fleetmux` cannot shadow the one being checked. Duplicates are dropped.
   - The order is fixed, not a parameter. The user's current environment always wins; a record is history and may be stale (an upgraded or moved program), so it only fills what the current `PATH` misses. A parameter would let callers build their own order, which is a second lookup.
   - A program started for a probe gets the lookup as its `PATH`, so an `#!/usr/bin/env node` script such as pi finds the node of the same lookup (`check_deps.py`, discovery, `setup --install`). `start` resolves pi's interpreter directory once when the process `PATH` lacks it, runs `pi --version` with it appended and pins it as `pi_path_dirs` in the run config; pi panes append it to their `PATH`.
2. **Record where each program was found.** The dependency check prints the resolved absolute path (links followed, as discovery records it) on every `ok` / `FAIL` line. `install_release.sh` keeps the check output and its rows (`FLEETMUX_DEPS_JSON`) under `$RELEASES/.logs/` and stores the rows as `dependencies` in `<release>/bin/installed.json`. Each found row also records `dir`, the `PATH` entry the program was found in, and for an `#!/usr/bin/env` script `interpreter`, `interpreter_path` and `interpreter_dir` (pi: node). Discovery records the same fields in its `clis` rows. When a program is missing and a record exists, the `FAIL` line, the `setup --install` row and the `start` error end with `last found at <path> (recorded in <file>)`.
3. **Change nothing on the user's machine.** Never edit the user's shell, ssh or system configuration. Never install or upgrade a program for the user, with one exception: `fleetmux setup --install` (below). Recorded directories are used for the lookup only: nothing is linked into a user directory and no `PATH` is exported. A missing or too-old program is reported with its minimum, and the release stops.
   Exception: `fleetmux setup --install`. It installs only what a fleet needs and this machine lacks: redis-server, git, tmux, Node.js 22 or newer, and pi. It finds each program with `search_path()` and prints the exact steps first. System packages use `apt-get` or `brew`. Node.js comes from the official tarball, checked by sha256. pi is installed with `npm` into `~/.fleetmux/tools` and linked next to the `fleetmux` command. It runs the steps only after the user says yes, or with `--yes`. A program that is present is never touched, even when it is below `min`. Other agent CLIs and model logins are never installed. `start` names this command when a needed binary is missing.
4. **Use the user's agent CLIs as they are.**

   | Program | Missing or below `min` |
   |---|---|
   | pi (and node with it) | Release check fails. `start` refuses a pi below `min`. |
   | codex, claude, opencode (`agent: true`) | Check prints `skip <name> <version> (below <min>: not used by fleets on this machine)` and passes. Discovery leaves it out of the default pair. `start` refuses a readable version below `min` with one line (`start: <cli> <version> is below <min> (deps.yaml): ...`). |

   An unreadable version is not judged against `deps.yaml`; the per-CLI start checks still apply. A new run with a readable codex below `min` is refused instead of falling back to typed delivery; a run pinned by an older start keeps its channel on resume.
5. **Tests**: `tests/test_check_deps.py` (optional CLIs skipped, pi and node required, pi found on the login shell's `PATH` with its path recorded, the fallback line), `tests/test_install_release.py` (log and rows outside the release, `dependencies` in `installed.json`), `tests/test_codex_queue_versions.py` / `tests/test_pi.py` (start refuses codex, claude and pi below `min`; start finds a needed CLI on the login shell's `PATH`), `tests/test_discover.py` (`which`, the fallback line, the wall chain leaves out a CLI below `min`), `tests/test_find_pi.py` (a pi only in a private directory found through the previous release's record, old and new row formats; pi's node only in a recorded directory; order, dedup and verification of recorded directories; the `last found at` hint; a check leaves the temp `HOME` unchanged; `start`'s pinned `pi_path_dirs`).

### 50.4 Release acceptance checklist

Run on a machine with a desktop after installing a release (`install_release.sh --activate`) and with the root
fleet running. These steps need the real desktop, so the test suite does not cover them.

1. In a desktop terminal, type `fleetmux`; then in the root fleet's sidebar bash page, type `fleetmux` again. After
   each, list the `fleetmux-web` windows on screen (for example `ps -axo pid,ppid,command | grep fleetmux-web`). Expect
   exactly one root fleet window, in front, after both commands.
2. With no root fleet running (stop it first, or use a machine without one), type `fleetmux` in a desktop terminal.
   Expect one line naming the `bare` fleet and its home, `start`'s output, and then the new (or resumed) root
   fleet's window in front within `bare.window_wait_s`; typing `fleetmux` again raises the same window. List the
   `fleetmux-web` windows:
   exactly one for the root, also after the daemon's first window check.

### 50.5 User installs and the container image

- **`scripts/install.sh`** installs one release in one command, without root, a system Python or a compiler. It creates `$FLEETMUX_RELEASES/v<version>` as a fresh directory and never overwrites an install. It builds the `.venv` on the Python named in `deps.yaml`, provided by `uv`. It installs the prebuilt `fleetmux` binaries of the same release, checked by sha256, and links them into `FLEETMUX_BIN_DIR` (default `~/.local/bin`). It then runs `scripts/check_deps.py`; missing programs are reported and do not stop the install. A failed install removes only the directory it created.
- **npm**: `scripts/build_npm.py` builds npm packages from the release assets: one per platform with the binaries, and a `fleetmux` package that runs them. The npm packages install the terminal only.
- **Container image**: `docker/build.sh` builds a headless Linux server image from `git archive` of one commit. The release inside is installed by `scripts/install_release.sh` and checked against `deps.yaml` during the build. Agent CLIs and logins are not in the image. `docker/fleetmux-docker up` starts one long-lived container with host network, the host home mounted at the same path and the host user. On first `up` it downloads the agent CLIs pinned in `deps.yaml` `agent_installs` into a volume. `fleetmux-docker <args>` runs `fleetmux <args>` in the container. `docker stop` and `up` keep runs resumable; `down` or a new image does not.

## 51. Named sub-fleet workers can run pi; a new session after every result: `worker_cli` / `pi_model` / `fresh_after_result`

Background: a named sub-fleet used to run only official Claude workers (section 19). An entry can now pick pi workers. The per-item `/clear` of section 28 is retired (section 48), so `fresh_after_result` gives a clean context after every result instead.

- **Roster keys** (`subfleets[]` entries, `roster.subfleet_cli_errors`): `worker_cli` accepts only `claude` (default) / `pi` - opencode / dsh startup needs the dedicated `start` checks (`opencode_start_problem` / `dsh_adapter.start_problem`), which sub-fleet startup does not run, so they are not allowed yet. `worker_cli: pi` requires `pi_model` (the `provider/model` of pi models.json) and may carry `pi_thinking` (same values as on a roster row); `pi_model` / `pi_thinking` without pi rejects the whole roster. Rows of a pi sub-fleet's child roster may not set `profile` (a card would switch that slot to another CLI, and mixed CLIs are refused under v2 anyway; all workers run the entry's `pi_model`).
- **Child config**: `child_worker_cfg` takes the entry's `worker_cli` (unset = claude, never inherited from the parent fleet); `pi_model` / `pi_thinking` / `fresh_after_result` work like the other entry keys (the entry's value, else the built-in default, never inherited from the parent); the parent fleet's `pi_models` / `pi_thinkings` / `pi_home` are never carried into the child config. The supervisor is still the adopted Claude pane of the parent fleet; the pair claude x pi service is already in the cli_pairs table.
- **pi home**: `spawn_child` builds an isolated pi home `sub/<name>/pi-home` for a pi sub-fleet (`pi_adapter.prepare_home`, source = the parent config's `pi_agent_dir`, the same as `start` does for a pi run; a named sub-fleet archives the previous round's directory every time it starts, so it never collides with an existing one).
- **Start gate** (`subfleet.check` -> `named_pi_problem`): the parent config's `pi` binary can be found and the parent fleet runs with `--allow-unsandboxed-pi` (pi has no sandbox, the same requirement as `start`); otherwise the program refuses `up` with a reason.
- **`fresh_after_result: true`**: when `RuntimeV2.publish` publishes a result that really was delivered to this slot (the same test as the original section 28), it marks the slot `fresh_pending=1` + `result_fresh=1`; once the slot is idle and settled on the bus (`bridge_available=1`), `Fleet.idle_rotate` immediately runs the existing section 37 `fresh_rotation` (next generation in the same slot, no handoff note), so by the time the next request arrives the new session is usually READY; if the request comes before the rotation, the fresh branch of `deliver()` rotates. `result_fresh` distinguishes "rotate after a result" from "a fresh dispatch was withdrawn": for the latter idle_rotate still clears the mark. Works for any CLI; no typing. A slot under an applet lease keeps one session for the whole lease: its results set no fresh mark, and when the lease ends the slot gets `fresh_pending=1` and `result_fresh=1` once. Chosen over a dispatch-level `"fresh": true` because the callers involved (a memory shell and a scanner) dispatch directly with `to`; the supervisor makes no dispatch decision, so nobody could set `fresh`.
- **Cost / gaps**: one extra CLI cold start + one READY turn after every result (about 10-20 seconds for pi), during which the slot is `starting` (a request arriving right after waits for it); every rotation saves an old screen snapshot and writes a history line (the `handoffs/` of a high-frequency sub-fleet grows). A pi pane returns results through the `fleet_result` tool; if the business instructions say "do not use any tools", the role file must state that `fleet_result` is the exception.
- **Tests**: `tests/test_subfleet_pi_workers.py` (roster keys accepted / rejected, no profile on pi rows, the child config inherits neither the parent's CLI nor its pi tables, after `up` the child config is pi + its own pi home and passes `validate_config`, refused by the program when the parent lacks `--allow-unsandboxed-pi`, `fresh_pending` after a published result with `fresh_after_result` on / off).

## 52. First-run discovery: local agent CLIs / login state / models; the default pair follows the user's ranking

Requirement: on first use fleetmux finds the local agent CLIs, their login state and their models, the same way Paseo and T3 Code do. It picks a default supervisor and worker pair from the ranking in `fleet.yaml#discovery`.

- **Same approach as T3 Code / Paseo**: only CLIs that are installed locally, recent enough and logged in count; fleetmux never installs or logs in for the user, and says plainly what is missing and how to fix it. `fleetmux setup` probes once and rewrites the cache; `fleetmux start` / `fleetmux gui` probe once automatically when there is no cache and print the result table in the current terminal. `fleetmux gui --runtime D` opens the window directly from Rust without Python and does not trigger it (§50).
- **Ranking**: `fleet.yaml#discovery` has `supervisor: [astra, fable, opus, sol, deepseek flash]` and `worker: [opus, sol, sonnet, terra, deepseek flash]`. Unlisted models rank last. A space inside a pattern means the parts appear in order, not necessarily adjacent (`deepseek flash` matches `deepseek-v4.1-flash-sg`, not `deepseek-v4-pro`). The same two lists are the wall fallback chains (section 23 "on a wall, move down a model chain").
- **Default worker thinking level is high**: `fleet.yaml#discovery.worker_thinking: high`. Without `--pi-thinking` the pi thinking level of the whole run takes it (previously off); a Claude worker not taken over by the advisor always gets `--effort <it>` (a slot the advisor takes over still uses the advisor's level, and the advisor's default level `advisor.DEFAULT_EFFORT` also changed from medium to high); a worker that switches to pi after a wall takes it as its thinking level (it travels with the wall chain into Redis `fx:wall:chains.worker_thinking`). An explicitly given level always wins.
- **Probing** (`fleetmux/discover.py`): CLIs are looked up on the login shell's PATH (a GUI launch lacks PATH, and cmux pane shim paths expire with the session), recording the realpath; minimum versions come from `deps.yaml`; login state: claude `auth status`, codex `login status`, pi / opencode count as logged in when their model list is non-empty; models: claude takes the aliases of model cards with cli=claude, pi `--list-models`, opencode `models`, ollama `list` (failure = installed but not running); each provider in pi `models.json` that has a `baseUrl` gets one `GET <baseUrl>/models` (no key, no proxy, 401 still counts as online), and models on an offline gateway are not candidates. Each CLI probe has a 10-second timeout. Each gateway `GET <baseUrl>/models` has a 3-second timeout. Probes run concurrently.
- **Ranking and default**: `fleet.yaml#discovery` (a block of the same name in the private file overrides it whole). Model ids are lowercased and split into words on `- / _ .` for contiguous matching; within one pattern newer versions come first, then `cli_order`; `only_via` enforces §6 (Claude models only through the official claude, gpt only through pi). Models that match only a supervisor pattern and no worker pattern (astra, fable) are supervisor-only; all others are workers, unlisted ones last. The default pair = the first pair, in order, of supervisor candidates x worker candidates whose `mode` in `cli_pairs` is verified; higher-ranked ones dropped because of the combination table are written into the result table as `passed over`.
- **Cache**: the `models` hot block of `fleet-superpowers.yaml` (section 56). An old `discovered.yaml` is read once to migrate it. `FLEETMUX_DISCOVERED` names a separate cache file instead (mode 0600). The private `fleet.yaml` is never written. `start` uses its default pair only when neither `--supervisor-cli` nor `--worker-cli` is given, and fills in only the model arguments not given (`--supervisor-model` / `--worker-model` now parse with default None and are filled in start: command line > cache default > `DEFAULTS`); without a cache or with `FLEETMUX_DISCOVERY=off` the behavior is as before. A failed probe only warns and never blocks start.
- **Privacy**: the login probe keeps only `logged_in` / `auth_method` (claude also returns the email and org, which are discarded); `models.json` is read only for provider names, `baseUrl` and model ids, `apiKey` is never read out or written to disk, and `auth.json` is not read; the cache and the output contain no gateway addresses. When the private `fleet.yaml` is readable by other users the result table suggests `chmod 600`; it does not change it for the user.
- **Not in this cut**: refusing an unusable `--pi-model` against the cache before starting (a stale cache would refuse wrongly; it would need a fresh probe); the result table in a fleetmux pane; the advisor's automatic model choice still only considers models with a model card. The default pair is checked as verified only for `discovery.mode` (task): when starting with `--mode service`, if the service row of the chosen pair is not verified, the combination table refuses it as usual (today pi+claude is verified in both modes). `discover.py` reads no repository file at import time (installed as site-packages without `scripts/` / `deps.yaml` it still starts, it just skips the minimum-version check); getting PATH from the login shell takes up to 10 seconds and is the main source of a slower first start.
## 53. Channels: WeChat / Feishu / Telegram / email / Slack / app push

Goal: give fleetmux inbound interfaces for WeChat, Feishu, Telegram, email and an app, so that fleetmux replaces every function of the earlier OpenClaw assistant gateway; WeChat must never go through a decryption route; deletes can be approved from a phone; the app is Paseo for now. The interface, item by item, is in `docs/contracts.md` §8; this section only states the design constraints. The single daemon, the private Redis, the slot / generation labels, the task / service protocols, the set of result states and the meaning of every existing Redis key are unchanged; **a fleet without `channels:` behaves exactly as before**.

- **In one sentence**: a channel = a second keyboard for the supervisor + a second exit for fleet events. All inbound traffic reuses §5's `operator_input` (the same path as typing in the supervisor pane: service creates a `ui-<uuid>` request, a running task queues `[Operator instruction]`, no task goes through `typed_task_allowed`; the refusal texts already exist); no new dispatch path is created; outside changes to the plan are told only to the supervisor, never given straight to a worker. Outbound watches existing state (`fx:result:*`, `fx:task`, `fx:escalations`, end of lifecycle) and pushes it out.
- **Where it attaches**: the resident service fleet on this machine (main fleet, transport v2). Task fleets and sub-fleets do not attach; a roster with `channels:` makes `start` refuse on those runs. One channel account serves only this machine's fleet; there is no cross-machine or cross-fleet routing (phase one only talks to the resident service fleet on this machine).
- **One actor child process per fleet, not a thread inside the daemon**: a crash or hang in a network library (lark-oapi and the like) must not take the daemon down, so the channels run in one child process of the daemon (`fleetmux channel actor <config.json>`). Inside it every roster entry (`type:account`) has its own inbound thread and its own outbound account queue (`fx:channel:outq:<type>:<account>`, each with its own rate limit / retry / dead letters); **a problem with one account (needs a new login, lease held by another run, adapter crash) stops only that account: the actor does not exit, other accounts are unaffected, the fleet is not paused and no slot is held**; an account with bad credentials retries when its account file changes or every 10 minutes, one whose lease is taken retries every 5 minutes. lark-oapi 1.5.3 allows only one WS per process, so a roster has at most one Feishu entry (added to `parse_channels` in P0b). The daemon starts and supervises this one process the way section 1 has "the daemon manages agents": after an exit it restarts with a backoff starting at 30 seconds, doubling, capped at 5 minutes, and reset after 10 stable minutes (all configurable defaults, run config `channel_supervise`); persistent failure only raises an alert; a run without `channels:` starts no actor process; nothing is hung on launchd / cron. The daemon process imports no adapter. Feishu without lark-oapi only makes Feishu inbound `missing_dependency`; sending works as usual. Proxies are bypassed only per client scope; the process environment is never changed. When the parent daemon is gone, the actor exits by itself within 5 seconds and releases its leases.
- **Purely deterministic mapping, no LLM guessing intent**: plain text = operator input; `/status` is read-only; `/approve` `/reject` only answer the delete pending items pushed by item 5.2; any other `/word` gets the `unknown_command` receipt (`envelope.RECEIPTS`) and does not enter `operator_input` (as section 5 "slash commands are not enqueued again"). Every refusal text goes back to the chat; silent swallowing is forbidden; senders outside the allow-list are only audited (`channel.drop_policy`), with no reply. A native Codex supervisor has no `operator_input` entry (section 5 input boundary), so the reply is the `no_operator_input` receipt. Images / files are downloaded to `<run>/inbox/files/`, their paths are appended to the body, and the message goes on as text; anything too large or unrecognized is refused explicitly (section 5 "never drop an image silently").
- **origin**: the conversation that started a request is stored in a separate key `fx:channel:origin:<request id>`; **it is not put into `fx:req:<id>`**: `publish` deletes `fx:req:<id>` when the request is settled and the public result does not carry it, so outbound would not find it; a separate key also leaves `publish` and resume replay untouched. **The actor writes this key after the fleet answers `accepted`, with `EX` 7 days; `Fleet.handle` does not write it** (`Fleet.handle` is not changed for channels). There is a small window between the actor writing origin and the worker answering, so when the outbound watcher sees a result without origin it checks again next round while the result is under 60 seconds old, and only after 60 seconds treats it as an external request (marked pushed, not sent). A service request's result is pushed back to the originating conversation; when big work is moved to a task sub-fleet, the sub-fleet's follow-up result `<id>.done` (ok / failed) returns to the conversation of `<id>` by `envelope.origin_id('<id>.done') == <id>`. Requests without origin (command line, other programs) are not pushed. Splitting **reuses section 44's existing mechanism** (the escalate answer carries `proposal` / `workers`, program-assigned `M<n>` numbers, approve may carry models, adoption after rotation); P7 only adds the service side: the original request is settled as `ok` + `delegated` in the `escalation_tick` beat that reads spawn `accepted`, followed by `<id>.done`, with the sub-fleet at `lifecycle=None` and the time limit `split_budget_h`. When a split settles, its failure / replay semantics and the sub-fleet lifecycle are in contracts section 8. **Resume pins a split**: `resume_run` calls `Fleet.split_pin_on_resume` before `respawn`: if the request still exists (`fx:req:<id>` not settled), the slot still exists and the request is not owned by someone else, it is pinned back to `dispatched / worker=<slot>` (restoring the ownership from before the restart, not counted as a `replay`, audited as `split.pinned`; `requeue_requests` skips it, audited as `request.pinned_to_split`), and the spawn then proceeds normally (an existing spawn request file runs next beat; `rotating` goes through rotation); for `rotating` without `rotating_at` yet, `split_launch` is written first and `respawn` starts the new generation with the card the supervisor chose (when the slot is resumed with an unchanged generation, the first branch of `split_rotate` rotates as usual). Only a split that cannot be pinned (request already settled / slot not in the roster / request owned by another slot) is voided: `split_drop` + one line to the supervisor, `[escalate] M<n> approval lapsed on resume: <reason>`. An approval is a decision the supervisor has persisted (possibly with a chosen model); resume already honors an `accepted` spawn (S11), and it honors `approved` / `rotating` the same way; voiding is only the fallback. Known boundary: section 17 `restart_lost` (one slot's session lost, not `--resume`) also replays that slot's requests through `replay_request`, and is not wired to pinning in this change.
- **Where proactive pushes go**: following OpenAlice `connector-registry.ts` "most recent interaction": task final states, confirmation dialogs, end of run and daemon down go to the channel / conversation of the allow-listed user's most recent private chat (`fx:channel:last`, mirrored to the local `state/last.json`); if the user has never written → the first channel in the roster whose account file sets `PUSH_TO` (`PUSH_TO` must be the allow-listed user's own one-to-one conversation; it cannot make the bot open a conversation, and a Telegram user must `/start` first); if neither exists → audit `channel.no_target`. Each channel pushes only the events listed under its roster `push:`.
- **Identity and security**: the allow-list accepts only the user (Telegram user id, Feishu open_id, WeChat `ilink_user_id`, email sender address **and** both DKIM and SPF passing and aligned with the From domain in the topmost `Authentication-Results` of the trusted receiving server; AR is only a mail header, not independent authentication, and holds only if the receiving service strips forged AR headers of the same name; to be verified on a real machine, no anti-spoofing claim is made), and only private chats. Every TLS connection verifies the certificate and the host name. The policy is enforced in the adapter base class (allow-list, private chat, authenticated, `message_id` idempotent for 1 hour), and every adapter runs the same parameterized contract tests. Credentials and the allow-list live only in the local `~/.fleetmux/config/` (§50.1; old default `~/.config/fleetmux/` until migrated; changeable with `FLEETMUX_CONFIG_DIR`), never in the repo, a kit, the run directory or any directory a worker can read; errors, audits and logs never carry credential values. Inbound text gets no extra privilege: what it can do = what typing in the supervisor pane can do.
- **One consumer per credential**: a machine-level lease `<config dir>/leases/<type>-<account>.lock` (flock + holder run id). If it cannot be taken → that channel does not open, audit `channel.lease_busy`, shown in control, never silent (Telegram allows one poller per token; Feishu delivers an app's events to one connection only). The lease covers only receiving; sending takes no lease, so the supervisor can still `channel send` while the daemon is down.
- **Deletes can be approved on a phone**: only delete-type terminal confirmation dialogs (section 24 delete classification; whether opencode / dsh delete permission requests are covered is left open), with a pending record registered only in runs that configure `channels:` (`fx:channel:pending:<p-id>`, `p-` + 8 hex digits, P6b's `channels/approve.py`; not in `fx:escalations`); the card is pushed only to the allow-listed user's private chat; two-step confirmation: step one `/approve <p-id>` makes `approve.handle_command` reply with a confirmation card carrying a one-time nonce (16 hex digits); step two `/approve <p-id> <nonce>` or `/reject <p-id>` goes as a `dialog_user_answer` event to `RuntimeV2.user_dialog_answer` for checking (the nonce appears only in the card sent to the user's private chat; Redis stores only its hash; it never enters audits / supervisor messages / exec); the confirm step lasts 120 seconds and the record 1800 seconds (configurable positive defaults in run config.json, approved by the supervisor; checks and behavior test-first in v2); expired means no key press; before pressing, the watchdog re-captures the screen on its next beat and runs the full `answer_dialog` check (generation, session, scope, pane ownership, screen-tail digest, same dialog, delete classification); if anything changed it does not press and replies "changed, please look again"; **approval presses only a consent key explicitly registered in the prompt table; inferring Enter from `YES_CURSOR` is forbidden; with no registered key no approval is offered** (where it is registered: the `watchdog_prompts.user_approve` block of an explicit `--prompt-table` file, empty by default, without widening what `config/fleet.yaml` and the private fleet.yaml may override; an entry's `match` may be the cursor text written by the operator, `keys` takes only keys the entry registers explicitly; it is registered in the `watchdog_prompts.user_approve` block of an explicit `--prompt-table` file (`watchdog.parse_user_approve`). By default nothing is registered, so by default a phone can only reject; with a registration a positive approval works as usual and the seven review checks are unchanged); reject presses Escape; **pressing a key does not resume dispatch automatically** (the `prompt_wait` pause stays; the receipt points to the existing resume entry; no automatic resume exists); audits `dialog.user_answered` / `dialog.user_refused`. **This item changes how section 24's "delete-type dialogs always go to the user; neither the program nor the supervisor presses a key" is carried out, not the principle**: the authority for the key press comes from the user's own two-step confirmation in a private chat; the program only presses on the user's behalf. Supervisor decision JSON and the fleet CLI have no branch leading to this path (meaning delete dialogs `kind=dialog`; P8l's local `channel approve` only accepts `kind=action` + `tier=high` + `confirm.high == desk`, see contracts section 8 "high-tier out-of-band methods"); a `dialog_user_answer` event without the correct nonce, sender and conversation is never accepted, pinned by tests; but the separate key and the nonce are **not** a security boundary against processes of the same OS user (see this section's known gaps). Non-delete dialogs are still ruled by the supervisor (section 24); the channel only informs and does not take over the ruling.
- **Lifecycle notices**: when the roster has `channels:`, the `tg` path of `lifecycle_close` goes through the channels instead (the new config wins); without it `FLEETMUX_TG_CMD` works as before. Daemon down cannot be announced by the daemon itself: §36's "Daemon down" section of the supervisor AGENTS.md gains a step `channel send --event daemon_down`.
- **Direct to a worker**: a channel user can talk straight to a worker without the supervisor relaying. The lower layer dispatches a service request with `to` directly; the channel entry uses it: (1) a message that opens by naming `@dev-1` / `dev-1:` -> the program sends it as a service request with `to`; the supervisor is not involved; (2) when the worker's result is pushed back, the thread under that message is recorded as belonging to that worker, and a reply in the thread goes straight back to it (same slot, continuing the session); (3) a new message that names no one goes to the supervisor as before. All parsing is deterministic; no model guesses. Every pushed result ends with a one-line hint on how to reach the worker directly. On Slack every reply goes into the thread under the user's message, and results are also `reply_broadcast` to the conversation (text posted only in the thread is visible on a phone only after opening it). Interface: contracts section 8 "direct to a worker".
- **app**: Paseo for now (zero development: supervisor-pane input inside Paseo already goes through `operator_input`); pushes use `type: exec` (the outbound item JSON is handed to a configured external command, with no business address built in; the command is the `COMMAND=` line of the local account file, split into argv by `shlex` rules and run with `shell=False`, no shell). The delete-approval `channels/approve.py` belongs to P6b. A future self-built fleet app plugs in as a new adapter on the same envelope / mapping / outbound, with no separate dispatch path.
- **Slack (the 6th channel)**: `fleetmux/channels/slack.py`. Socket Mode receives direct messages; replies go to the same conversation; proactive pushes use `conversations.open`; buttons use callbacks. The interface is the same as the other channels (contracts section 8 "Slack specifics"): account file `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` / `SLACK_APP_TOKEN` / `SLACK_ALLOW_USERS` (empty = reject all); the optional dependency `websocket-client==1.8.0` is only for fleets that configure slack, and when missing it only stops this one channel; **every send uses `unfurl_links=false`, `unfurl_media=false`**; Slack tokens (`xoxb-` / `xoxp-` / `xapp-`) are masked whole by `redact`.
- **WeChat**: only Tencent's official ClawBot (iLink protocol, rewritten with the Python standard library from the source of the MIT plugin 2.4.10; protocol details in contracts section 8 "WeChat specifics", none of it verified on a real machine; the QR-scan `redirect_host` only changes the polling address, the business address comes from `confirmed.baseurl`; `bot_agent` honestly says fleetmux; the batch cursor is committed only after the batch is settled, not commit-first-then-process as the plugin does); local decryption, reading the WeChat database and pulling keys from memory are **forbidden**. QR login is the WeChat module-level `LOGIN` (per the 2.4.10 source, the POST that fetches the QR code carries no `base_info`; business POSTs do); the batch cursor is stored as `get_updates_buf` in this account's store; session expiry `-14` -> health `needs_login`. Phase one is plain text only: `PENDING = ()`, inbound media is explicitly refused with `weixin.REFUSED` (WeChat phase one accepts text only); CDN media needs the protocol's AES and comes later. Provenance (P8): this phase does not carry `ref_msg`, does not query a quote cache and does not download quoted media, and P8b gives WeChat no always-empty provenance function; fixed media labels are the closed-constant text exception (no file names); top-level TEXT / VOICE is treated as direct input but its real author is not guaranteed; iLink cannot detect forwarding, which is listed as a capability limit, and no claim is made that source detection is complete on every platform (contracts section 8 "WeChat specifics"). Three protocol questions (can a non-OpenClaw client send and receive, can a push go out without `context_token`, how many ClawBots can one WeChat account bind) need a real QR-scan test by the user and are outside the development run.
- **Ported, with the business removed**: the send and receive parts (adapter base class, throttle, queue retry and dead letters, actor backoff, idempotency, plain urllib sending in 4000-character segments) were ported from an earlier notification bus with every business-specific part removed. One contract test scans the `fleetmux/channels/` source to keep business leftovers out.
- **Health states and redaction**: health states are unified as `ok` (normal) / `needs_login` (credentials rejected, or account file missing / broken; the error kind is still recorded as `auth` / `bad_config`; only this account stops) / `retrying` (transient failure); the actor reads only the `health()` state and never parses error text. Every error string and audit field goes through `channels.redact` (account values, `Authorization` / `Bearer`, `bot<token>` in URLs, values whose names contain token / secret / password). A roster has at most one Feishu entry (lark-oapi: one WS per process). Button commands longer than 64 bytes are not truncated: the Telegram adapter itself degrades the whole card into typed commands, and the shared `card_problem` no longer rejects long commands. Audits `channel.cursor_reset` and `channel.lease_reclaimed`. The audit name `dialog.user_resumed` is reserved but never written, because a phone approval never resumes dispatch. The send metadata `Channel.fleet_name` / `send_ref` (`channels.fleet_label(cfg)`) are only instance attributes; constructor and send signatures do not change. The actor hash gains `sending / sending_at / drain / drained`; the heartbeat runs on a separate thread every 10 seconds; the parent check looks only at `getppid`.
