Usage: [relay.md](relay.md). Refs, records and files: [contracts.md](contracts.md) section 13.

Decision: relay development is a built-in fleetmux feature. The code is `fleetmux/relay/`, the command is
`fleetmux relay`, the settings are `fleet.yaml#relay`, and nothing is installed separately. There is one hub:
the state remote, which defaults to the code remote. There are no meeting points, no arbitration between hubs
and no `diverged` refs.

"Relay" here is unrelated to the advisor's mid-task rescue relays (section 27, `fx:advice:relays`).

- **Principles.**
  1. Only state pushed to the hub counts. Machines never send state to each other.
  2. Push all the time: a commit, a snapshot every `snapshot_s`, a heartbeat every `beat_s`. A goodbye helps
     but is never relied on, because a closed lid gives no warning.
  3. The program does what is deterministic: liveness, claims, snapshots, launching and stopping sub-fleets.
     Supervisors do what needs judgment.
  4. One owner per task at a time. This is enforced by an atomic compare-and-swap push.
  5. Every threshold is a setting in `fleet.yaml#relay`. No number is hard-coded.
- **Process.** The root daemon starts the program as the built-in applet `applet-relay-1`, with
  `{python} -m fleetmux.relay run --node <name>`. Conditions: a root service fleet on transport v2, and
  `relay.enabled` true (default false).
  - No task fleet or sub-fleet starts it.
  - It also needs this machine's name in the inventory (the node name) and at least one worker row for the
    applet lease policy. Without either, the daemon logs `applet.builtin_error` once and does not start it.
  - The applet host restarts it after a crash. No cron or launchd entry exists.
  - The relay main loop writes the applet heartbeat itself (`relay_applet.LoopHeartbeat`), so a hung loop is
    judged stalled like any applet.
  - The name `applet-relay-1` is reserved in rosters, and `fleetmux applet list` shows the built-in.
  - `relay.enabled` is read through `fleet_config.relay()` at daemon start and resume and at every roster reload.
    Any error reads as off.
  - Limitation: every root service fleet with the switch on starts its own relay, so turn it on for one root
    service fleet per machine.
  - Code: `fleetmux/relay_applet.py` (`enabled`, `hosts`, `specs`, `compose`, `reserved`),
    `Fleet.builtin_applets`, `Fleet.reload_roster`, `applet_cli.declared`.
- **Modules.**
  - Git layer: `gitio`, `refs`, `records`, `repo`, `hub`, `errors`, `clock`.
  - Snapshots, checkpoints and work trees: `snapshot`, `checkpoint`, `materialize`.
  - Heartbeat and liveness: `beats`, `nodestatus`, `liveness`.
  - Scoring and takeover: `assign` (capability score), `reclaim`, `takeover`, `progress`.
  - Local state and notices: `state`, `events`.
  - Settings: `params`.
  - Flow layer:
    - Job files: `jobfile` (job and spec files, the machine-path scan).
    - Dependencies: `deps`.
    - Placement: `toolchain` (needs parsing), `placement` (filter, rank, stagger, `ClaimQueue`).
    - Launch: `launch` (spec rendering, `Executor`, `FleetmuxExecutor`, gate queue reading).
    - The main loop: `flow` (`Flow`: claims, takeover, dependency advance, delivery, leave, return).
    - Hub and work trees: `hubstore` (`HubStore`, `GitWorkspace`), `backend` (`GitBackend`).
    - Inventory copy: `inventory`.
    - Coordinator role: `coordinator`.
    - Text limits from the settings: `knobs`.
    - Command line: `cli`, `__main__`.
  - Outside the package: `fleetmux/relay_applet.py` (built-in applet), `fleetmux/fleet_superpowers_capability.py`
    (inventory fields), `fleet_config.relay()` (the one settings accessor).
- **State in git.** Task records, node records, snapshots and salvage refs live under `refs/relay/<job>/` on the
  state remote. Code branches `relay/<job>/<task>/e<epoch>-<machine>` live on the code remote.
  - Each ref has one way of writing. Node, snapshot, salvage and branch refs have a single writer and are
    pushed directly.
  - Every task-record change is a compare-and-swap push that names the expected old sha. A rejection means
    another machine was first, and the program does not retry.
  - A task record commit carries a random `nonce`, so a push whose outcome is unknown (timeout) is settled by
    sha on the next listing (`cas_pending`).
  - The checkpoint CAS pushes the record and the `snap/<task>` ref atomically.
  - The CAS is one `git push --atomic --porcelain` with `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<expected sha>` for each
    record update.
  - The program keeps its own cache repository under its state directory. Git network calls are bounded by
    `git_timeout_s`, and a checkpoint push by `snapshot_timeout_s`.
- **Start of a job.**
  1. The initiator writes `.relay/<job>/` into the repository and pushes it.
  2. `relay init` creates one record per task with a create-only CAS. A task with `depends_on` starts
     `blocked`, the others `pending`. The `merge` task and, when enabled, the `coordinator` record are added.
  3. `init` refuses the job when it finds reserved task names (`merge`, `merged`, `coordinator`), unknown
     dependencies, a cycle, or a file under `.relay/<job>/` that names a machine's own absolute path: a path
     under `/Users`, `/home`, `/Volumes`, `/private`, `/root` or `/mnt`, a path that starts with `~/`, `$HOME`,
     or a drive letter. System paths such as `/bin/bash` are allowed.
- **Placement and claims.** Every machine ranks every claimable task with the same rule, on the same data.
  1. **Filter**: the machine is `online` (not `rejoining`, not `leaving`), has every toolchain in `needs`
     (`node>=20` style bounds), has free disk above `disk_floor_any_gb` (heavy tasks: above
     `disk_floor_heavy_gb`), and is under `max_tasks_per_node` / `max_heavy_per_node`.
  2. **Order**: an eligible `prefer` machine comes first. Otherwise a heavy task goes by capability score
     (`cores`, `mem_gb` and `disk_free_gb`, each times its weight from `score`), highest first, then by the fewest
     owned tasks, then by name. A light task goes by the fewest owned tasks, then by capability score, then by name.
  3. **Stagger**: the machine ranked k waits (k-1) x `claim_stagger_s` and claims only if the task is still
     claimable.
  4. **Claim**: a CAS sets `owner` to the claiming machine, adds 1 to `epoch`, sets `state: working` and
     appends to `owners`.
  5. When no machine is eligible, the task stays `pending` and the coordinator's supervisor gets
     `relay.unplaceable`: `<task> cannot be placed: <reason>`. The reason is `toolchain: <missing>`, `disk`,
     `full` or `no machine online`; several reasons are joined with `; `.
  - Code: `placement` decides claims. It provides `candidates`, `refusal` (toolchain via `toolchain.missing`,
    disk floors, task and heavy caps), `rank`, `unplaceable_reason`, `stagger_s` and `ClaimQueue`.
  - `assign` (`score_of`, `why_not`, `rank`, with a `candidate_filter` hook) is the state layer's primitive, used
    by `reclaim`. Its disk-floor and cap checks give the same answers as `placement`.
  - The machine data comes from each node record's `machine`, which mirrors the inventory fields of
    section 56. `refresh` writes `machines.<self>.hardware`, `always_on` and `toolchains`; the relay reads them
    and copies the values of other machines.
- **Launch from repository files.** The job definition is a directory in the repository, never a local fleet
  directory, so any machine with a clone can run any task. After a claim the program:
  1. Builds the work tree from the start point.
  2. Renders `.relay/<job>/<task>/job.spec.yaml` (`jobfile.load_spec`) into a local dev_task job spec
     (`launch.render`):
     - Sets `name` (`relay-<job>-<task>-e<epoch>`; the executor finds a task's running sub-fleet by this name),
       `lifecycle: dev_task`, and `deliver.repo` (the work tree), `deliver.feature_branch` (the task
       branch), `deliver.deploy_branch` and `deliver.queue`.
     - Copies `hours`, `budget`, `artifacts`, `workers`, `supervisor_profile` and `worker_profiles` from the task
       spec. A `roster.yaml` file and a `roles` directory in the task directory are passed as `roster` and `roles`.
     - The deploy branch is `relay/<job>/<task>/delivered`, or `relay/<job>/merged` for `merge`. It is created
       at `base` and never moved by hand.
     - Prefixes `deliver.test_cmd` with `/usr/bin/env RELAY_PYTHON=<interpreter>`.
     - The goal starts with "First read <task dir>/TAKEOVER.md and continue from its Next step." on a takeover,
       and with "Then run: git merge <commits>" when `merge_in` is set. It ends by telling every slot to keep
       and commit `<task dir>/notes/<slot>/PROGRESS.md`.
     - A spec that sets a key the program fills is refused.
  3. Submits it to the local root fleet with the existing commands (attached `start`, `task -`; `stop` later).
     The executor first checks that the local root fleet runs, and refuses otherwise. The supervisor plays no
     part. A sub-fleet that cannot start parks the task with the reason.
  4. Sends `relay.take` to the root supervisor.
  - Delivery: the relay watches the dev_task gate's queue file. When the gate has queued a commit for the
    deploy branch, the relay pushes that commit to the code remote from the task's work tree. Only then does it
    record `delivered`. A failed push is retried next round, and the dependents stay `blocked`.
  - Code: `launch` (`render`, `deploy_branch_of`, `queued_delivery`, `Executor`, `FleetmuxExecutor`),
    `hubstore.GitWorkspace`, `Flow._delivery_watch`. Tests use `FakeExecutor` and never start a fleet.
- **Checkpoints and snapshots.**
  - A phase checkpoint (`relay checkpoint <task> --phase <name>`) records the commit, a snapshot and the
    progress summary of the slots' `PROGRESS.md`.
  - An auto snapshot every `snapshot_s` is taken only when the work tree changed. It writes all files git does
    not ignore into a commit through a temporary index, with the worker's HEAD as parent. It never touches
    the worker's index, HEAD or files.
  - A snapshot leaves out only files that match `relay.secret_globs` and that the worker's HEAD does not track.
    A secret-named file that HEAD tracks (for example `.env.example`) stays in the snapshot, because leaving it
    out would read as a deletion on takeover. A snapshot that leaves files out sends the `relay.snapshot` notice.
  - The progress summary comes from the newest `notes/<slot>/PROGRESS.md` of the task: the number of Done items,
    and the Doing, Next and Red tests items clipped to `text_max`.
  - A rejected checkpoint CAS means the task changed owner, and the machine enters the return flow.
- **Heartbeat and liveness.**
  - Each machine pushes its node record every `beat_s` and at once on a state change.
  - Each machine lists the hub every `poll_s` and notes, on its own monotonic clock, when each machine's refs
    last changed.
  - Online = a change within `lost_after_s`. Leaving = the record says so; others may take its tasks after
    `offline_grace_s`. Lost = no change for `lost_after_s` and no goodbye.
  - A failed listing pauses every timer, so a machine without network never judges the others lost.
  - Timestamps of other machines are never compared.
- **Leave.** `relay leave` runs three steps, and each step goes on even if an earlier one failed:
  1. Snapshot and push each owned task.
  2. CAS each one to `handed_over`.
  3. Push a `leaving` node record with the reason.
  - When it cannot run, others fall back to the lost rule from the last auto snapshot.
- **Takeover.** For a `working` or `handed_over` task of a lost or leaving owner, the first-ranked machine runs a
  takeover CAS (epoch + 1, `owners` gets `takeover:lost` or `takeover:goodbye`). It then:
  1. Fetches the start point (the snapshot when it is newer than the checkpoint commit, else the commit, else
     `base`).
  2. Creates `relay/<job>/<task>/e<epoch>-<machine>` and a work tree byte-identical to that start point.
  3. Writes `TAKEOVER.md`: previous owner, why it left, the checkpoint, the five progress sections, red tests
     and the old branch.
  4. Launches the sub-fleet as above.
  - With no eligible taker the record turns `reclaimed` and waits. The taker reads only the hub, never the
    machine that left.
- **Dependencies and merge.**
  - On a newly `delivered` record, any machine moves each `blocked` task whose dependencies are all delivered
    to `pending` by CAS. `base` = the delivered commit of the first dependency, `merge_in` = those of the
    others. The sub-fleet merges `merge_in` first.
  - The `merge` task depends on every task. It is claimed and taken over like any task. It merges the
    delivered branches into `relay/<job>/merged` and runs the full gate once. Its delivery ends the job.
  - A failed gate never marks a task delivered, so its dependents stay `blocked`.
- **Return.** Trigger: a gap between two rounds above `wake_gap_s`, a restart, or any rejected task-record CAS.
  1. The node record turns `rejoining`. No task, snapshot or branch ref is pushed until a listing succeeds.
  2. For each task this machine believes it owns, it checks the table. When owner and epoch are unchanged, it
     keeps working. A task this machine handed over (`handed_over`) and that nobody took yet goes back to
     `working` with the same owner, epoch and branch; only the owner may resume it. When a one-shot
     `relay leave` handed the task over while `relay run` is online, the run process leaves as well.
  3. Otherwise the task was taken. The program stops that sub-fleet (`stop`), sends `relay.lost`, snapshots
     the whole local work tree to `salvage/<task>/<machine>-e<epoch>` without judging it, and never writes that
     task or its new branch again. The local work tree is kept.
  4. The new owner sees the salvage ref, writes `notes/SALVAGE.md` and sends `relay.salvage`, and its supervisor
     merges what is useful.
  5. A returning machine claims nothing until it has been online for `settle_s`. Each new wake gap restarts
     that wait, so a lid that wakes for seconds and sleeps again never claims work.
  6. When settled, the node turns `online` with its real `idle` value.
  - The coordinator's holder sends `relay.peer` when another machine is lost (`<node> is lost`), says goodbye
    (`<node> is gone (goodbye)`) or comes back online; a machine that comes back idle is reported as
    `<node> is online, idle`.
  - A machine that is awake but offline keeps working locally. After it reconnects, its first CAS is rejected
    and the same flow runs, so its offline work ends up in the salvage ref.
- **Coordinator role.** A light record `coordinator` with no toolchain needs, placed on an always-on machine
  first. Its holder's supervisor receives `relay.peer` and `relay.unplaceable` and decides on changes to the
  split.
  - It is taken over like a task, so the job never depends on one machine.
  - Placement, takeover and dependencies never go through it.
  - The role is optional. It is on by default and turned off with `relay.coordinator.enabled: false`; then no
    `coordinator` record is created, no machine sends `relay.peer`, and each machine sends `relay.unplaceable` to
    its own supervisor.
  - Settings: `relay.coordinator.enabled` (default `true`), `prefer` (default `""` = always-on machines first),
    `takeover` (default `true`). Fixing it to one machine = `prefer` set and `takeover: false`.
- **Notices.** `relay.take`, `relay.lost`, `relay.salvage`, `relay.peer` and `relay.unplaceable` go to the
  local root supervisor through the existing applet notice channel. Each is sent once per key, also across
  restarts. Two more notices exist:
  - `relay.snapshot`: a snapshot left out files matching `relay.secret_globs`.
  - `relay.leave`: a leave step failed.

  A supervisor talks to the program only through `fleetmux relay ...` commands.
- **The run process** (`cli.py`, `run`).
  - Under the applet, each loop pass ticks the applet heartbeat through `relay_applet.loop_heartbeat()`. The SDK
    is built without its heartbeat thread, so a hung pass stops beating.
  - Hot values live in `fx:relay:params`. They are seeded at start, read on every use, and written back every
    `write_back_seconds` and on a clean stop.
  - Exit codes:
    - 0 after SIGTERM or SIGINT, with tasks handed over and reason `shutdown`.
    - 75 (restart) when every joined job's hub stayed unreachable for `lost_after_s`. With no joined job,
      `relay run` does not exit: it stays up and polls for a job.
    - 78 (held) under the applet when the configuration or the inventory name is unusable, with one notice.
      `relay run` typed by hand outside a running root fleet has no way to start sub-fleets: it is refused and
      exits 69, and the error says to set `relay.enabled: true` and let the root fleet start it as the built-in
      relay applet.
    - 1 on a refusal or a bug.
  - Hub backoff: `poll_s`, doubling, capped at `max(poll_s, beat_s)`.
  - A restart that cannot list its running sub-fleets stays `rejoining`, so it never starts a duplicate.
- **Inventory fields** (section 56; the relay reads them).
  - `fleetmux superpowers refresh` writes `machines.<self>.hardware` (`cores`, `perf_cores`, `mem_gb`,
    `disk_free_gb`; sizes in GiB), `always_on` (default: false with a battery, true otherwise; a stored value is
    never overwritten) and `toolchains` (`{name: version}`, found on `discover.search_path()`, recorded only, never
    installed).
  - The toolchain probes are a fixed table in `fleetmux/fleet_superpowers_capability.py` (`TOOLCHAINS`: `rust`,
    `node`, `python`, `go`). Code: `hardware_of`, `has_battery`, `toolchains_of`, `write`, `machine_facts` (the
    flat node-record shape).
  - Node records carry the same values flat in `machine`, with `disk_free_gb` measured again on every beat.
  - On join and on every heartbeat round, each machine copies `hardware`, `always_on` and `toolchains` from
    the other machines' node records into its own inventory (`machines.<peer>`). It writes through the
    inventory's own write path (`inventory.sync_peers` via `fleet_superpowers.import_copy`) and only when a peer
    changed. A copy keeps its other fields, and the machine's own entry is never written.
- **Sleep and the time budget.**
  - A machine that just woke does not mark its applets as crashed. The applet host's wake detection
    (`runtime_v2.daemon_gap`) already covers this, and a test keeps the built-in relay applet running across
    repeated sleeps.
  - The task time budget is counted in awake time (section 35): the time the machine sleeps does not use the
    `hours` budget. The deadline is `fleet.task_deadline`, set by `Fleet.submit` with `awake.deadline`, and
    checked by the budget-exhausted checks of `Fleet.tick` and `RuntimeV2.tick`. The relay adds no budget logic
    of its own. `relay.hot.budget_pause_gap_s` is validated, but no code reads it.
- **Failure table.**

  | Case | Result |
  |---|---|
  | Hub unreachable for everyone | No claims, no deliveries, nobody judged lost; work goes on locally and is pushed later |
  | One machine offline | Its tasks are taken after `lost_after_s`; its extra work goes to a salvage ref when it returns |
  | Two machines claim one task | Exactly one CAS wins |
  | The taker leaves too | Taken again, epoch + 1, from its last checkpoint |
  | Snapshot push times out | Retried next period; the record keeps the last good checkpoint |
  | Table unreadable after a return | The machine stays `rejoining` and writes no task ref |
  | No eligible machine | The task stays `pending`; `relay.unplaceable` names what is missing |
  | Relay program crashes | The daemon restarts it; it starts with the return flow |
  | Gate fails | The task is not delivered; its dependents stay `blocked` |

- **Tests** (`tests/test_relay_*.py`, helper `tests/relay_kit.py`).
  - The hub is a local bare repository, and three temporary directories stand in for three machines.
  - A fake clock and a fake executor are used. The tests use no network, ssh or real fleet, and never touch the
    real data directory.
  - Concurrent claims are released at the same moment by a server-side hook and repeated.
  - `test_relay_guard.py` holds the package rules.
    - The flow tests check that no module calls a model or starts an agent, and that the package deletes
      nothing. Each guard is also run against a planted violation. No module may import
      `fleetmux.relay.needs`; `toolchain` and `placement` are the only parsers of `needs`.
    - The `test_port_*` tests check that the git-layer modules exist, that there is one hub only, that package
      text is ASCII, and that tests delete only their own temp files.
  - Cross-machine tests run against the real local bare repository. An in-memory store is used only in unit
    tests.
  - Git layer:
    - `test_relay_refs.py`: the git runner, the ref layout, ls-remote and the CAS push.
    - `test_relay_records.py`: `task.json`, `node.json` and every task-record change.
    - `test_relay_hub.py`: the cache repository and the one state remote.
    - `test_relay_params.py`: the `relay` block, overlay, validation, hot store and write-back.
  - Snapshots and takeover:
    - `test_relay_snapshot.py`: temp-index snapshots and salvage snapshots.
    - `test_relay_materialize.py`: byte-identical work trees and checkpoints.
    - `test_relay_progress.py`: progress headings and summary.
    - `test_relay_takeover.py`: `TAKEOVER.md`.
  - Placement and liveness:
    - `test_relay_assign.py`: capability score, order and the candidate filter hook.
    - `test_relay_beats.py`: node records per beat.
    - `test_relay_liveness.py`: liveness on the local clock and notices.
    - `test_relay_reclaim.py`: takeover of a machine that left or went silent.
  - `test_relay_three.py`: three machines on one hub at the state layer.
  - Core:
    - `test_relay_builtin_applet.py`: the built-in applet switch, hosting, restart and sleeps.
    - `test_relay_budget_pause.py`: skipped. Its skip reason names the awake clock as missing, but the task
      budget already runs on awake time (section 35).
    - `test_fleet_superpowers_capability.py`: the inventory fields.
  - Flow:
    - `test_relay_jobfile.py`: job and spec files, reserved names, cycles, machine paths.
    - `test_relay_deps.py`: dependency advance, `base` and `merge_in`.
    - `test_relay_placement.py`: toolchains, disk floors, caps, `prefer`, stagger.
    - `test_relay_coordinator.py`: the coordinator role.
    - `test_relay_launch.py`: spec rendering and the executor.
    - `test_relay_flow.py`: the main loop on an in-memory store.
    - `test_relay_flow_git.py`: the main loop on a bare hub with three machines.
    - `test_relay_flow_three_machines.py`: dependencies, merge, return and salvage across three machines.
    - `test_relay_cli.py`: the commands, `status`, `run`, exit codes, heartbeat and backoff.
    - `test_relay_backend.py`: the production backend on a bare hub, inventory copy, hot parameters.
    - `test_relay_inventory.py`: the inventory copy.

## 60. Restart state: snapshot before a restart or a supervisor change, reconcile after

A restart of a fleet (an operator `stop` and `start`, a crash or a reboot) and a new supervisor generation (any path: regeneration after a hold, a frozen session, a fresh session after refusals, a context handoff, a wall or profile switch) must not lose what the fleet was doing. The daemon records the fleet's state before such a change and, after it, compares that record with the fleet as it is, restores what can be restored as it was, and hands every item that stopped, got lost or errored to the supervisor, which rules on each one. Code: `fleetmux/restart_state.py`; configuration `fleet.yaml#restart_resume`.

The switch is `restart_resume.enabled` (default `true`). When it is `false`, no snapshot is written and no reconcile runs. Each key of the block is validated on its own; a bad value keeps its default.

- **Snapshot.** One JSON file per write under `<fleet home>/restart/<fleet key>/snap-<seq>-<reason>.json` (fleet key: `root`, `sub-<name>` for a named or attached sub-fleet, `job-<job id>`, `split-<root run>-<M n>`). It lives in the fleet home, not in the run directory, so a new run reads it. It is written atomically (temp file, fsync, rename, fsync of the directory), carries `schema` (a reader refuses another schema as unreadable, never guesses), the run, the host name, the supervisor generation, and one entry per kind. Kinds are an open table (`KINDS` and one `collect_<kind>` function each); a collector that fails leaves its kind out and names the error in `kind_errors`, which the reconcile reports as an item.

  | Kind | Content |
  |---|---|
  | `hot` | The run's roster copy (path, hash, rows), workers added at run time and what for (applet lease, job, attached sub-fleet), applet hot values (`hot_add_max`), a refused roster reload. |
  | `subfleets` | Every declared sub-fleet (roster and attached) and every running job or split child: form, entry, the attach options (`spec`) and a copy of the attached roster, the record (`state`, `outcome`, `detached`, `attention`), the parent hash (`crashloop`, `blocked`, applets), alive or not. |
  | `applets` | Each applet of this host: state, pid, restarts, open leases, escalations awaiting a ruling, notices. |
  | `jobs` | The rows of `fx:jobs` (section 58). |
  | `inflight` | Requests without a result (queued, dispatched to a worker, or being routed again), escalations and sub-fleet operations awaiting a ruling, messages for the supervisor not delivered yet (queued, offered to it and not committed, or committed `cancelled` because the generation it was offered to was retired before it answered, found through the bus audit `stop_resolved`; with their text; only the outcome `completed` counts as delivered; the ledger's own notices are left out), confirmations that wait for the user. |
  | `errors` | Held slots (with the wall-clock time of the hold), a pause that is not the operator's stop, notices for a human that left on no channel, and the error-level audit events since the last reconcile (not only since the previous snapshot: two timer snapshots between reconciles keep the whole backlog). The audit stream is read newest first, page by page, down to the last reconcile; at most 200, newest kept. |

  Text of one item is capped at `item_payload_max` characters (default 20000). A longer text is cut and ends with a `[cut N chars]` mark.

- **When.** (1) Every `snapshot_every_s` (default 300 s) from the `restart` part of `Fleet.tick`, frozen while the fleet stops. (2) The first statement of the explicit-stop tail of `RuntimeV2.run`, before applets and sub-fleets are taken down; it records how the run ended: `operator`, `final` (`stop --final`), `lifecycle` (the mode policy stopped it), `suspend` (a job sub-fleet suspended by its root) or `self` (the run had already ended by itself before the stop tail ran: a result.json of this daemon life says so; one an earlier life left behind does not count). (3) The first statement of `RuntimeV2.resume_run`, before anything is cancelled or replayed (Redis as the previous daemon left it). (4) `Fleet.spawn` of the supervisor for a new generation within a daemon's life (every generation path ends there). (5) Right after an attached sub-fleet is declared or detached (`named_subfleet.attach` / `detach`), so a crash before the next timer snapshot neither loses a new attach nor brings back a detached one. A snapshot write never raises: an error is audited as `restart.error` and the stop, resume or generation change goes on.
  The first tick of a daemon life writes no timer snapshot; the first timer snapshot comes `snapshot_every_s` later. Only a root fleet prunes the `job-*` and `split-*` directories, after its timer snapshot.
- **Kept.** The newest `keep_timer` (default 12) timer snapshots and `keep_events` (default 20) others; a snapshot a boot has not consumed yet is never removed. The `restart/job-*` and `split-*` directories of child fleets that no longer run and have no open ledger are kept to the newest `keep_child_dirs` (default 20).
- **Which runs come back.** Only a run the operator stopped (`stop`) or one that died (crash, reboot) is brought back. A run that ended by itself (its task completed, its budget ran out, it failed, the mode policy stopped it) leaves nothing to reconcile: the next start is quiet (audit `restart.quiet`). `stop --final` ends a fleet for good: its open jobs are cancelled (published, never resumed), every attached sub-fleet gets a cancel tombstone, and the next start restores nothing. `start --no-restore` reconciles without restoring: every item waits for the supervisor. The first start of a new fleet home is quiet; a start whose fleet had an earlier run (that was stopped or died) but has no readable snapshot reports that as an errored item, audits `restart.snapshot_unreadable` and pushes the opening summary, and the start goes on.
  A job sub-fleet that its root suspended (`suspend`) also starts quiet: its own restart state restores nothing, and the root's `jobs` kind and section 58 bring the job back.
- **Reconcile, phase one (no supervisor needed).** In the daemon's first tick (a new run or a continued one) the baseline is chosen: the stop snapshot of the previous daemon life; after a crash, this life's resume-entry snapshot or the newest snapshot of the previous life; unreadable files are skipped and named. It is consumed at once (a second boot never restores it again). The fleet now is collected with the same collectors and each baseline item is classed:
  - cancelled by the operator (a job's `cancel` or `cancelled` result, a sub-fleet's `detached` record or tombstone `<fleet home>/restart/root/cancels/subfleet-<name>.json`): not brought back, listed in the report. A tombstone written while the root was down also holds when the same run is continued: the entry of `resume_run` takes that sub-fleet's declaration out (it drains like a detach);
  - a roster sub-fleet whose row the operator removed while the root was down also counts as cancelled (`removed from the roster`): it is not listed as lost and needs no ruling. A restore and a `redo` declare only attached sub-fleets again, so a former roster sub-fleet is never declared as an attached one;
  - fine or finished (running now, or a result exists): not listed;
  - stopped (it ran, its record is here, it does not run now);
  - lost (no trace now and no result);
  - errored (an error kind entry, an error state, a failed restore, a collector error).
  An error a closed ledger already ruled is not listed again unless it happened again after the ruling (by its timestamp). The snapshot never writes old values over the fleet's current state: an item only describes, and a restore re-checks the current state right before it acts.
- **Restores (program).** An attached service sub-fleet that is not declared in the new run is declared again through the ordinary `subfleet.attach` operation, with the options it was attached with; the autostart tick stays the only launcher, so its name, liveness and slot checks apply as for any attach. The source roster and roles named at attach time are used when they still exist; otherwise the previous run's copy, and only then the snapshot's copy; the ledger item names which. A child whose previous daemon still holds its lock is not started again (the item says why). Join stubs of the fleet home that name the child follow the new root, and the same `start` run again gets the restored child's address and keeps its one stub. Applets come back with their host (section 54). Jobs follow section 58. An applet `hot_add_max` changed at run time is set again when its roster row is unchanged. Nothing that could run twice is replayed by the program: requests, deliveries, confirmations and workers added for ended leases wait for the supervisor.
- **Ledger.** The items go into the open ledger `<fleet home>/restart/<fleet>/ledgers/L<n>.json` (with a Markdown view next to it); an item still open from an earlier restart or generation is updated, never doubled. A new ledger with items pushes an opening summary on the notify channels (`lifecycle_close.notify`, event `reconcile_opened`: how many were restored automatically, how many wait for a ruling).
- **Phase two (the supervisor).** When a supervisor generation says READY, the next tick reconciles that generation against the snapshot taken before it started (a later generation of the same daemon life; every generation, whatever its cause) and then hands the open items to it as one message through the daemon's queue (`Fleet.queue`, never its input box), at most `inline_items` (default 30) inline and the rest in the ledger's Markdown file. An empty result sends nothing. Open items are sent again to every new generation and every `ruling_remind_s` (default 3600 s); nothing is dropped for want of a ruling.
- **Rulings.** The decision JSON key `reconcile: [{item, verdict, reason}]` in both the service and the task decision (a RUNNING reply with only `reconcile` may dispatch nothing), or `fleetmux reconcile rule R<n> --verdict ... --reason ...` (event `reconcile`); `fleetmux reconcile show` prints the ledger. `recovered` confirms; `drop` needs a reason (dropping a restored sub-fleet takes it down again and writes its tombstone); `redo` runs the program's action where there is one (declare the sub-fleet again; submit a job again as a new job with the spec it was submitted with: goal, hours, workers, artifacts, budget, lifecycle, its sub-fleet fields, and the model chains and cards the job spec itself set (a fleet.yaml or discovery layer is read again); route a lost request again) exactly once per item, and otherwise leaves the item `redo_pending` for the supervisor to do with the usual commands and rule `recovered`. Each ruling is written to the ledger at once. When every item is done the ledger closes: the report `<fleet home>/restart/<fleet>/reports/reconcile-L<n>.md` (and `.json`) is written and a closing summary is pushed (`reconcile_closed`: recovered, redone, dropped with their reasons, and what the operator had cancelled).
- **Continuing a run with a new supervisor.** A root service run with open jobs is continued (section 58). Its root supervisor is never reattached on resume but started as a new generation from the run's config, so `start` writes a changed `--supervisor-model`, `--supervisor-pi-model`, `--supervisor-pi-thinking` or `--supervisor-profile` into the config before the daemon resumes (`continue_supervisor`), but only the option the run's supervisor CLI reads (claude: `--supervisor-model`; codex: `--supervisor-profile`; pi: `--supervisor-pi-model` / `--supervisor-pi-thinking`, also written to the supervisor's `pi_models` / `pi_thinkings` entry); any other is not applied and listed. An applied change is recorded with an id; the entry of `resume_run` drops the supervisor's launch record once for that id (a usage-wall, fallback or profile card of the old generation would otherwise override the config), so the next generation launches from the changed config. Another supervisor CLI cannot apply to a running fleet (its delivery channel is fixed): the printed line and the ledger item say that `stop --final` then `start` changes it but cancels the open jobs, so to keep them the operator waits until they are done; that option, and every other start option a continued run does not apply, is printed by `start` and listed in the ledger as an errored item. A continued run also takes the interpreter of the release that resumes it: `start --resume` writes that release's interpreter (`releases.python()`: the release's own `.venv`, else the interpreter of the resuming process) into the run config and into every sub-fleet config whose daemon is not running, and prints which sub-fleets still run on the old one; the daemon, every actor started after the resume and the hook lines it writes, the history pane and every relaunched sub-fleet then run on it. A sub-fleet whose daemon still runs switches at its own next resume, and a reattached session keeps its interpreter until its next generation. A new run is unchanged: `start` writes the interpreter of the starting process.
- **Same behaviour everywhere.** Every daemon runs this code for its own fleet key: a root and a named or attached sub-fleet the same way; a job or split child too, while the parent's `jobs` kind stays the authority on whether a job runs. Paths come from the fleet home only; the host name is recorded and a liveness check (a lock, a pid) is only trusted on the machine that made it.
- **Known boundaries.** A crash keeps only what the last timer snapshot (at most `snapshot_every_s` old) and the continuous records (`jobs.json`, the attached declarations in the snapshot) hold. A new run starts with an empty Redis: the previous host's applet leases and escalations can only be listed, not restored. A restore re-attaches only stubs in the root's own fleet home. Job sub-fleets that section 58 publishes `not_carried` start again from the beginning when the supervisor rules `redo`.
- **Verification.** `tests/test_restart_snapshot.py` (write points, atomic write, schema, retention, a broken or missing snapshot, quiet starts, hooks that swallow their errors), `tests/test_restart_scenario1_attached.py` (attached sub-fleet and its applet come back with no supervisor READY, the stub follows, the same start again, source roster first), `tests/test_restart_scenario2_jobs.py` (jobs in the ledger, a not-carried job redone once, a restart while the job slot rotates, the model chain on resume), `tests/test_restart_scenario3_backlog.py` (the backlog reaches the next generation and a new run; empty results send nothing; ruled errors are not repeated), `tests/test_restart_cancel.py`, `tests/test_restart_never_twice.py`, `tests/test_restart_idempotent.py`, `tests/test_restart_ledger.py`, `tests/test_restart_continue_supervisor.py`. `tests/test_resume_interpreter.py`: a run built by one release and resumed by another runs on the resuming release's interpreter.

## 61. `fleetmux demo` and the `acceptance.verdict` event

### 61.1 `fleetmux demo`

`fleetmux demo` shows the program acceptance without a model and without a login. The agents are scripted. Every
other part is the product code.

- **Isolation.** The demo creates one new directory, `/tmp/fmxd-<random>`, mode 0700. It holds HOME,
  `FLEETMUX_HOME`, `FLEETMUX_STATE`, the Redis runtime root, the runs directory and the actor launcher. The short
  path keeps the Unix sockets of Redis and the terminal server within their length limit. The demo does not pass on
  the caller's `FLEETMUX_*`, `CLAUDE*`, `ANTHROPIC_*`, `OPENAI_*`, `CODEX_*`, `CMUX_*`, `TMUX*` or `WEZTERM_*`
  variables. It starts with `--no-join`, so it never joins a root fleet that runs on the machine. It sets
  `FLEETMUX_DISCOVERY=off`, so first-run discovery does not probe the machine, and `FLEETMUX_BIN` to the `fleetmux`
  program it found. The orphan Redis
  sweep of `start` is limited to the demo's Redis root (section 61.3).
- **The run.** `start --allow-development-v2 --mode task --lifecycle smoke --backend fleetmux --no-view --no-join
  --workers 2 --supervisor-cli claude --worker-cli claude`; the options `--claude`, `--redis-runtime-dir`,
  `--fleet-home`, `--cmux-state-dir` and `--runs-dir` point inside the demo directory. One supervisor and two
  workers. Then `task` with two declared artifacts: a function file and its test
  file in `worker-1/`.
- **Scripted actors.** `fleetmux/demo_actor.py` stands in for the Claude Code CLI. It keeps the contract the fleet
  relies on: the Stop and asyncRewake hooks from `--settings`, the transcript under the demo's HOME, and the ruled
  editor that `claude_input` reads. It never calls a model. The launcher `bin/claude` is a shell script that only
  `--claude` names; it is not on PATH. `--version` prints the fixed string `demo_actor.VERSION`, which contains the
  word `scripted`; its version number equals the `claude` minimum in deps.yaml and must be raised with it. Every
  pane shows the banner `demo_actor.BANNER`: `scripted agents, no model calls`, a dash, and `the acceptance is real
  fleetmux code` (the code uses a non-ASCII dash in this line). Every reply carries
  the word `scripted`, except the `READY` replies of the startup handshake, which must be exactly `READY`.
- **Liveness marker.** `RuntimeV2.cli_check` restarts an official-CLI supervisor when its periodic check finds no
  process named `claude` or `codex` on the supervisor's pane twice in a row. The actor is a Python process. While it
  runs, it therefore runs `bin/live/claude`, a symbolic link to the host's `sleep`, on its own pane and in its own
  process group. Closing the pane ends the marker. No fleet code or default changes for this. While the demo runs,
  each pane therefore has a process named `claude` that is in fact `sleep`; it only satisfies the CLI liveness
  check and takes no part in the acceptance.
- **The acceptance path.** With the decomposition tree on (section 62), the scripted supervisor first hands in a
  tree of one block, `M0`, and dispatches `M0` once the program accepts the tree. It then claims `COMPLETED` after the worker's
  first report, although the test file is missing. `Fleet.on_supervisor` runs `check_artifacts` on the declared
  artifacts and rejects the claim: the task stays `running`, the bounce counter grows, and the supervisor receives
  the checker's text. The supervisor passes the text on to the worker. The worker writes the file, the second claim
  passes, and the task completes. The `smoke` lifecycle then stops and closes the run. The demo itself judges
  nothing.
- **The summary.** The demo follows the run's audit stream with `control_view.iter_events`, the reader of
  `fleetmux logs`, and prints every `acceptance.verdict` record: time, result, reason, and the id of the audit
  record. Without a view it also prints each step from the same stream.
- **Views.** On a desktop with `fleetmux-web` and not over ssh: a window (`fleetmux gui`), in its own session.
  On macOS a background thread raises the window as `start` does; the demo does not wait for it, and a failure
  leaves the window behind.
  Otherwise, at a terminal: `fleetmux attach` in the foreground; the attach client exits when the run's server
  shuts down, and detaching ends the demo. `--no-view`: no view.
- **Dependencies.** `redis-server`, `tmux`, `git` and `fleetmux` from deps.yaml, found the way `start` finds
  programs. A missing or too old program is listed in the rows of `scripts/check_deps.py`, and the demo exits
  without starting anything. Nothing is installed.
- **Clean-up.** It runs on the normal end, on an error, and on Ctrl-C or SIGTERM; a second signal does not cut it
  short. It finds every target inside the demo directory: the run config (the daemon is stopped through `stop`),
  each terminal server runtime (`server.shutdown` with `force` through that server's control socket, else
  `fleetmux reap` of that runtime), and each Redis socket (`SHUTDOWN NOSAVE`). It first waits for the product's
  detached closer (`lifecycle-close`). It signals no process by name and does not touch any tmux server. Then it
  removes the directory. `--keep` keeps the directory and the followed audit stream as `audit.jsonl`.
- **For tests and recordings.** `FLEETMUX_DEMO_TURN_S` sets the length of a scripted turn; the demo's time limit grows
  with it. `scripts/record_demo.sh` records the demo (docs/images/README-demo-recording.md).
- **Tests.** `tests/test_demo_unit.py`, `tests/test_demo_e2e.py` (a clean machine, one rejected and one accepted
  claim, the summary against the audit stream, Ctrl-C inside `start` and in the middle), `tests/test_demo_liveness.py`
  (a slow run across several liveness checks keeps its first supervisor session), `tests/test_demo_recording.py`.

### 61.2 The `acceptance.verdict` audit event

One event at every point where a supervisor reports a task done and the program judges the claim. It is
bookkeeping only: it is written after the judgement and changes no judgement, status or return value. The existing
events (`lifecycle.gate`, `lifecycle.status`, `complete.refused`, `complete.held_for_jobs`, `task.*`) stay.

Fields: `mode` (`task_artifacts` or `dev_task`), `task_id`, `ok`, `problem` (the first line of the
reason, cut to 500 characters; empty when `ok`), `claimed_by` (the slot that reported done; a COMPLETED decision comes only from the
`supervisor` slot). `task_artifacts` adds `bounce`, the value of the bounce counter.

Write points:

1. `task_artifacts`: `Fleet.on_supervisor`, the `COMPLETED` branch, after `check_artifacts` and the contract gate.
   It is written when the claim passes (also when the task is then held for open jobs) and when it is rejected
   (after the bounce counter grows, including the rejection that blocks the task).
2. `dev_task`: `lifecycle_close.completion_problem`, next to `lifecycle.gate`. The gate result is cached per task,
   so there is one event per task.

Not written: the refusal of a COMPLETED claim while workers still have unsettled deliveries (`complete.refused`),
and the manual gate re-run (`lifecycle_recheck`).

In `dev_task`, one COMPLETED claim writes two events: first `task_artifacts` (the declared
artifacts), then the lifecycle mode's own check.

### 61.3 `FLEETMUX_ORPHAN_SWEEP_ROOT`

`start` and `start --resume` call `sweep_orphan_redis`, which shuts down the private Redis instances of this user
whose run was abandoned (`reap_orphan_redis`). When `FLEETMUX_ORPHAN_SWEEP_ROOT` is set, the sweep looks only at Redis sockets under that
directory. Only `fleetmux demo` sets it, to its own Redis runtime root, so a demo never touches another fleet's Redis.
When it is not set, the sweep is unchanged. A sweep error is printed as `orphan Redis sweep skipped` and does not
block the start.

## 62. Decomposition tree: the supervisor hands in a tree, dispatches from it, the program keeps the accounts

Requirement: the supervisor splits a task on paper before any work goes out, so that blocks that share an open decision or a file are found before two workers collide; the program, not the supervisor's feeling, decides when splitting further is worth it (the time the whole task saves against the extra session minutes it costs); a wrong cut goes back to the tree instead of to a worker; at the end the program shows how the decomposition went and the supervisor may tune the numbers for the next task. The single daemon, slot/generation identity, the one-page layout, the task/service protocol and `fx:items` (all eight writers, all delete paths) are unchanged; the tree lives beside them. Code: `fleetmux/decompose.py` (pure functions plus the parameter keys) and the `tree_*` / `decompose_*` methods of `Fleet`.

- **Switch**: `fleet.yaml#decompose.enabled`. The switch is False in the code defaults (`decompose.DEFAULTS`), and a bad block reads as off. The shipped `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml` sets `enabled: true`. A `decompose` block in the private fleet.yaml replaces the shipped block as a whole; keys it leaves out take the code defaults in `decompose.DEFAULTS`, where `enabled` is false and `split.margin` and every cost except `spawn` are null (a null cost counts 0). So with the shipped configuration the tree is on. The write-back writes the full block, so a block the program wrote keeps every key. It is read once per task at `submit` and stored on the task hash (`decompose=1`); a task submitted while it is off runs the old path to its end: `fx:items` dispatch, the dispatch-item `leaf` of section 44, the section 44 long-running nudge at `check_minutes`, contracts as in section 18, prompts and audits identical to the baseline. Off is the baseline, not a degraded mode. The test suite runs with the switch off (`tests/conftest.py` `decompose_default_off`, autouse) and turns it on per test (`decompose_on`).
- **Scope**: task and dev_task root fleets, job sub-fleets (section 58), split sub-fleets (section 20, section 44, the service split and `supervisor_split`) and task-mode named sub-fleets (section 29) all go through `submit` and get a tree. A service fleet keeps no tree and its decisions take no new key (`parse_service_decision` and the pi service schema are unchanged); a service split hands its pieces to the child's tree as a seed (below) and is not put to the account.
- **One level per fleet**: a tree has this fleet's root (`root` in a top fleet, the task id inside a sub-fleet) and one level of blocks with ids of this level (`M<n>`; inside sub-fleet `M1`: `M1.<n>`; inside a named sub-fleet `<name>.<n>`). A block worth splitting further is still one block here: its worker asks to split it, and the sub-fleet's supervisor splits that level. Deeper nodes are written only by the program (an approved split's pieces, a returned sub-fleet's blocks).
- **State**: Redis `fx:tree` (one JSON string) is the truth; every save copies it to `<run>/tree.json` and `<workspace>/tree.json` (tmp + rename; the tick re-syncs), and each accepted change appends one line to `<run>/tree.log.jsonl`. A daemon start whose task runs and whose Redis lost `fx:tree` (no AOF on transport v1) restores it from `<run>/tree.json` (`tree.restored`). `fx:tree`, `fx:tree:summary`, `fx:tree:notes` and `fx:decompose:tuned` are cleared when a new task is submitted. The tree: `rev`, `accepted` (the rev of the first tree the program took; 0 before), `root`, `goal`, `decisions[{id, q, owner, status: open|decided|pending_user, answer}]`, `nodes[]`, `splits[]`, `chain{ids, minutes}`, `width`, `review`. A node: the supervisor writes `id parent goal cut owns decides consumes after depends_on serial_reason est{minutes, sessions} accept claim{leaf, reason} split_from merge_of`; the program writes `leaf leaf_reason critical bound state (planned|running|delegated|reported) to actual{minutes, session_minutes, returns, causes, started, ended, dispatches, overrun_noted, outcome}` and refuses them in a reply. `cut` is one of `function`, `decision` or `serial`. A tree holds at most 200 nodes. The `goal` and `accept` text of a node holds at most 2000 characters.
- **First reply hands in the tree**: decision keys `tree` (nodes by id, added or changed field by field, and `{"decision": {...}}` entries) and `tree_drop` (ids; refused for the root, a block with children, a running or delegated block). Until the first tree is accepted, a RUNNING reply must carry a non-empty `tree` and an empty `dispatch` (`decompose is on: your first reply hands in the tree only ...`); this replaces "RUNNING must dispatch" for that reply, and a seeded tree (a task spec `tree` file, a sub-fleet seed) needs the same confirming reply. BLOCKED and COMPLETED are not held to it. A later reply may change only the tree; that counts as the reply's progress.
- **Shape checks** (all errors in one refusal, through the usual correction path): ids unique and of this level; parent is the root; parallel siblings never own the same path (`owns` compared after `normpath`, a directory prefix counts) unless `after` / `depends_on` orders them; every decision has one owner (`supervisor` or the block whose `decides` holds it) and appears in one `decides` only; `consumes` points at existing decisions; `pending_user` and `decided` carry an `answer`; `depends_on` carries `serial_reason` (`integration` / `exploratory`); no cycle over `after`, `depends_on` and open consumed decisions; a dispatchable block has `est` and `accept`; a block below `split.floor` minutes with a sibling is refused ("merge it into a sibling"); `merge_of` blocks are dropped in the same reply and their `owns` are kept; `split_from` blocks drop the block they split.
- **The accounts**: over this level, with unlimited workers, the program computes earliest start, the longest chain (`chain`), each block's slack, and the maximum parallel width (`width`). For a split of block X: dT = how many minutes the whole task finishes earlier (0 when X is off the longest chain), dC = extra session minutes: in this fleet `cost.cold_start x (n - 1) + cost.per_decision x new decisions + cost.merge`; into a sub-fleet `cost.spawn + cost.cold_start x n + cost.supervisor x the sub-chain + cost.per_decision x new decisions + cost.merge`. A split pays only when dT > 0, dT >= `split.k` x dC and dT >= `split.margin`. On every change the program re-judges `leaf` per block: a block with children is not a leaf; a block that has reported is a leaf; off the chain, a leaf; on the chain, the best halving's bounds (`bound{dT, dC}`); when even that does not pay, a leaf; otherwise the supervisor's `claim.leaf` decides, without one the block is `leaf=false`. `leaf` never blocks a dispatch.
- **Dispatch from the tree**: every dispatch item needs the `id` of a block of this level without children (`dispatch id M7 is not in the tree: add it to the tree first ...`); a delegated block, a block whose `after` / `depends_on` has not reported, or one that consumes an open decision is refused with the reason; the blocks out at once (this dispatch plus the running ones; a correction of a reported block starts nothing) may not exceed `width`. The program appends `[tree M0] owns ...; uses D1 = ...; accepted by: ...` to the instruction, writes `fx:items` exactly as before, marks the block running and counts its session minutes from each dispatch to its report. The dispatch audit gains `items[{id, to, parent, est_minutes, est_sessions, critical, leaf}]` (switch on only). With contracts on (section 18) the contract gates stay; a contract item's `edits` / `depends_on` / `serial_reason` default from the tree block and must not differ from it.
- **Accepted line and opening summary**: after an accepted change the supervisor gets `[tree] rev <n> accepted: longest chain ... (<m> min), max parallel width <w>; dispatchable now: ...`, plus the `leaf=false` blocks with their dT / dC bounds ("split it in the tree or dispatch it as is"); it is appended to the supervisor's next message (`fx:tree:notes`), and sent alone only when nothing else would wake the supervisor. The first accepted tree also queues an opening summary for the user (first-level blocks, the chain, the width, every decision the supervisor took for the user, `pending_user` marked): the tick sends it once, outside any v2 transaction, through the routes of a held slot's notice (a managed root: `lifecycle_close.notify`, kind `decompose_summary`; an unmanaged root: `notify_route.unmanaged_send`); a sub-fleet writes `subfleet-notices/tree-*.json` that its parents pass up (`relay_notices`). It does not wait for an answer.
- **Split errors go back to the tree**: with the switch on no worker gets the section 44 long-running nudge (`split_due` returns nothing). Instead the tick checks running blocks on the planned longest chain: past `est x overrun_ratio` minutes, the supervisor gets one `[overrun] M0 is on the longest chain and has run ...` note per block with four ways out: re-estimate, merge with a block it waits on, extract a shared decision, split in the tree. A `blocked:` ruling must carry `escalations[].cause` (`shared_decision` / `file_overlap` / `missing_dep` / `scope` / `other`; optional on a split answer); each cause counts one return of the block, and at exactly `returns_alert` returns the supervisor's next message gets one line with the block's causes. Any ruling on a running block, with or without a cause, restarts its session-minute clock. A report that is Blocked or asks to split does not settle its block.
- **Split, merge, extract**: in the tree, `split_from: {id, dT, dC}` on the new pieces (the program recomputes both numbers and refuses a split that does not pay: `split of M3 refused: dT ... < k x dC ... (the program's account; yours: ...)`), `merge_of: [...]` on the merged block, extract = a new decision plus changed `decides` / `consumes`. A worker's split request (section 20) needs `delta_t` and `delta_c` while the switch is on (task mode; a service split request does not); the supervisor sees both accounts (`Accounts: worker dT 20 / dC 3; program dT <= 3.2, dC 23 (sub-fleet, k=2): does not pay`), and an approve the program's account refuses is handled like a limit (no strike, the request stays open). An approved split writes the pieces under the block (`delegated`) and seeds the sub-fleet's tree with the block as its root and the pieces as its first-level blocks (`<run>/tree-seeds/<task>-<hex>.json`, spec key `tree`; a job spec may carry its own `tree` file, renumbered into the child's level). When that sub-fleet cannot start (spawn refused, the request back to the supervisor, a job split dropped) or returns failed, the pieces go again and the block becomes dispatchable (back to its state before the approve, or `planned` after a failed sub-fleet); the split stays in `splits` with `rolled_back`. A returned sub-fleet fills in the block and its pieces from the child's `tree.json` (states, minutes, session minutes).
- **Review**: `tree.json`'s `review` is recomputed on every save and the final one goes into `result.json` (`decompose: {rev, chain, width, review, tune}`). Three tables, numbers only, no verdict, never a gate: `returns` (per block and per cause class); `parallel` (`wall_minutes` from the first start to the last end, `chain_minutes` of the longest chain over the actual durations, `fill` = wall / chain, 1 meaning the chain alone set the pace, and `work_minutes` beside them); `splits` (each split's claimed and program accounts, `est_minutes`, `actual_minutes`, `saved_minutes` = estimate minus actual, `session_minutes`, `extra_session_minutes` = actual session minutes minus the estimate it replaced; a rolled-back split saved nothing and cost what its sub-fleet spent).
- **Tuning**: the closing COMPLETED may carry `decompose_tune: [{param, value, reason}]` (param one of `split.k split.margin split.floor cost.cold_start cost.per_decision cost.merge cost.supervisor cost.spawn overrun_ratio returns_alert`; on any other status the reply is refused). It applies only when the COMPLETED passes verification (finish, or a job-held COMPLETED); a bounced COMPLETED changes nothing. It goes into the Redis parameters (marked dirty, `fx:decompose:tuned` records it with its origin). A sub-fleet's applied tune is in its `result.json`; the parent takes it at collect (`decompose.tune_forwarded`), task or service parent alike, and passes it up in its own result, so it reaches the root. Only a root fleet (a service root too) writes the block back to the private `fleet.yaml`, every `write_back_seconds` and on exit; the next task reads it.
- **The submitter's tree (the seed)**: a task or job spec may carry `tree` (the absolute path of a tree file: the submitter's first-level division) and `by` (who wrote it; default the file's top-level `by`, else `submitter`; at most 80 characters). The program keeps that division as written in `tree['seed']` (`by`, `at` = submit time, `root`, `blocks[{id, goal, est, owns, after, depends_on, consumes, decides}]`, `decisions[{id, owner, status}]`); nothing writes it afterwards, so it survives the supervisor's changes, splits, merges, restarts and resumes. A file without blocks (the program's seed of a job that brought no tree) is no seed. A split's seed names the worker who proposed the pieces as `by`; a job's seed carries the job's `by`. The review's `seed` section compares the seed with the tree as it ran: each seed block `kept`, `changed` (which of est / owns / after / depends_on / consumes / decides), `merged` (`into` the block whose `merge_of` names it), `split` (`pieces`: the supervisor's `split_from` pieces or the program's pieces under it) or `dropped`; the blocks the supervisor added; each block's actual minutes / seed estimate (`ratio`); the longest chain of the seed, of the tree's estimates (`plan`) and over the actual durations. Numbers only, no verdict; null without a seed.
- **Check before submit**: `fleetmux tree check <file>` (`fleetmux/tree_cli.py`; `--config` lays a private fleet.yaml's `decompose` block over the shipped one, `--prefix` checks a sub-fleet level, `--json`) runs the same code a handed-in tree goes through (`seed_from`, `decompose.apply` with its shape checks and message, `judge`) and prints the shape errors, the longest chain, the maximum parallel width and the blocks the program would mark leaf=false with their dT / dC bounds. Exit 1 on shape errors, 2 on a bad command line or parameter block, else 0. Nothing is started or contacted.
- **Development work brings its tree**: with the switch on, a task of a root dev_task fleet and a job with `lifecycle: dev_task` are refused at submit when the spec has no `tree`; the message names the three steps (write the tree file, run `fleetmux tree check`, give `tree` in the spec). Other lifecycles are not held to it. Only new submissions are checked: a job taken before the switch went on, a restarted or resumed run and a sub-fleet the program starts (a job's, a split's) are never refused. The ACP entry takes `tree` and `by` as job keys, and a join stub's `task` passes its spec whole, so a dev_task fleet started next to a running root brings its tree the same way. Text an operator types into an idle dev_task root becomes a task without a tree and is refused like any other.
- **The submitter gets the review back**: a job's one result (`fleetmux result <job>`, and the `<run>/job-results/<job>.json` copy a join stub reads) carries `result.decompose`, the sub-fleet's result.json `decompose` block (`rev`, `chain`, `width`, `review` with its `seed` section, `tune`).
- **Parameters in Redis**: daemon start and resume copy the merged `decompose` block into `fx:decompose:params` (a value changed and not yet written back survives a resume), with `fx:decompose:meta` (`write_back_seconds, loaded_at, flushed_at, dirty`), as `fx:superpowers:*` does (contracts section 9). The write-back replaces only the `decompose` block of the private file (other blocks and comments stay), under the private file's lock (`<file>.lock`, 10 seconds), which the relay's write-back (section 59) takes too. A missing private file is created (0600). A block that fails validation is logged (`tick.error`, part `decompose_load`). The switch then reads as off, so a new task runs the old path. A fleet that is already running uses the code defaults in `decompose.DEFAULTS`, not the shipped values.
- **Prompts**: while the switch is on, `role_scope` replaces the "Leaf blocks" section and the `dispatch[].id` / `dispatch[].leaf` format lines of `supervisor.md` / `supervisor_v2.md` with `agents/decompose_supervisor.md` (the cut order, the two accounts, the closure condition, the opening summary, the new keys, what to do on `[overrun]`, reading `review` before COMPLETED); `contracts_supervisor.md`'s leaf line goes; the worker split JSON of `worker.md` / `contracts_worker.md` gains `delta_t` / `delta_c`. Off, the shipped files are used verbatim. The pi `fleet_decision` task schema takes `tree`, `tree_drop`, `escalations[].cause` and `decompose_tune` with the same enums and nulls as the Python checks.
- **Audit**: `tree.accepted {rev, nodes, chain, chain_minutes, width, first}`, `tree.summary {rev, status}`, `tree.summary_relayed`, `tree.overrun {task, minutes, est, ratio}`, `tree.returns_alert {task, causes}`, `tree.split {of, pieces, dT, dC}`, `tree.split_rolled_back {of, pieces, reason}`, `tree.restored`, `tree.seed_unreadable`, `tree.collect_failed`, `decompose.tune`, `decompose.tune_forwarded`, `decompose.tune_failed`, `decompose.flushed`, `decompose.collect_failed`.

**Initial values** (`fleet.yaml#decompose`): `split: {k: 2, margin: 7.4, floor: 2}`, `cost: {cold_start: 7.4, per_decision: 0.32, merge: 1.45, supervisor: 1.0, spawn: 0.3}`, `overrun_ratio: 5`, `returns_alert: 3`, `write_back_seconds: 300`, `session_minute_tokens: 9472` (validated and stored; no code reads or displays it yet). `k = 2` accepts a longer wall time in exchange for fewer session minutes; cold_start, per_decision and merge are medians of measured task-mode distributions (cold start: session start to its first file edit; per decision: a blocked request to the next delivery to that slot; merge: a delivery to an integration slot to its settle); margin follows cold_start (a saving under one cold start is noise); supervisor 1.0 is the upper bound by definition (a sub-fleet supervisor online the whole time), not measured; spawn is the approval-to-up median of service splits.

**Known boundaries**: dT / dC are computed with unlimited workers; the width check therefore rarely bites (the dispatchable blocks fit the width by construction). The session minutes of a block are wall-clock minutes between dispatch and report, not the worker's busy time. `saved_minutes` and `extra_session_minutes` are measured against the estimate, not against a run that did not split. Overrun is judged on the planned chain (estimates), once per block. A tune applied under a job-held COMPLETED stays applied when new work lapses the hold. A pi worker cannot ask to split (its pane cannot be handed over, section 19), so `worker_v2.md` is unchanged. The pi schema's harness test needs the installed pi dependencies and is skipped without them.

**Verification**: `tests/test_decompose_*.py` (the seed and its review section, `tree check`, dev_task work without a tree refused and what is not, a started dev_task fleet's job bringing its tree, the review in a job's result; tree model and shape checks, config and switch, decision keys, first-reply rule, dispatch from the tree, off path equals the baseline, opening summary routes, seeds of split / job / service / named sub-fleets, prompt swaps and the pi schema keys, parameters in Redis and the write-back lock, the accounts, overrun / returns / split requests, merge / extract / split actions, contract fill, keeping the tree across item deletes and restarts, split rollback and the session clock, the review table and tuning with forwarding to a service root).

## 63. Interface language: `ui.language`

The texts a person sees in the terminal (topbar, status line, sidebar cards, close boxes, placeholders, notices, the
cards in panes and on the history page, and the history page's fixed texts) follow one setting. Prompts sent to
models, log and audit fields, comments and test data do not change.

### 63.1 Values

`fleet.yaml#ui.language` takes one of:

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `system` (default) | Follow the system language. |
| `en` | English. |
| `zh-Hans` | Simplified Chinese. |

The table leaves room for more languages (for example `zh-Hant`): each new language is one more value and one more
string table.

### 63.2 Resolution order

Python resolves the language once per process. The first source that is set wins:

1. `FLEETMUX_UI_LANGUAGE` (`en` or `zh-Hans`). This variable has two uses only: Python sets it for the terminal
   server it starts (63.5), and the demo recording and the tests set it to force a language. To pin a language for
   daily use, set it in the private file instead.
2. Inside a run: the language its `start` recorded (63.5).
3. The `ui:` block of the private file (`$FLEETMUX_CONFIG`, else `~/.fleetmux/fleet.yaml`). Like every private
   block, it replaces the shipped block as a whole. The language is not kept in Redis.
4. The `ui:` block of the shipped `fleetmux/config/fleet.yaml`: `system`.

`system` is read once:

- macOS: the first entry of `defaults read -g AppleLanguages`. `zh-Hans-*`, `zh-CN` and `zh-SG` give `zh-Hans`.
  `LANG` is not used on macOS.
- Linux and other POSIX systems: the first non-empty value of `LC_ALL`, `LC_MESSAGES` and `LANG`, without its
  encoding and modifier. `zh_CN`, `zh_SG` and `zh_Hans` give `zh-Hans`.
- Anything else gives `en`: other languages, `zh-Hant` / `zh-TW` / `zh-HK`, `C` / `POSIX`, an unreadable value,
  other platforms.

An invalid value (not one of the three, not a string, a `ui` block that is not a mapping, an unreadable config)
prints one line on stderr and gives `en`; nothing stops.

To pin a language on one machine, write in the private file:

```yaml
ui:
  language: en        # or zh-Hans
```

### 63.3 Template

`fleetmux setup` makes sure the private file says how to set the language:

- No private file: it creates one (mode 0600) holding only the commented template below. A file of comments only
  is read as no blocks, so nothing changes.
- A private file without a `ui` block and without the template: the template is appended after the existing bytes.
  Nothing the user wrote changes.
- A `ui` block or the template already there, or a file that is not valid YAML: the file is left as it is.

```yaml
# Interface language (docs/design.md section 63). Uncomment to set it; restart fleetmux to apply.
# ui:
#   language: system  # system | en | zh-Hans
```

The relay's write-back of hot values (§59) rewrites the private file and drops its comments, the template among
them; a `ui` block the user wrote stays. The next `fleetmux setup` adds the template again.

### 63.4 String table

Every interface text is written in the code as a key and its English default: `t('sidebar.line1', '{state}, workers
{workers}, busy {busy}', ...)` in Python, `tr(lang, "bottom.sidebar.hide", "hide sidebar")` in Rust. The Chinese texts
are one table, `fleetmux/locales/zh-Hans.json`, used by both:

- Python (`fleetmux/i18n.py`) reads it from the package (`pyproject.toml` package data).
- Rust (`rust/crates/app/src/i18n.rs`) embeds the same file at build time with `include_str!`; the Docker build
  stage copies `fleetmux/locales/` next to `rust/`.

A key missing from the table shows the English default. Placeholders are `{name}` in both languages.
`tests/test_ui_lang_keys.py` checks that every key the code uses is in the table, that the table has no unused key,
that English defaults are ASCII and that both languages name the same placeholders.

Notices that leave the terminal (Telegram / parent-fleet notices of used-up API retries) keep their Chinese text.

### 63.5 How the terminal gets the language

The terminal server never reads YAML or the system language. Python starts it (`backends/fleetmux.py`
`_spawn_server`) with `FLEETMUX_UI_LANGUAGE` set to the resolved language. The server reads the variable once at
start, keeps it in the sidebar state and does not pass it to its panes (a pane spawn that names it explicitly, as
the demo recording does, keeps it). A missing or unknown value is English.

The daemon and the agents run in those panes, so they do not read the variable either. `start` writes the
resolved language into the run's `config.json` as `ui_language`, and every process of the run applies it with
`pin_paths`, as it does for the private config path. `start --resume` resolves the language again and rewrites the
value. `fleetmux demo` runs its fleet with its own HOME and no `FLEETMUX_*` variable, so it resolves the language in
the caller's process and passes it in `FLEETMUX_UI_LANGUAGE`.

### 63.6 When it applies

At the next `fleetmux start`, or when the terminal server restarts. A server that is already running keeps its
language: `_spawn_server` reuses a server that answers. A running fleet does not switch languages.

### 63.7 Where to see it

`fleetmux setup` prints the language in effect and where it came from, for example:

```
ui language: zh-Hans (setting: system; source: default; system: AppleLanguages zh-Hans-CN)
ui.language template: appended in /Users/me/.fleetmux/fleet.yaml
  set ui: {language: system | en | zh-Hans} there; applies at the next fleetmux start (or terminal server restart)
```

The source is one of `env FLEETMUX_UI_LANGUAGE`, `run config`, `private config <path>`, `default` or
`invalid (...) -> en`.
`fleetmux setup --json` has the same data under `ui_language` and `ui_template`.

### 63.8 Demo and recording

`fleetmux demo` follows `ui.language` like any other start. `scripts/record_demo.sh` sets
`FLEETMUX_UI_LANGUAGE=en` for the recording server and for the demo pane, so the recording is in English whatever the
private file and the system language say.

## 64. `fleetmux acp`: the root fleet as an Agent Client Protocol agent

`fleetmux acp` runs the live root fleet as an Agent Client Protocol agent over stdio.

- Messages are newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0. Stdout carries only protocol messages.
- It supports `initialize`, `authenticate`, `session/new`, `session/prompt` and `session/cancel`. Other requests get error -32601.
- Each prompt becomes one job of the root fleet (section 58), submitted through the join path. An optional fenced JSON block at the end of the prompt carries job keys.
- A job without declared artifacts gets a default artifact, so every job ends with a program check.
- Progress goes back as `session/update` chunks, with a heartbeat after 90 seconds of silence (`HEARTBEAT_S`).
- If no root fleet runs or the job is refused, the turn ends with one visible ERROR chunk. It never starts a fleet.
- If the root daemon stops beating (180 seconds with no lock holder, or 900 seconds), the turn ends with an ERROR. The job stays recorded.
- `session/cancel` stops only that job's sub-fleet. Closing stdin ends the process and never withdraws submitted jobs.
- The client contract is in `docs/omnigent.md`.

## 65. Cross-session board

Claude Code and pi sessions each keep one record on a shared board. The board is a directory, default `~/.fleetmux/board` (`FLEETMUX_BOARD_DIR`). The code is `fleetmux/session_board.py`.

- A record holds the repository, worktree, branch, touched files, git actions and the latest real user prompt.
- Two sessions collide only when they share a repository and edited the same file, or merged into the same branch within 30 minutes.
- The session that started first keeps going. The later one is told to leave those files alone until released.
- Each collision is announced once, and again only when it grows.
- `session_board.py list` shows sessions and collisions. `note <session> <text>` releases a waiting session.
- Every supervisor prompt carries these commands (`{{SESSION_BOARD}}`), and a supervisor may write the board directory.
- The interface is in `docs/contracts.md` section 7.

## 66. The opencode-free pi extension and the launchers

The repository ships standalone pi tools. They are not part of a fleet run.

- `extensions/opencode-free` lets pi use a free model through the user's logged-in OpenCode.
- It keeps one resident `opencode serve` per pi process, inside macOS `sandbox-exec`, writing only its own temp directory. It refuses to run without the sandbox.
- Pi tools reach the model as OpenCode native tools through an empty MCP stub, named `pi_<name>`.
- The context window is declared as the bridge input budget, so pi compacts before the bridge limit.
- An optional fixed proxy is read from `PI_OPENCODE_PROXY`.
- `launchers/*.command` start pi, dsh, Codex or Claude sessions with fixed models.
- User guide: `docs/opencode-free-pi.md`.

## 67. Parent heals child: the supervisor one level up recovers a sub-fleet supervisor

Rule: the daemon looks after a root fleet's own supervisor only (section 24: regeneration, startup retry, wall
chain). A sub-fleet's supervisor -- job, named, attached or split, at any depth -- is a worker slot of its parent
fleet, adopted by the child daemon (section 19). Whatever stops it is reported to the parent fleet's supervisor
with a line to copy, and the parent supervisor's `heal` decision makes the program act. Nothing is switched on or
off: there is no setting for this.

### What the parent supervisor is told

Notices go to the parent's supervisor queue, at the front, as standalone messages (task and service mode alike).
Each names the sub-fleet (or the slot) and ends with a `"heal": [...]` line to copy into the next decision.

| Line | When | Offered `heal` |
|---|---|---|
| `[sub-fleet supervisor held]` | the child's supervisor is held (a StopFailure, a refusal, an exhausted retry; section 24 escalation) | `{"subfleet": <id>, "reopen": "stay"}` |
| `[sub-fleet supervisor dead]` | its start failed or timed out before READY | `{"subfleet": <id>, "reopen": "next"}` |
| `[sub-fleet walled]` | the child braked on a usage wall (its supervisor or a worker) | `clear_wall` of the child's wall records for that slot, then a reopen (`head`) |
| `[sub-fleet held]` | a worker of the child is held / could not switch model | `{"subfleet": <id>, "slot": <worker>, "reopen": "stay"}` |
| `[slot down]` | a slot of this fleet that supervises (or is to supervise) a sub-fleet is dead or held; told at once | `{"slot": <slot>, "reopen": "stay"}` |
| `[sub-fleet not up]` | an approved `subfleet.up` (or an autostart entry) waited 600 s (`named_subfleet.SLOT_WAIT`) without coming up; the cause is slot dead / held / busy or quota | a slot reopen, or `clear_wall` for a quota wait |
| `[sub-fleet recovered]` / `[slot recovered]` | the target moves again | -- |
| `[heal result] <target>: done: ...` / `refused: <reason>` | every heal item | -- |

Dedup: `fx:heal:notices` keeps one record per target and cause. The same cause is not told again within
`daemon_watch.held_realert_s`; a new cause is told at once; the recovered line deletes the record. `[sub-fleet stuck]`
(section 24) keeps its role for a child whose own daemon is down and now also carries a reopen line.

Where the child reports from: `Fleet.brake_notify` (a wall trip), `Fleet.wall_target` / `Fleet.wall_rotate` (a switch
that failed), the startup event and the startup timeout (`RuntimeV2.handle` / the timeout check), the service and
task StopFailure branches (`RuntimeV2.service_stop_failure`, the exhausted supervisor branch) and the section 24 held
escalation. A child writes `heal-*.json` (or `held-*.json`) into the parent's `subfleet-notices/`; the parent's
`relay_notices` hands them to `heal.child_note` / `heal.held_line`. A parent's own slots are watched by
`heal.watch_own_slots`; ups by `heal.up_waits` from `named_subfleet.op_up` and the autostart loop.

### The `heal` decision key

Accepted in task (`parse_decision`) and service (`parse_service_decision`) decisions; a `RUNNING` reply with only
`heal` may dispatch nothing. Items run in order, outside any transaction (`heal.tick`, from `subfleet.tick`):

- `{"subfleet": <id or name>, "reopen": "stay" | "next" | "head" | <card id>}`: reopen that sub-fleet's supervisor
  (adopted or not). The child is suspended in place (`post_stop(suspend=True)`: its daemon stops, its Redis AOF, run
  directory, task or open requests stay); `subfleet.collect` gives the slot its next generation on the chosen model
  (`split_launch`) instead of the usual return; `heal.progress_reopens` hands the same sub-fleet to that generation
  with `subfleet.readopt_child` (`daemon --resume`, `adopt.heal_lift`). The child lifts the pause its supervisor owned
  once the new session is READY. `stay` keeps the delegated session's launch; `next` / `head` walk
  `fleet.yaml#discovery.supervisor` with this run's walls and the card gates (a sub-fleet supervisor stays a Claude
  pane, section 23); a card goes through `split_supervisor_launch`.
- `{"subfleet": <id>, "slot": <worker>, "reopen": ...}` and `{"clear_wall": ..., "in": <id>}`: sent to the child as a
  `heal` control event; the child runs them as its own items and answers under `fx:heal:result:<id>`; the parent
  relays each answer as `[heal result]`.
- `{"slot": <own slot>, "reopen": ...}`: a dead, held or walled slot of this fleet (a root's own supervisor is
  refused: it is the daemon's). Next generation through `Fleet.wall_rotate` (or a respawn when the tab is gone), the
